Ganesha Sahasranama
Composed by Bhaskararaya Dikshita, along with his commentary


· Invocation by the Commentator -




गणेशोवः पायात् प्रणमत गणेशं जगदिदं
गणेशेन त्रातंनम इह गणेशाय महते ॥
गणेशान्नास्त्यन्यत्रिजगतिगणेशस्य महिमा
गणेशेमच्चित्तं निवसतु गणेश ! त्वमव माम् ॥
May Ganesha protect you. Bow down to Ganesha; 
this entire world is sustained by Ganesha. 
Salutations to that great Ganesha here. 
There is none other than Ganesha in the three worlds; 
Such is the glory of Ganesha. May my mind dwell upon Ganesha. 
O Ganesha! Please protect me.










गुरुरेव गतिर्गुरुमेव भजेगुरुणैव सहास्मि नमो गुरवे ॥
न गुरोःपरमं शिशुरस्मि गुरोर्मतिरस्ति गुरौ मम पाहि गुरो !॥
The Guru alone is the ultimate goal, I worship the Guru alone, 
I exist because of the Guru alone, salutations to the Guru. 
I know nothing higher than the Guru, I am but a child; 
my intellect is fixed upon the Guru. O Guru! Please protect me.






· The Commentator's Pledge -


प्राचांकुसृष्टिरचनैकमयस्वभावामज्ञातगाणपततन्त्रपुराणभावाम्॥
व्याख्यामुदस्य सुविभाव्यगणेशनामान्यर्थापयामितदुपासकतोषहेतोः ॥
The compositions of the ancients, whose nature is imbued with the essence of the Vedas, whose true nature regarding the Ganapatya Tantra and Puranas is not understood... Setting aside (other interpretations), I will elucidate the meanings of the thousand names of Ganesha, for the satisfaction of his devotees.


नन्वर्धानाझ्यनंताः फणिपतिधिषणा शक्यकृत्स्नप्रकाशा
मन्दस्तत्र प्रवृत्तोऽप्युपहसितपदंस्याज्जनस्येति चेन्न ॥
यत्तार्थ्यद्योमणिभ्यामपिदुरधिगमं कृत्स्नशो दुष्प्रकाशं
तत्खद्योतःपतन् खं किमपि विशदयत्येव नेहास्त्यवद्यं
If it is said: "The names are infinite, profound, and as vast as the serpent-king Shesha; how can one of dull intellect, attempting to explain them fully, avoid becoming an object of ridicule?" Then, no! That which is difficult to grasp even for the sun and the moon, which is entirely hard to illuminate... even a firefly, falling in the sky, clarifies it to some extent. Here, there is no fault.


नाम्नामर्थश्चिदाकाशस्तंद्योतयितुमुद्यतः ।
ग्रन्थःखद्योतनामाऽस्तु ब्रह्म खं गगनं च खम् ॥
The meaning of the names is the Consciousness-Space (Chidakasha). This text, which endeavors to illuminate it, may be called "Khadyota" (The Firefly). (For) Brahman is Space, and Space is the sky (Akasha).


केषांन दुष्करं नाम्नां साकल्येनार्थवर्णनम् ।
अतःसंक्षिप्य वक्तव्ये श्लोकैरेव स वर्ण्यते ॥
For whom is it not difficult to describe the meaning of all the names in their entirety? 
Therefore, that (meaning) which needs to be stated is described concisely, in verses alone.














• Examination of Other Viewpoints •


त्रिविधभेदमवाङ्मनसगोचरंस्वप्रकाशं मुक्तोपसृष्यं परं ब्रह्म ।
The Supreme Brahman is beyond the reach of speech and mind,self-luminous, and is served by the liberated. It is considered to have a threefold distinction.


तन्निर्विकल्पेन तद्विषयकसविलासाज्ञाननिवृत्त्याकण्ठचामी-
करन्यायेन तदात्मतावाप्तिरूपो मोक्ष इत्यविवादम् ।
It is undisputed that liberation(Moksha) is the attainment of identity with that (Brahman), through the cessation of the ignorance pertaining to it, by means of Nirvikalpa (non-dual) knowledge, just as a necklace is realized to be nothing but gold.


स च निर्विकल्पःशुद्धचित्तानामेव न मलिनाशयानाम्; चित्तशुद्धौ 
तु श्रौतस्मार्तकर्माणि तत्तद्वर्णाश्रमोचितानि वेदे कर्मकांडसम -धिगम्यानि बहिरङ्गोपायाः।
And that Nirvikalpa(state) is only for those with pure minds, not for those with impure inner dispositions. For the purification of the mind, the Vedic and scriptural duties (Shrauta and Smarta Karmas) prescribed for the various castes and stages of life, as obtained from the Karma Kanda section of the Vedas, are the external means.


तस्यैव ब्रह्मणःसगुण-निर्गुणभेदेन द्विविधस्योपासनानि वेदे रहस्यतरोपनिषत्कांडसमधिगम्यानि अन्तरङ्गोपायाः ।
For that very Brahman,which is of two kinds—Saguna (with attributes) and Nirguna (without attributes)—the modes of worship, as obtained from the more secret Upanishad section of the Vedas, are the internal means.


बहिरङ्गानुष्ठानशीलानामेव त्वन्तरङ्गानुष्ठा-नेऽधिकारः,सगुणं तु 
रूपमुपासकानुग्रहार्थं कल्पितमेवेत्युपा-सकरुचिवैचित्र्येण नानाविधम् ।
However,only those who are diligent in the external practices are entitled to the internal practices. The Saguna (form with qualities) is indeed imagined for the grace of the worshipper, and is of various kinds due to the diversity of tastes among devotees.












तेषु'आदित्यमंबिकां विष्णुं गणनाथं महेश्वरम्' इति स्मृत्युक्तरूपाणि 
तत्तदवांतरमूर्ति-भेदाश्च तत्तज्जन्मायातत्संस्कारानुगुण्येनोपास्यानीति सर्वेऽपी-श्वरा एव ।
Among these,the forms mentioned in the Smritis—such as Aditya, Ambika, Vishnu, Ganapati (Gananatha), and Maheshvara—and their various sub-forms are to be worshipped according to the inherent predispositions (Samskaras) brought from previous births. Thus, all of them are indeed (different forms of) the same Ishvara (Lord).


अत एव पुराणान्यपितत्तदुपासकानुजिघृक्षया प्रवृत्तानि तस्य तस्यैव सर्वोत्तमत्वम्; अन्येषां ततोऽपकर्ष च नहिनिंदान्यायेन प्रतिपादयंति कल्पभेदेनैकस्मादन्येषामु-त्पत्तिः संभवतीत्यादिकमाकरेषु स्पष्टम्ः
For this reason alone,the Puranas also, composed with the desire to protect their respective worshippers, proclaim the supreme excellence of that particular (deity) and the inferiority of others, following the maxim of "praising by not praising" (Nahi Ninda Nyaya). It is clear in the source texts that, through different narrations, the origin of one from another is possible, and so on.


तत्र गणपतिविषयेहेरंबोपनिषद्भणेशोपनिषदाद्याः श्रुतय ऐदंपर्येण गाणपत-तन्त्राणि 
गणेशोपपुराणं पुराणान्तरेष्वपि तत्र तत्र सर्वोत्तम-त्वनिरूपणभागाश्चेति शतशः प्रमाणानि तत्तद्विदां स्पष्टानि ।
Regarding Ganapati,the Upanishads like the Heramba Upanishad and the Ganapati Upanishad, the Ganapatya Tantras which have this intent, the Ganesha Purana, and the sections in other Puranas that establish his supreme status—these hundreds of evidences are clear to the experts in those fields.


एतेन निघण्टुग्रन्थेऽग्न्यादिदेवपत्यन्तदेवतानांमध्ये गणपतेरनिर्देशादसत्त्वमाशंक्य 
'मरुतो गणानामधिपतये' इत्यभ्यातानमंत्रे कथनात्सत्त्वसमर्थनं प्राचामपास्तम्;
Through this(reasoning), the refutation by the ancients is established: (they refute) the doubt about Ganesha's non-existence based on his not being mentioned in the Nighantu lexicon among the deities from Agni to the lord of the deities, and the subsequent effort to prove his existence by citing the Abhyatana mantra, "To Marut, the lord of the hosts (Ganas)".


कर्मकाण्डीयदेवतामध्येगणनाभावस्याप्रयोजकत्वात्, तत्रैकपंचाशदधिकशतदेवतानामेव 
गणनेन ततोऽधिकानां परःशतदेवतानां वेदे पुराणेषु च प्रसिध्दानामसिद्ध्यापत्तेः;
(Because)the non-inclusion in the list of Karma-kanda deities is inconclusive, for if only the 151 deities are counted, then the other hundreds of deities well-known in the Vedas and Puranas would have to be considered non-existent.






'वायु'पदस्य सूत्रात्मनि क्वचित्प्रयुक्तत्वेन तत्पर्याय-'मरुत्' शब्दस्य 
बहुवचनान्तस्यापि परमेश्वरपरत्वसमर्थनप्रयासस्य क्लिष्टतरत्वात् ।
And the effort to prove that the word 'Marut', which is in the plural and a synonym of Vayu (who is sometimes used to denote the Sutratman, the thread-soul), refers to the Supreme Lord, is far-fetched and overly complicated.


उपनिषदांप्रमाणमूर्धन्यानां सत्त्वेऽपि एवं प्रमाणगवेषणस्य करभमनुसरामः । 
'कामधेनौ स्थितायाम्' इति न्यायेन निरसनीयत्वात् ।
When the Upanishads,which are the foremost authority, exist, pursuing such (inferior) evidence is like (seeking) a donkey (when one has a wish-fulfilling cow). It is to be rejected by the maxim, "When the Kamadhenu (wish-fulfilling cow) is present" (why seek lesser things?).


'पुराणेष्वर्थवादत्वंये वदन्ति नराधमाः' इत्यादिबृहन्नारदीयादिवचनेनार्थवादशून्य 
पुराणानामर्थवादजटिलं कर्मकाण्डमपेक्ष्य तत्त्वनिर्णये प्रबलत्वात् ।
For,by statements like "Only the worst of men say that the Puranas are mere Arthavada (eulogistic explanations)" from the Brihan-Naradiya and other texts, the Puranas, which are not devoid of profound meaning, are stronger than the Karma-kanda (which is full of Arthavada) in determining the truth.


'श्रुतिस्मृतीहि नेत्रे द्वे पुराणं हृदयं मतम् । श्रुतिस्मृतिभ्यां हीनोऽन्धः काणः स्यादेकया विना । पुराणहीनाद्धृच्छून्यात्काणान्धावपि तौ वरौ' इत्यादिवचनैर्देवतातत्त्वनिर्णये कर्मकाण्डमपेक्ष्य 
पुराणानामेवाधिक्यस्य भक्तिमीमांसातंत्रे समर्थितत्वात् ।
And by statements such as"The Sruti and Smriti are the two eyes; the Purana is considered the heart. One without Sruti and Smriti is blind; without one of them, he is one-eyed. But one without the Purana is heartless; the blind and one-eyed are better than him," the superiority of the Puranas over the Karma-kanda in determining the true nature of the deities is established in the Bhakti Mimamsa texts.


शिवविष्णुदेवीविषयकाणामिव गणपतिविषयाणामुपनिषदामपि जागरूकत्वाच्च ।
And because the Upanishads pertaining to Ganapati are also well-known,just like those pertaining to Shiva, Vishnu, and the Goddess.


कर्मकाण्डेऽपिअभ्यातानामत्र कदर्थनमपेक्ष्य स्पष्टतरस्य 'गणानां त्वा' इति मंत्रस्य 
वेदत्रयेऽपि पठ्यमानस्य शरणीकर्तुं युक्तत्वाच्चेति दिक् ।
And even within the Karma-kanda,instead of misinterpreting the Abhyatana mantra, it is more appropriate to take refuge in the clearer mantra, "Gananam Tva" ("I invoke you, the leader of the Ganas"), which is recited in three Vedas. And so on (this is the direction of the argument).


तदुपासनंचैकाक्षरषडक्षराष्टाविंशत्यक्षरादिबहुविधगणपतिमंत्रजपादिरूपं बहुविधम् तदंगत्वेन क्रत्वर्थः । स्वतंत्रतया पुरुषार्थश्च सहस्रनामस्तवपाठोऽपि सहस्त्रनामस्तवस्यापि पुराणतंत्रभेदेन बहुविधस्य मध्येऽयमेव बहुभिरुपासकैः परिगृहीत इत्ययमेव व्याक्रियते ॥
The worship of him(Ganesha) is of many kinds, such as the repetition of various Ganesha mantras like the one-syllable (e.g., Gam), the six-syllable, and the twenty-eight-syllable mantras. It can be performed as an auxiliary part of a Vedic ritual (Kratvartha) or independently for one's spiritual goal (Purushartha). The recitation of the Thousand Name Hymn (Sahasranama Stotra) is also (a form of worship). Even among the many versions of the Sahasranama Stotra found in different Puranas and Tantras, this one alone is accepted by many devotees, and hence this one alone is being explained.


• Introduction to This Text •
अयंच स्तवः पद्मपुराणीय इति प्राचो व्याचक्षते । गणेशपुराणे तु बहुभिरवलोक्यते ।
The ancients declare that this hymn(stotra) is from the Padma Purana. 
However, it is widely found in the Ganesha Purana.


गणेशपुराणेतावदुपा-सनाखण्डे- 'अष्टादशपुराणानि विस्तराच्छ्रावितानि नः । 
अन्ये-षामपि शुश्रूषा वर्तते वह्निसंभवा' इति सूतं प्रत्यूषीणां प्रश्ने सूतवाक्यम्-'अन्यान्युपपुराणानि वर्ततेऽष्टादशैव तु । गाणेशं नारदीयं नारसिंहादिकान्यपि' इत्युक्त्वा पुनर्गणेश-पुराणे मूलप्रश्नस्योत्तरम्
Now,in the Upasana Kanda of the Ganesha Purana itself, in response to the sages' question to Suta, Suta's statement is: "You have narrated the eighteen major Puranas to us in detail. We are also curious about the others, O son of Fire (Suta)." Replying to this, Suta said, "There are other Upa-Puranas as well, indeed eighteen in number, such as the Ganesha, the Naradiya, the Narasimha, etc." Then, again in the Ganesha Purana, the answer to the original question [about its own origin] is given.


'ब्रह्मणाकथितं पूर्व व्यासायामित-तेजसे । भृगवे कथितं तेन सोमकान्ताय तेन च' इत्यादिना 
पुराणमवतार्य कतिचिदूव्याख्यानानि निर्वर्ण्य षटूत्रिंशम-ध्यायमारभ्य गृत्समदोपाख्यानं प्रवर्तितम् ।
With verses beginning with,"It was first narrated by Brahma to Vyasa of immeasurable splendor. By him, it was told to Bhrigu, and by him to Somakanta," the Purana is introduced. After describing a few commentaries, beginning from the thirty-sixth chapter, the story of Gritsamada is commenced.










तदीयक्षुतात्त्रिपुरासुरस्योत्पत्तिस्तस्य तपस्तस्मै वरदानं तस्य त्रैलोक्यजयः शिवेन सह युद्धं 
शिवपराजयो नारदं प्रति शिवकृतप्रश्ने-त्येतावदुत्क्रांते नारदवाक्ये- 'युद्धाय गन्तुकामेन 
नार्चितो गणपस्त्वया । अतः पराभवं प्राप्तोऽस्यग्निनेत्रपिधानधृक्' इत्यादि ।
From his(Gritsamada's) hunger, the origin of the demon Tripurasura, his penance, the boon granted to him, his conquest of the three worlds, his battle with Shiva, the defeat of Shiva—when this much had been narrated, in response to a question posed by Shiva to Narada, Narada said, "You, who wished to go to battle, did not worship Ganapa (Ganesha). Therefore, you have met with defeat, O one who holds the fire (of the third eye)."


ततःसोऽतप्यत तपोऽत्युग्रं दशवर्षाणि शंकरः । ततस्तस्य मुखांभोजान्निर्गतोऽभूत्पुमान् परः । 
पञ्चवक्रो दश-भुजो भालचन्द्रः शशिप्रभः । मुण्डमालः सर्पभूषो मुकुटाङ्ग-दभूषणः । 
अग्न्यर्कशशिनो भाभिस्तिरस्कुर्वन् दशायुधः ।' इत्यादिना गणेशाविर्भावतत्प्रसादवरदानादिकमुक्तत्वा,
Then,Lord Shankara (Shiva) performed an extremely severe penance for ten years. Then, from his lotus-like mouth emerged the Supreme Being. He had five faces, ten arms, a crescent moon on his forehead, and the radiance of the moon. He was adorned with a garland of skulls and serpent ornaments, and wore a crown and armlets. With his splendor, he eclipsed the light of Agni (Fire), Surya (Sun), and Shashi (Moon), and he held ten weapons. In this manner, the manifestation of Ganesha, his grace, the granting of boons, etc., are described.


'ततःस्वनाम्नामवदत् सहस्रं तस्मै गणेशः प्रणताय दिव्यम् । शिवस्य भक्त्या परितुष्टचित्तो 
जयप्रदं कामकरं जनानाम् ॥' इत्यादिना तपःसमाप्तिः पञ्चचत्वारिंशेऽध्याये वर्णिता ॥
Then,with verses like, "Then, Ganesha, whose heart was pleased with Shiva's devotion, recited the divine thousand of his own names to him (Shiva) who was bowing down. It bestows victory and fulfills the desires of the people," the completion of the penance is described in the forty-fifth chapter.


• The Structure and Ritual Details of the Hymn • 
तत उत्तरेऽध्यायेब्रह्माणं प्रति व्यासकृतप्रश्नः
व्यास उवाच॥
कथंनाम्नां सहस्रं स्वं गणेश उपदिष्टवान् ।
शिवायैतन्ममाचक्ष्व लोकानुग्रहतत्पर ! इति ॥
Then,in the subsequent chapter, there is a question posed by Vyasa to Brahma:
Vyasa said:
"O you who are intent on blessing the worlds!Please tell me how Ganesha instructed Shiva in his own thousand names."


ततः-ब्रह्मोवाच॥
'देव! एवं पुरारातिः' इत्यादिश्लोकचतुष्टयेन पूर्वोक्तविस्तृ-तकथायाः संक्षेपेणानुवादः ।
Then,Brahma replied:
With four verses beginning with'Deva! Evam Purarātih' ("O God! Thus, the enemy of the triple fortress [Shiva]..."), there is a concise recapitulation of the previously mentioned detailed story.


तदिदंस्तोत्रं गणेशपुराणे षट्चत्वा-रिंशत्तमाध्यायरूपम् । अत्र पूर्वपीठिकाभागः पंचश्लोकात्मकः । 
मध्ये नामभागः सार्धषट्षष्टट्युत्तरशतश्लोकात्मकः । अन्त्यः फलश्रुतिभागः सार्धत्रिचत्वारिंशच्लोकात्मकः ।
This very hymn,in the Ganesha Purana, constitutes the forty-sixth chapter. Here, the preliminary section (Peethika) consists of five verses. The middle section containing the names consists of 166 and a half verses. The final section, which describes the benefits (Phalashruti), consists of 43 and a half verses.


संहत्य पञ्च-दशोत्तरेद्वे शते श्लोकानामुक्त्वा सप्तचत्वारिंशेऽध्याये 
पुन-र्युद्धं त्रिपुरसंहारादिकं चोक्त्वा त्रिपुराख्यानमुपसंहृतमिति स्थितिः ।
In total,having stated 215 verses, the narrative of Tripura is concluded in the forty-seventh chapter by again describing the battle and the destruction of Tripurasura, etc.


पद्मपुराणीयेतु पाठे पूर्वपीठिकायां प्रथमश्लोको नास्ति । द्वितीयश्लोके 'देवः पूर्वम्' इति पाठः । 
फलश्रुतौ यो 'नामभिहुनेदेतैः' इत्याद्याश्चत्वारः श्लोका न सन्ति इति । दशोत्तरैव शतद्वयीति विशेषः ।
However,in the version from the Padma Purana, the first verse of the preliminary section is absent. In the second verse, the reading is 'Devah Pūrvam' ("The god, previously..."). In the Phalashruti section, the four verses beginning with 'Nāmabhir hunedetaih' ("One who performs a fire ritual with these names...") are not present. The special feature is that it consists of only 210 verses.


अस्य स्तोत्रस्य महागणपतिरेव मंत्रद्रष्टत्वादृषिः,प्रतिपाद्य-त्वाद्देवता च, अनुष्टुप् छंदः, 
गंबीजं, हुंशक्तिः, स्वाहा कीलकम्, चतुर्विधपुरुषार्थसिद्धयर्थे जपादौ विनियोगः ।
For this hymn,the sage (Rishi) is Mahaganapati himself, as he is the seer of the mantra. The deity (Devata) is also he, as he is the one being propounded. The meter (Chandas) is Anushtup. The seed (Bija) is 'Gam'. The power (Shakti) is 'Hum'. The pin (Kilaka) is 'Svaha'. Its application (Viniyoga) is in recitation, etc., for the attainment of the fourfold goals of human life (Purushartha: Dharma, Artha, Kama, Moksha).


गांगींइति षट् दीर्धेः षडंगानि ।
The six limbs(Shadanga) for meditation are (to be visualized with) the six long vowels (in the mantra 'Gām', 'Gīm', etc.).


'पञ्चवक्त्रोदशभुजः' इत्यादि पूर्वोक्तं ध्यानमिति द्रष्टव्यम् ॥
The meditation(Dhyana) form is to be understood as the one stated before, beginning with "He who has five faces and ten arms..."


• Now, the Explanation of the Verses •
व्यासप्रश्नःस्पष्टार्थः ॥
The question posed by Vyasa is clear in meaning.


एवंव्यासेन पृष्टमर्थ दीनजनानुजिघृक्षयोपदिदिक्षुर्भगवान् ब्रह्मा 'सहस्रनाममंत्रोऽयं जपितव्यः शुभाप्तये' इति मंत्रजपं विधास्य गुरुसंप्रदायशुद्धिमन्तरेण लब्धस्य फलानावहत्वाद्गुरुपरंपरा ज्ञानावश्यकत्वं ध्वनयितु-माख्यायिकां पूर्व विस्तृत्य वर्णितानामपि पुनः संक्षिप्यानु-वदति-देव इत्यादिना ।
Having been asked this meaning by Vyasa,Lord Brahma, who wished to teach out of compassion for the distressed, and who was about to prescribe the recitation of the mantra with the words "This thousand-name mantra should be chanted for attaining auspiciousness," now reiterates the story concisely with the words beginning with "Deva..." He does this to imply the necessity of the Guru lineage (Guru Parampara), because knowledge obtained without the purity of an authentic tradition yields no fruit, even though it had been described in detail before.


॥ व्यास उवाच ॥
कथंनाम्नां सहस्रं स्वं गणेश उपदिष्टवान् ।
शिवायैतन्ममाचक्ष्व लोका-नुग्रहतत्पर ! ॥१॥
Vyasa said: O you who are intent on blessing the worlds!
Please tell me how Ganesha instructed his own thousand names to Shiva.


॥ ब्रह्मोवाच॥
देव एवंपुरा-रातिः पुरत्रयजयोद्यमे ।
अनर्चनाद्गणेशस्य जातोविघ्न्नाकुलः किल ॥ २ ॥
Brahma said: 
O God(Vyasa)! It is said that the enemy of the triple fortress (Shiva), while undertaking 
the campaign to conquer the three cities, became beset with obstacles due to not worshipping Ganesha.






मनसास विनिर्धाय ततस्तद्विघ्नकारणम् ।
महा-गणपतिंभक्त्या समभ्यर्च्य यथाविधि ॥ ३ ॥
Having mentally ascertained the cause of those obstacles,
that unconquered one (Shiva) devoutly and properly worshipped Mahaganapati.


विघ्नप्रशमनोपायमपृच्छदपराजितः।
संतुष्टःपूजया शंभोर्महागणपतिः स्वयम् ॥ ४ ॥
He,the undefeated one (Shiva), asked for the means to pacify the obstacles. 
Mahaganapati himself, pleased by Shambhu's (Shiva's) worship,


सर्वविघ्नैकहरणंसर्वकामफलप्रदम् ।
ततस्तस्मैसर्वनाम्नां सहस्रमिदमब्रवीत् ॥ ५ ॥
...then spoke to him this collection of all names,a thousand in number, 
which removes all obstacles and bestows the fruits of all desires.


Commentary on the Verses and the Importance of Guru Parampara
एवंपूर्वाध्यायैरुक्तरीत्या उक्तं च 'गुरुक्रममविज्ञायोपास्ते यः स्वेष्टदेवताम् । 
सोपास्तिर्निष्फला ज्ञेया भस्मन्यर्पितहव्यवत्। तस्माद्यत्नेन विज्ञेया मूलाद्गुरुपरंपरा' इति ।
As stated in the previous chapters in this manner,it is also said: "The worship performed by one who worships their chosen deity without knowing the Guru lineage is to be known as fruitless, like an oblation offered into ashes. Therefore, one must diligently know the Guru lineage from its origin."


Sanskrit then details a specific Guru lineage (Parampara) for Ganapatya mantras:
· Ādi-Guru (Primordial Guru): Ganapati himself.
· Kula-Gurus (9): Gana-krīḍa, Vikata, etc.
· Vidyā-Gurus (9): Surānanda, etc.
· Santāna-Gurus (20): Vināyaka, etc. (Each of these 38 are further divided into Divine, Siddha, and Human streams).
· Subsequent Gurus (21): From Kapila to Vyasa.
· Later Gurus (42): From Ganaka to Shankaracharya.
· Total: 101 Gurus, as established by Vidyaranya Shripada in the "Vidyarṇava."








प्रायेणास्मिंस्तोत्रेप्राथमिकनामशतद्वयमध्ये तानि सर्वाणि व्यक्तीभवन्तीति रहस्यम् ।
The secret is that almost all of these (Guru names) are manifested within 
the first two hundred names of this hymn.


शङ्करभगवत्पादेभ्योऽ-र्वाचीनातु स्वनाथपर्यंता गुरुपरंपरा स्वस्वदेशिकमुखा देवावगन्तव्या ॥
The Guru lineage after Shankaracharya up to one's own spiritual preceptor(Svanatha) 
should be learned from the mouth of one's own Guru.


Commentary on the Act of Receiving the Mantra
गुरोःप्रसादफलमाह-संतुष्ट इति । उपदेशक्रममाह-सर्वविघ्नेति । 
अखिलानिष्टबाधकेषूपायेष्वस्य मुख्यत्वकथ-नाय-एकेति । चतुर्वर्गसाधनत्वकथनाय-सर्वकामेति ।
The phrase"संतुष्टः" (pleased) indicates the fruit of the Guru's grace. The phrase "सर्वविघ्नैकहरणं" (removes all obstacles) explains the method of instruction. The word "एक" (the one) is used to state its primacy among all means that remove inauspiciousness. The phrase "सर्वकामफलप्रदम्" (bestows the fruits of all desires) states its power to accomplish the four goals of life (Purusharthas).


ईदृश-गुरुपरंपरामूलभूतोगणेश एव शिष्यभक्तिमाहात्म्यात् 
स्वमु-खेनैव स्वकीयं महामन्त्रं एनमुपादिक्षदित्याह-स्वकमब्रवी-दिति ।
It is said,"स्वकमब्रवीत्" (he spoke his own [mantra]), meaning that Ganesha himself, who is the root of such a Guru lineage, directly instructed this great mantra of his own through his own mouth, due to the greatness of the disciple's (Shiva's) devotion.


ततश्चान्यैरप्ययंमंत्रः प्रोक्तगुरुपरंपराश्चरमेष्वन्यतमं गुरुं भक्त्या संतोष्य 
तन्मुखादेव लब्ध्वा जपनीयः; न पुस्तक-मात्रेण गृहीत्वा जप्तव्य इति तात्पर्यार्थः ।
Therefore, the essential meaning is:This mantra should be received from the mouth of one's own Guru—who is one of the last in the proclaimed Guru lineage—by pleasing him with devotion, and then chanted. It should not be taken merely from a book and chanted.
















ॐ गणेश्वरो गणक्रीडो गणनाथो गणाधिपः ॥
एकदंष्ट्रो वक्रतुण्डो गजवक्त्रो महोदरः ॥ ६ ॥


Shloka 1: Gaṇeśvaraḥ (गणेश्वरः)
ॐवियदादिप्रपञ्चस्य समूहोगण उच्यते ।
तदात्मकस्तदी-शश्च तेन देवोगणेश्वरः ॥ १ ॥
Om! The collection of the entire universe, beginning from space (Vyoma/Akasha), is called a "Gaṇa" (host/group). He whose very nature is that (Gaṇa) and who is its lord (Īśa)—by that (definition), the Lord is called Gaṇeśvara (The Lord of Hosts).


Names 2-3: The Guru Lineage as Forms of Ganesha
साक्षाच्छिष्योगणेशस्य गणक्रीडाभिधो गुरुः ।
तच्छिष्योविकटस्तस्य शिष्यो वै विघ्न-नायकः ॥ २ ॥
The direct disciple of Ganesha is the Guru namedGaṇakrīḍa. His disciple is Vikaṭa, and his disciple, indeed, is Vighna-nāyaka (The Leader of Obstacles).


इत्यादयोऽप्यभेदेन गणेशाएव देशिकाः ।
मंत्रारंभेगुरुं स्मृत्वा वश्यकत्वेन कीर्तिताः ॥ ३ ॥
These and others(in the Guru lineage) are, without difference, themselves Ganesha in the form of spiritual teachers (Deśikāḥ). They are mentioned at the beginning of the mantra (in the Nyasa rituals) to be remembered as the Guru, for the purpose of bringing the mantra under one's control (Vashyakaraṇa).


Shloka 4: Gaṇakrīḍa (गणक्रीड)
वियदादि-गणस्यान्तःप्रविश्य क्रीडनेन वा ।
गणक्रीड इतिप्रोक्तस्तं-त्सृष्ट्वेत्यादिविश्श्रुतेः ॥ ४ ॥
He is called Gaṇakrīḍa because he enters into and "plays" (Krīḍa) within the host of elements beginning with space (Vyoma), or because he creates that very host and then sports within it, as stated in the Vedic revelation (Śruti).




Shloka 5: Gaṇanātha (गणनाथ)
गणनंगुणसंख्यानामथशब्दात्तु मंगलम् ।
कृतेतयोर्बहुव्रीहौ गणनाथ इति स्मृतः ॥ ५ ॥
"Gaṇa"means the counting (or collection) of the primary qualities (Sattva, Rajas, Tamas), and "Nātha" (from the word "Nātha") signifies auspiciousness. When a compound (Bahuvrihi Samasa) is formed from these two (words), he is remembered as Gaṇanātha (The Lord of the Qualities and the Auspicious Lord).


Shloka 6: Gaṇādhipa (गणाधिप)
आदित्यविश्ववस्वाद्यारुद्रान्ता गणदेवताः ।
तदाधिपत्यशालि-त्वान्मम देवोगणाधिपः ॥ ६ ॥
The groups of deities(Gaṇa-devatās), beginning with the Ādityas and Viśvadevas and ending with the Rudras, (are presided over by him). Because he holds lordship over them, my Lord is Gaṇādhipa (The Overlord of the Hosts of Deities).


Shloka 7: Vikaṭa / Ekadanta (विकट / एकदन्त)
एकामुख्या केवला वा दंष्ट्रा यस्य गणेशितुः ।
भूम्युद्धारेजगन्नाशे वैकदंष्ट्रः स कीर्त्यते ॥ ७ ॥
He who has one(Eka), prominent or single, tusk (Damshtra), the ruler of the Ganas (Gaṇeśituḥ), is celebrated as Vikaṭa (The Formidable One) and Ekadanta (The Single-Tusked One). (The story is that he broke it off) for lifting the earth (as an instrument) and for the destruction of the universe (at the end of time).


Shloka 8: Vakratuṇḍa (वक्रतुण्ड)
शुण्डादण्डस्य वक्रत्वाद्वक्रतुण्ड इतीर्यते ।
श्रियंतनोति रम्याणां विकारोऽपीति लोकवाकू ॥ ८ ॥
Because his trunk(Śuṇḍā) is curved (Vakra), he is declared as Vakratuṇḍa (The Curved-Trunked One). A popular saying (Lokavāk) also states that "even a curved (or unique) form bestows (Sri) prosperity and beauty upon the lovely."


Shloka 9: Gajavaktra / Mahodara (गजवक्त्र / महोदर)
गजस्येव मुखंयस्य गजवक्त्रः स उच्यते ।
अनंतकोटि-ब्रह्माण्डपूर्णकुक्षिर्महोदरः॥ ९ ॥
He who has a face(Vaktra) like an elephant's (Gaja's) is called Gajavaktra (The Elephant-Faced One). He whose belly (Udara) is vast (Maha) and filled with countless crores of universes (Brahmāṇḍas) is called Mahodara (The Great-Bellied One).


लंबोदरो धूम्रवर्णो विकटो विघ्ननायकः ॥ 
सुमुखो दुर्मुखो बुद्धो विघ्नराजो गजाननः ॥ ७॥


Shloka 10: Lambodara and Dhūmravarṇa (लम्बोदर / धूम्रवर्ण)
ब्रह्माण्डालंबनंयस्य कुक्षिर्लम्बोदरस्तु सः ।
वायोबींजेधूम्रवर्णं तद्रूपत्वादसावपि ॥ १० ॥
He whose belly(Kukshi) is the support of the universe (Brahmāṇḍa) is indeed Lambodara (The Pot-Bellied One). The seed syllable (Bija) of Vayu (the wind god) is of a smoky/gray color (Dhūmravarṇa); because he is of that form, he is also (called) Dhūmravarṇa (The Smoke-Colored One).


Shloka 11: Vikaṭa (विकट)
'कटेवर्षावरणयो' रिति धातोरनावृतः ।
विभुत्वाद्विकटः प्रोक्तः स यस्य विगतः कटः ॥ ११ ॥
From the verbal root'Kaṭ' (meaning 'to cover' or 'to surround'), he is called Vikaṭa (The Formidable One) because he is uncovered (Anāvṛtaḥ) and all-pervading (Vibhutvāt). Or (it can mean) he from whom all coverings (Kaṭa) have vanished (Vigataḥ).


Shloka 12: Vighnanāyaka (विघ्ननायक)
विघ्न्नान्नयत्यभक्तौघान्विघ्नानामधिपोऽथ वा ।
यद्वाविहन्ति नयति प्राणिनो विघ्ननायकः ॥ १२॥
He is Vighnanāyaka because he leads away (Nayati) hosts of non-devotees through obstacles (Vighnān), or because he is the lord (Adhipa) of obstacles. Alternatively, because he destroys (Vihanti) obstacles and leads (Nayati) beings to liberation.


Shloka 13: Sumukha (सुमुख)
आरंभाःशोभना येन यस्य शोभनमाननम् ।
स एव सुमुखस्तस्मैसुमुखाय नमो नमः ॥ १३॥
He by whom all undertakings become auspicious(Śobhanā), and whose face (Ānanam) is beautiful (Śobhanam), he alone is Sumukha (The Auspicious-Faced One). Salutations again and again to that Sumukha.








Shloka 14: Durmukha (दुर्मुख)
दुःस्पर्श वासुदुर्दर्शमग्नेः सूर्यस्य वा मुखम् ।
तद्रूपत्वादभक्तेषुतथात्वाच्चैष दुर्मुखः ॥ १४ ॥
The face of Agni(fire) or Surya (sun) is painful to touch or very difficult to look at. Because he assumes that form for non-devotees, and because of that nature, he is (also) Durmukha (The Fearsome-Faced One).


Shloka 15: Buddha (बुद्ध)
नित्यबुद्धस्वरूपत्वात्अविद्यावृत्तिनाशनः ।
यद्वाजिना-वतारत्वाद् बुद्ध इत्यभिधीयते ॥ १५ ॥
Because his essential nature is eternally enlightened(Nitya-Buddha) and he is the destroyer of the modifications of ignorance (Avidyā), or because he is an incarnation of a Jina (a conqueror, like the Buddha), he is described as Buddha (The Enlightened One).


Shloka 16: Vighnarāja (विघ्नराज)
विन्नैर्विराजतेतेषां राजा विघ्नस्तु विघ्नकः ।
भक्ताधीनश्च राजाच विघ्नराज-स्ततो मतः ॥ १६ ॥
He shines(Virajate) with obstacles (Vighnaiḥ) for (non-devotees); he is the king (Rājā) who creates obstacles (Vighnakṛt). And for devotees, he is a king who is under their control (Bhakta-adhīna). Therefore, he is considered Vighnarāja (The King of Obstacles).


Shloka 17: Gajānana (गजानन)
'अन प्राणन'इत्यस्माद्धातोर्णिचि ततो ल्युटि ।
गजाननयतीत्यर्थेसाधुरेष गजाननः ॥ १७॥
From the verbal root'An' (meaning 'to breathe' or 'to live'), in its causal form (Nici), and then with the suffix 'Lyut', the word is formed with the meaning "he who causes (elephants) to breathe/live" or "he who leads/moves elephants." Thus, this (name) Gajānana (Elephant-Faced) is grammatically correct.


Philosophical Defense Against Repetition


यत्तु-प्राचा गजवक्त्र-गजाननपदयोरवयवार्थाभेदे पौनरुक्त्या सहस्र-नामस्तुतिबाध एव...
Now, if some (critics) say:"Since the words Gajavaktra and Gajānana have the same meaning in their components ('Gaja' meaning elephant, and 'Vaktra/Anana' meaning face), their use constitutes repetition (Punrukti), which invalidates the very purpose of a hymn of a thousand names...


...न ह्येकमेव नाम सहस्रवारमावर्तितंस्तुतये पर्याप्तं भवतिः...
...for a eulogy is not accomplished by repeating the same name a thousand times...


ततश्च नाम्नांसहस्रेण स्तुतिर्न कृता भवतिः अतः सर्वाणि नामानि प्रत्येकं 
परस्परभिन्नार्थप्रकाशकान्येवेत्युक्तम्, तत् प्रतिज्ञा-मात्रम् ;
...and therefore the eulogy with a thousand names would not have been made.Hence, the claim that all names individually reveal mutually distinct meanings is merely an assertion (Pratijñāmātram).


The Commentator's Rebuttal


नामशब्दस्य प्रातिपदिकवाचकत्वेनाथैक्येऽपिशब्द-भेदमात्रेण नामसहस्रकरणकस्तुतिबाधाभावात् ।
Our Refutation:Even if the meaning (of two names) is the same, the word 'Nāma' (name) refers to a specific word-form (Prātipadika). Therefore, merely by the difference in the words (Śabda-bheda), there is no invalidation of the eulogy composed of a thousand names.


एतेनैकस्यैव नाम्न आवृत्तौतदापत्तिर्निरस्ता।
By this reasoning,the problem (you raised) concerning the repetition of a single name is refuted.


किंबहुना, अर्थभेदाच्छब्द‌भेदं गौणमप्यंगीकृत्याचार्य भगवत्पादैर्विष्णुसहस्रनामस्तुति निर्वाह-करणात्...
Moreover,even accepting a secondary meaning based on a difference in words, the revered Acharya Shankara Bhagavatpada (Adi Shankara) also maintained the validity of the Vishnu Sahasranama in this way...


...शब्दभेदेनामभेदात् कैमुतिकन्यायेनापि तदबाधः ।
...since a difference in words constitutes a difference in names,by 
the principle of "how much more so" (Kaimutika Nyāya), there is no invalidation.




The Final Verdict on Interpretation


अतोनिगमनिरुक्तादिप्रसिद्ध्यनुरोधादक्लिष्टोऽर्थो यथालाभं वर्ण्यतां नाम । न पुनरर्थभेद एवापेक्षित इति निर्बन्धो युक्तः।
Therefore,the meaning of a name should be explained as one finds it, in accordance with the well-known (etymological) principles of Nirukta, striving for a straightforward (Aklishta) interpretation. It is not justified to insist that a difference in meaning is absolutely required in every case.


A Challenge to the Critic


एवंप्रौढिमतस्तव गस्थः खान्तान्तस्थ इति नाम्नोरेका-र्थत्वेन व्याचक्षाणस्य कथं न लज्जा...
O, one who is so learned!How is it that you feel no shame while explaining names like 'Gajastha' (seated on an elephant) and 'Khāntāntastha' (residing in the cavity of the heart) as having the same meaning (i.e., 'residing within')?...


...कथंवा किरीटी कुंडली हारी वनमालीति नाम्नां त्वदीयपाठरीत्या पुनरुक्तानां 
अर्जुन-शेषमनोहरबलरामपरत्वेन संभवदर्थातराणामप्येकविधानार्थक-त्वेन तावकं वर्णनं युज्यताम् ?
...Or,according to your method of interpretation, how can your own commentary justify names like 'Kirīṭī' (wearer of a crown), 'Kuṇḍalī' (wearer of earrings), 'Hārī' (wearer of a garland), and 'Vanamālī' (wearer of a forest garland)? Though they have potentially different meanings (by referring to different ornaments), you would have to consider them repetitive, as they all essentially mean 'one who wears something'. How is your description consistent?


Dismissal of Overly Complex Interpretations


यदपि-लंबोदरपदंविराडात्म-परं, विघ्न्नपदं वायुपरं, गजाननपदं सूर्यपरमित्यादिरीत्या सां-प्रतिकलक्षणानेयार्थलक्षणादिभिर्जघन्यवृत्तिभिर्व्याख्यातम्,-तन्मुखे संभवतीत्यादिबहुतरन्यायविरुद्धं क्लिष्टतरं कुसृष्टिरूपं चेति न तादृशार्थज्ञानमदृष्टं जनयितुं अलमिति प्रामाणिकैरु-पेक्ष्यमिति दिक् ।
And as for that (other type of interpretation)which explains 'Lambodara' as referring to the Virat (cosmic) form, 'Vighna' as referring to Vayu (the wind), 'Gajānana' as referring to Surya (the sun), etc., using forced, secondary, and implied meanings (Lakshana) like Sampratikalaksana—such interpretation is contradicted by many logical principles, is extremely convoluted (Klishtatara), and is a product of poor scholarship (Kusrishti). Such interpretation cannot generate the spiritual merit (Adrishta) that comes from true knowledge. Therefore, it should be disregarded by authoritative scholars. This is the direction (of our conclusion).


भीमः प्रमोद आमोदः सुरानन्दो मदोत्कटः ।
हेरम्बः शम्बरः शम्भुर्लम्बकर्णो महाबलः ॥ ८ ॥


Shloka 18: Bhīma (भीम) & Pramoda (प्रमोद)
दुष्टानां भयकारित्वाद् भीम इत्युच्यते बुधैः ।
इष्टलाभा-त्सुखं यत्तु स प्रमोद इति स्मृतः ॥१८॥
Because he causes fear in the wicked, he is called Bhīma (The Terrifying) by the wise. And the happiness that arises from the attainment of a desired object is called Pramoda (Joy), and he is that.


Shloka 19: Āmoda (आमोद)
ततः प्राक् सुखमामो-ददृष्टार्थप्राप्तिनिश्चयात् ।
इष्टार्थभोगजनितं सुखमानन्द उच्यते ॥ १९ ॥
The happiness that arises prior to that (Pramoda), from the certainty of attaining a seen object, is called Āmoda (Delight). The happiness generated by the enjoyment of a desired object is called Ānanda.


Shloka 20: Surānanda (सुरानन्द)
स सुरोत्थः सुरथ्यो वा सुरानन्दस्तदात्मना ।
अत एवान्यविषयमोषादात्मैकभावतः ॥ २० ॥
That bliss (Ānanda) which arises from the gods (Sura) or which exists in heaven (Surathī), is Surānanda (Bliss of the Gods). His very nature is that. Therefore, he steals away the mind from other sense objects, leading to a state of oneness with the Self.


Shloka 21: Madotkaṭa (मदोत्कट) & Heraṃba (हेरंब)
मदेनावरणोत्क्रांत्या मदोत्कट इति स्मृतः ।
हः शंकरे रविः शब्दे शैवतंत्रप्रवर्तनात् ॥ २१ ॥
Because he has transcended (Uttkrānti) the veil of spiritual intoxication (Mada), he is remembered as Madotkaṭa (Supremely Intoxicated). The syllable "Ha" refers to Shiva (Shankara), "Ra" to fire (Pavaka), and "Mba" to sound (Shabda). Because he propounds the Shaiva Tantras, he is called Heraṃba.


Shloka 22: Śaṃbara (शंबर)
उद्दामशौर्यवत्त्वाद्वा हेरंब इति कथ्यते ।
शं सुखं तद्वरं श्रेष्ठं यस्मिन्नस्ति स शंबरः ॥ २२ ॥
Alternatively, due to his immense valor, he is called Heraṃba. He in whom exists supreme (Vara), excellent (Shreṣṭha) bliss (Śhaṃ) is Śaṃbara.
Shloka 23: Śaṃbhu (शंभु)
शंबं वा कुलिशं राति यद्वा सलिलविग्रहः ।
शंभुस्तु शं भवत्यस्माच्छिवाभेदेन वोदितः ॥ २३ ॥
Or, he who bestows (Rāti) the weapon (Kuliśa, i.e., the goad) of bliss (Śhaṃ). Or, he whose form is water (Salila, i.e., cool and soothing). But Śaṃbhu is so called because from him comes welfare (Śhaṃ - auspiciousness). He is declared to be non-different from Shiva.


Shloka 24: Lambakarṇa (लम्बकर्ण)
यत्र कापि कृतं भक्तैराह्वानस्तवनादिकम् ।
नातिदूर इवाकर्ण्य लंबकर्ण इति स्मृतः ॥ २४ ॥
Wherever the invocation, praise, etc., is made by devotees, he hears it as if it is not very far away. Thus, he is remembered as Lambakarṇa (The Long-Eared One).


नन्दनोऽलम्पटोऽभीरूर्मेघनादो गणञ्जयः ।
विनायको विरूपाक्षो वीरः शूरवरप्रदः ॥ ९॥


Shloka 25: Mahābala (महाबल) & Nandana (नंदन)
महान् बला-सुरो येन बलित्वाद्वा महाबलः ।
नंदिः समृद्धिवाचीति तद्धेतौ ल्युटि नंदनः ॥ २५ ॥
He by whom the great demon Bala was (destroyed), or due to his great strength (Bali), he is Mahābala (Of Great Strength). "Nandi" means prosperity. With the suffix 'Lyut' indicating the reason, he is Nandana (The Prosperous One) because he is the cause of prosperity.


Shloka 26: Alampaṭa (अलम्पट) & Abhīru (अभीरु)
अलं-पटसमृद्धत्वादलंकारः पटोऽस्य वा ।
अभीरुर्भयशून्यत्वादं-गनारहितोऽथ वा ॥२६॥
Because he is abundantly (Alaṃ) adorned with silk garments (Paṭa), he is Alampaṭa (The One Abundantly Clad in Silk). He is Abhīru (The Fearless) because he is devoid of fear, or because he is without (a) body (i.e., he is formless).


Shloka 27: Meghanāda (मेघनाद)
मेघनादः प्रसिद्धार्थो मेघान् नाश-यतीति वा ।
एकैकं यत्नतः शत्रु जयति प्राकृतो जनः ॥ २७ ॥
Meghanāda (Cloud-Roarer) has the well-known meaning (of thunder). Or, it means he who destroys (Nāśayati) the clouds (Megha) (of ignorance). An ordinary man conquers one enemy at a time with great effort...


Shloka 28: Gaṇañjaya (गणंजय) & Vināyaka (विनायक)
अनायासादयं व्यूहान् जयतीति गणंजयः ।
विः पक्षिरूपो जीवौघस्तन्ने तृत्वाद्विनायकः ॥ २८ ॥
...but this one conquers (Jayati) entire armies (Vyūhān) without effort, hence he is Gaṇañjaya (Conqueror of Hosts). "Vi" can mean a bird (in the form of Garuda) or the host of individual souls (Jīvaugha). Because he is the leader (Netṛtva) of them, he is Vināyaka (The Supreme Leader).


Shloka 29: Virūpākṣa (विरूपाक्ष)
धीरशूरो धैर्यशौर्यसामानाधिकरण्यतः ॥ २९ ॥
He is Virūpākṣa (The Odd-Eyed One) because his eyes are dissimilar—one is the sun and the other is the moon (or fire). (The name Dhīraśūra is mentioned separately in the commentary).


महागणपतिर्बुद्धिप्रियः क्षिप्रप्रसादनः ।
रुद्रप्रियो गणाध्यक्ष उमापुत्रोऽघनाशनः ॥ १० ॥


Shloka 30: Varaprada (वरप्रद) & Mahāgaṇapati (महागणपति)
वरान् स्वपादसेविभ्यो ददातीति वरप्रदः ।
गुणगुल्मादयः सेनाभेदास्तेषु य उत्तमः ॥३०॥
He is Varaprada (The Boon-Giver) because he gives (Dadāti) boons (Varān) to those who serve his feet. The different armies (like Guṇa, Gulma, etc.) are various types of hosts (Gaṇa)...


स्थूलास्तानपि पातीति महागणपतिर्मतः ।
...and he who protects (Pātī) even those gross ones (Sthūlāḥ) is considered Mahāgaṇapati (The Great Lord of Hosts).


Shloka 31: Buddhipriya (बुद्धिप्रिय) & Kṣipraprasādana (क्षिप्रप्रसादन)
बुद्धिप्रियः संशयप्न्नो बुद्धिः स्यान्निश्चयात्मिका ॥ ३१ ॥
क्षिप्रप्रसादनः क्षिप्रं भक्तैर्ध्यातः प्रसीदति ।
He is Buddhipriya (Dear to Intellect) because he is the beloved of Buddhi (the goddess of intellect), whose nature is decisiveness, which destroys doubt. He is Kṣipraprasādana (The Quickly Appeased One) because he becomes pleased (Prasīdati) quickly (Kṣipram) when meditated upon by devotees.


Shloka 32: Rudrapriya (रुद्रप्रिय)
रुद्रप्रिय इति प्रोक्तो रुट्रैकादशिनीप्रियः ॥ ३२ ॥
He is proclaimed as Rudrapriya (Dear to Rudra) because he is dear to the group of eleven Rudras.




Shloka 33: Gaṇādhyakṣa (गणाध्यक्ष) & Umāputra (उमापुत्र)
गणाध्यक्षस्तु षटूत्रिंशत्तत्त्वसंदोहपालकः ।
उमापुत्रस्तु पॅल्लोकात्पार्वत्या अपि तारकः ॥३३॥
He is Gaṇādhyakṣa (The Superintendent of Hosts) as he is the protector of the collection of thirty-six cosmic principles (Tattvas). He is Umāputra (The Son of Uma) as he is the son of Pārvatī. He is also the savior (Tāraka) of even her (i.e., he saved Parvati from grief).


Shloka 34: Mahodara (महोदर) & Aghanaśana (अघनाशन)
महोदरोऽप्यसावल्पनैवेद्येनैव तृष्यति।
अशनं न घनं दुःखहरणाद्वा घनाशनः ॥३४॥
Although he is Mahodara (The Great-Bellied One), he is satisfied with even a small offering of food. He is Aghanaśana (The One whose Food is Not Abundant / The Destroyer of Sorrows) because his food (Aśana) is not abundant (Ghana), or because he destroys (Aśana) dense masses (Ghana) of sorrow.


कुमारगुरुरीशानपुत्रो मूषकवाहनः ।
सिद्धिप्रियः सिद्धिपतिः सिद्धः सिद्धिविनायकः ॥ ११ ॥


Shloka 35: Kumāraguru (कुमारगुरु)
सनत्कुमाररूपोऽपि विद्यामुपदिशन् गुरुः ।
स्कन्दपूर्वभव-त्वाद्वा कुमारगुरुरीरितः ॥ ३५॥
Although he is in the form of Sanatkumāra (the eternal youth), he is the Guru who instructs knowledge. Or, because he existed even before Skanda (Kartikeya), he is declared as Kumāraguru (The Preceptor of the Young Gods/The Preceptor who is ever-youthful).


Shloka 36: Īśānaputra (ईशानपुत्र) & Mūṣakavāhana (मूषकवाहन)
ईशानपुत्र इत्युक्तः शिति-कण्ठात्मजत्वतः ।
उन्दुरुं वाहनीकृत्य ख्यातो मूषकवाहनः ॥३६॥
He is called Īśānaputra (The Son of Īśāna/Shiva) because he is the son of Shiti-kaṇṭha (the blue-throated one, Shiva). He is famous as Mūṣakavāhana (The One whose Vehicle is a Mouse) because he has made a mouse (Ūnduru) his vehicle (Vāhana).


Shloka 37: Siddhipriya (सिद्धिप्रिय) & Siddhipati (सिद्धिपति)
सिद्धिप्रियः प्रिया अस्येत्यणिमाद्यष्टसिद्धयः ।
अणि-माद्यष्टसिद्धीनां पाता सिद्धिपतिस्ततः ॥३७॥
He is Siddhipriya (The Lover of Siddhis) because the eight supernatural powers (Siddhis) starting with Aṇimā (the power to become minute) are his beloveds (Priyāḥ). Thereafter, he is Siddhipati (The Lord of Siddhis) because he is the protector (Pātā) of the eight Siddhis.


Shloka 38: Siddha (सिद्ध) & Siddhivināyaka (सिद्धिविनायक)
गोरक्षादिमहा-सिद्धरूपत्वात् सिद्ध उच्यते ।
भक्ते सिद्धिर्विशेषेण नयन् सिद्धिविनायकः ॥ ३८ ॥
He is called Siddha (The Perfected One) because he assumes the form of great perfected beings like Gorakṣa. He is Siddhivināyaka (The Vināyaka who Bestows Accomplishment) because he specially bestows perfection (Siddhi) upon his devotee.


अविघ्नस्तुम्बुरुः सिंहवाहनो मोहिनीप्रियः ।
कटङ्कटो राजपुत्रः शाकलः संमितोऽमितः ॥ १२॥


Shloka 39: Avighna (अविघ्न)
भावप्रधाननिर्देशादवित्वं पशुतां हरन् ।
विन्नैर्विरहितत्वाद्वाऽप्यविघ्न इति कथ्यते ॥ ३९ ॥
Because the primary intent (in worship) is to indicate his state of being without (obstacles), and because he removes the beast-like nature (Paśutā, i.e., spiritual ignorance) from beings, or simply because he is devoid of obstacles (Vighna), he is called Avighna (The Non-Obstacle).


Shloka 40: Tumburu (तुम्बुरु)
रोरूयमाणस्तुंवेन वैणि कैरिति तुंबुरुः ।
इमे गायंति वीणायां ऐतमेवेति हि श्रुतिः ॥ ४० ॥
He is called Tumburu (named after a celestial Gandharva musician) because he is praised (Rorūyamāṇaḥ) by the Tumburu Gandharvas playing the Vīṇā. Indeed, the Vedic scripture (Śruti) says, "These (Gandharvas) sing of him alone to the accompaniment of the Vīṇā."


Shloka 41: Siṃhavāhana (सिंहवाहन)
मयूरमूषकौ यद्वत्तथा सिंहोऽपि वाहनम् ।
गौर्या वा सिंहवाहाया अभेदात्सिंहवाहनः ॥४१॥
Just as (he has) a peacock and a mouse (as vehicles), similarly a lion (Siṃha) is also his vehicle (Vāhana). Or, due to his non-difference from Gaurī (Pārvatī), who has a lion as her vehicle, he is Siṃhavāhana (The Lion-Mounted).
Shloka 42: Mohinīpriya (मोहिनीप्रिय)
मोहिनीशः शिवस्तस्मादभिन्नो मोहिनीप्रियः ।
कटमावृत्ति-मज्ञानं नीहारेणेति हि श्रुतेः ॥ ४२ ॥
Mohinīśa means "The Lord of Mohinī" (Shiva). Therefore, being non-different from him (Shiva), Ganesha is Mohinīpriya (The Beloved of Mohinī). As the Śruti says, "(He) covers ignorance with a veil."


Shloka 43: Kaṭaṅkaṭa (कटंकट) & Rājaputra (राजपुत्र)
कटयत्वावृणोतीति ज्ञानदानात्कटंकटः ।
वरेण्याख्यस्य पृथिवीनेतुः पुत्रवदाचरन् ॥ ४३ ॥
चंद्रो वा पुत्रवद्यस्य राजपुत्रः स उच्यते ॥
He is Kaṭaṅkaṭa because he covers (Āvṛṇoti) with a veil (Kaṭa) and gives (Ka) knowledge (through his grace). Behaving like a son towards the ruler of the earth named Vareṇya, or because the moon (Candra) is like his son, he is called Rājaputra (The Prince).


Shloka 44: Śālaka (शालक) & Saṃmita (संमित)
शः परोक्षेऽलकः केशे यस्यैकांशोऽप्यचाक्षुषः ॥ ४४॥
शातींद्रियः शालतेऽसौ राजतेवेति शालकः ।
अंगुष्ठमात्रमित्यादि श्रुत्या व्याप्तोऽपि संमितः ॥ ४५ ॥
He whose smallest part (Ekāṃśa) is imperceptible to the senses (A-cākṣuṣaḥ)—where "Śa" means "subtle" and "Alaka" can mean "lock of hair" or "subtle"—is Śālaka. He shines (Śālate) and glows like a king, controlling the senses (Śatīndriya). Although he is all-pervading, as stated in Śruti ("of the size of a thumb"), he is also Saṃmita (Measured/Limited, i.e., he has a specific, knowable form).


Shloka 46: Amita (अमित)
मशकेनापि नागेन शम इत्यपि च श्रुतेः ।
परिमाणं त्वणु स्थूलं ह्रस्वं दीर्घ चतुर्विधम् ॥४६॥
(He can be perceived as being as small as) a gnat or (as large as) a great serpent, as stated in the Śruti. Measurement is of four kinds: minute (Aṇu), gross (Sthūla), short (Hrasva), and long (Dīrgha)...


तन्नास्तीत्य मितोऽस्थूलमनण्वित्यादितः श्रुतेः ।
...Because, as the Śruti says ("not gross, not minute..."), He is beyond these very measurements, He is Amita (The Immeasurable).


कूष्माण्डसामसम्भूतिर्दुर्जयो धूर्जयो जयः ।
भूपतिर्भुवनपतिर्भूतानां पतिरव्ययः ॥ १३॥


Shloka 47: Kūṣmāṇḍa-sāma-sambhūti (कूष्माण्डसामसम्भूति)
कूष्माण्डसामसंभूतिः कूष्माण्डैर्जुहुयादिति ॥ ४७ ॥
विधौ प्रसिद्धा मंत्राश्च सामान्यस्य विभूतयः ।
He is Kūṣmāṇḍa-sāma-sambhūti (The One Born from the Kūṣmāṇḍa Sāman) because he is born from the Kūṣmāṇḍa Sāman (a Vedic chant), or because one offers oblations with pumpkins (Kūṣmāṇḍa) as prescribed in ritual codes. The mantras (associated with him) are well-known in the injunctions, and they are the manifestations of the universal (principle).


Shloka 48: Durjaya (दुर्जय)
अजय्यो बलिभि-दैत्यैर्मनसापीति दुर्जयः ॥४८॥
He is Durjaya (The Difficult to Conquer) because he cannot be conquered even in mind by demons like Bali.


Shloka 49: Dhūrjaya (धूर्जय) & Jaya (जय)
धुरं वहत्यनायासाज्जगतामिति धूर्जयः ।
जयो जयस्वरूपत्वाद्भारताद्यात्मकत्वतः ॥ ४९ ॥
He is Dhūrjaya (The Bearer of the Burden) because he effortlessly bears the burden (Dhura) of the worlds. He is Jaya (Victory) because his very nature is victory, and because he is the essence of (epics like) the Mahābhārata, etc., which culminate in victory.


Shloka 50: Bhūpati (भूपति), Bhuvanapati (भुवनपति), Bhūtānāmpati (भूतानांपति)
भूपतिर्भुवनपतिर्भूतानां पतिरित्यमी ।
भ्रातरोऽग्नेस्तदात्म-त्वाद्भूपत्यादिः प्रकीर्तितः ॥ ५० ॥
These—Bhūpati (Lord of the Earth), Bhuvanapati (Lord of the World), and Bhūtānāmpati (Lord of Beings)—are the brothers of Agni (the fire god). Because Ganesha has their nature (i.e., he is the overlord they serve), he is celebrated by these names beginning with Bhūpati.


विश्वकर्ता विश्वमुखो विश्वरूपो निधिर्गुणः ।
कविः कवीनामृषभो ब्रह्मण्यो ब्रह्मवित्प्रियः ॥ १४॥


Shloka 51: Avyaya (अव्यय) & Viśvakartā (विश्वकर्ता)
अव्ययो नाशराहित्याद् विश्वकर्ता जगत्कृतेः ।
विश्वस्य मुखमारंभो यस्माद्विश्वं मुखेऽस्य वा ॥५१॥
He is Avyaya (The Imperishable) because he is devoid of destruction. He is Viśvakartā (The Creator of the Universe) because he is the creator (Kartā) of the world (Jagat). He is the source/origin (Mukha) of the universe (Viśva), or the universe is within his mouth.


Shloka 52: Viśvamukha (विश्वमुख) & Viśvarūpa (विश्वरूप)
विश्वस्य मुखवत्तृप्तिहेतुर्विश्वमुखस्ततः ।
सर्वप्रपंचरूपत्वाद् विश्वरूप इति स्मृतिः ॥ ५२ ॥
Therefore, he is Viśvamukha (The One whose Face is the Universe) because he is the cause of satisfaction, like food (which enters through the mouth). Because his form is the entire phenomenal creation, he is remembered as Viśvarūpa (The One of Universal Form).


Shloka 53: Viśvarūpa (विश्वरूप) - Alternate Meaning
अथवा विश्वरूपोऽसौ त्वष्ट्रा दैवपुरोहितः ।
वियदादिजगज्जातं नित-रां यत्र धीयते ॥ ५३ ॥
Alternatively, he is Viśvarūpa (here, referring to the sage of that name) who was the priest of the gods. (The name is apt for Ganesha because) the entire universe, from space (Vyoma) onwards, is meditated upon within him most intensely.


Shloka 54: Nidhi (निधि) & Ghṛṇi (घृणि)
महापद्मादिनवकरूपत्वाद्वा निधिः स्मृतः ।
घृणिरादित्यरूपत्वाद् घृणिः सूर्य इति श्रुतेः ॥५४॥
He is remembered as Nidhi (The Treasure) because he is the nine treasures (Nidhis) like Mahāpadma. He is Ghṛṇi (The Radiant One) because of his solar nature; as the Śruti says, "Ghṛṇi is the sun."


Shloka 55: Kavi (कवि) & Kavīnāmṛṣabha (कवीनामृषभ)
कार्यत्वात्काव्यकर्तृत्वात् कविरेष तथा कविं ।
कवीनामुप-मश्रुत्या कवीनामृषभोऽप्ययम् ॥ ५५ ॥
He is Kavi (The Wise Poet/Seer) because he is the object of poetic description and the composer of poetry. And he is the one who protects the wise. Through the Vedic analogy, he is also the Kavīnāmṛṣabha (The Foremost among all Poets).




Shloka 56: Brahmaṇya (ब्रह्मण्य) & Brahmanaspati (ब्रह्मणस्पति)
ब्रह्मण्यो ब्राह्मणे वेदे साधुस्तपसि धातरि ।
वाग्वै ब्रह्मपतिस्तस्या इत्येष ब्रह्म-णस्पतिः ॥ ५६ ॥
He is Brahmaṇya (The Upholder of Sacred Law) because he is virtuous (Sādhu) in relation to Brahmins, the Vedas, and penance. He is Brahmanaspati (The Lord of Sacred Speech) because, as the Śruti says, "Speech is indeed Brahman; he is its lord."


ज्येष्ठराजो निधिपतिर्निधिप्रियपतिप्रियः ।
हिरण्मयपुरान्तःस्थः सूर्यमण्डलमध्यगः ॥ १५ ॥


Shloka 57: Jyeṣṭharāja (ज्येष्ठराज) & Nidhipati (निधिपति)
ज्येष्ठराज इति ख्यातो ज्येष्ठाख्ये सान्नि राजनात् ।
एष नाम्ना निधिपतिर्निधीनां परिपालनात् ॥ ५७॥
He is famous as Jyeṣṭharāja (The King of the Eldest) because he reigns (Rājanāt) in the assembly (Sānni) of the deity Jyeṣṭhā. By name, he is Nidhipati (The Lord of Treasures) because he protects (Pari-pālanāt) the treasures (Nidhīs).


Shloka 58: Nidhipriyapati-priya (निधिप्रियपतिप्रिय)
निधीनां त्वा निधिपतिं हवामह इति श्रुतेः ।
निधिप्रिया ये पतयो राज-राजादयो नृपाः ॥ ५८ ॥
तैरप्युपास्य इत्येष निधिप्रियपतिप्रियः ।
As the Śruti says, "We invoke you, O Lord of Treasures." The lords who are dear to the treasures, such as the king of kings and other rulers, also worship him. Therefore, he is Nidhipriyapati-priya (The Beloved of the Lords who are dear to the Treasures).


Shloka 59: Hiraṇmayapura-antaḥstha (हिरण्मयपुरान्तःस्थ)
हिरण्मय-पुरान्तःस्थो दहराकाशमध्यगः ॥ ५९ ॥
चिन्मयब्रह्मपूर्दे-हस्तदंत हृदये स्थितेः ।
He who resides within the city of gold (the heart) is Hiraṇmayapura-antaḥstha (The One who Dwells Inside the Golden City). He dwells in the subtle space within the heart. His body is the abode filled with the consciousness that is Brahman, and he is situated within the cavity of the heart.


Shloka 60: Sūryamaṇḍala-madhyaga (सूर्यमण्डलमध्यग)
ध्येयत्वादंतरादित्ये सूर्यमंडल-मध्यगः ॥ ६० ॥
He is to be meditated upon as the inner sun and as the one residing in the center of the solar orb; thus He is Sūryamaṇḍala-madhyaga (The One Situated in the Center of the Solar Orb).


कराहतिध्वस्तसिन्धुसलिलः पूषदन्तभित् ।
उमाङ्ककेलिकुतुकी मुक्तिदः कुलपावनः ॥ १६॥


Shloka 61: Karāhati-vidhvastasindhusalila (कराहतिविध्वस्तसिन्धुसलिल)
शुण्डादण्डाभिघातेन रिक्तमब्धि चकार यः ॥
कराहति-ध्वस्तसिंधुसलिलः स ततो मतः ॥ ६१ ॥
He who, with the blow of his trunk, emptied the ocean and destroyed its water is therefore considered Karāhati-vidhvastasindhusalila (The One who with a Blow of His Trunk Destroyed the Ocean's Water).


Shloka 62: Pūṣadantabhit (पूषदंतभित्)
वीरभद्रो दक्ष-मुखे पूष्णो दंतानपातयत् ।
तद्रूपत्वादसौ देवः कथ्यते पूषदंतभित् ॥ ६२ ॥
(Virabhadra, a form of Shiva,) knocked out the teeth of the god Pūṣan in the assembly of Dakṣa. Because he (Ganesha) is of that same form (as the supreme Shiva), this god is declared as Pūṣadantabhit (The Smasher of Pūṣan's Teeth).


Shloka 63: Umāṅkakelikutukī (उमाङ्ककेलिकुतुकी)
गौर्युत्संगे कृताक्रीडो न बाल्यात्किंतु कौतुकात् ।
अत एवोमाङ्ककेलिकुतुकी विबुधैः स्मृतः ॥६३॥
He who played in Gauri's lap, not out of childishness but out of divine delight (Kautuka). For this very reason, the wise remember him as Umāṅkakelikutukī (The One who takes Delight in Playing on Uma's Lap).


Shloka 64: Muktida (मुक्तिद) & Kulapālana (कुलपालन)
निगडो मोचनान्मोक्षदानाद्वा मुक्तिदो मतः ।
वंशस्य कौल-तंत्रस्याप्यवनात् कुलपालनः ॥ ६४ ॥
He is considered Muktida (The Giver of Liberation) because he releases from fetters or bestows liberation (Mokṣa). He is Kulapālana (The Protector of the Family Lineage) because he protects (Avana) the lineage (Vaṃśa) and the Kaula Tantra tradition.


किरीटी कुण्डली हारी वनमाली मनोमयः ।
वैमुख्यहतदैत्यश्रीः पादाहतिजितक्षितिः ॥ १७॥


Shloka 65: Kirīṭī (किरीटी) & Kuṇḍalī (कुंडली)
( यदाभिमुख्यराहित्यान्नष्टा संपत्सुरद्विषाम्) ।
किरीटी मौलिभूषावानथवाऽर्जुनरूपधृक् ।
कुंडली कर्णभूषावान् अथवा शेषरूपधृक् ॥ ६५ ॥
(He is the one from whose lack of favor the prosperity of the enemies of the gods perishes). 
He is Kirīṭī (The Wearer of the Crown) as he is adorned with a crown, or because he assumed the form of Arjuna. He is Kuṇḍalī (The Wearer of Earrings) as he wears earrings, or because he assumed the form of Śeṣa (the serpent).


Shloka 66: Hārī (हारी) & Vanamālī (वनमाली)
हारी मुक्तादिमाला धृगथवाऽ-त्यंतसुंदरः।
वनमाली दधात्येष मालामापादलंबिनीम् ॥६६॥
He is Hārī (The Wearer of Garlands) as he wears a garland of pearls, etc., or because he is extremely beautiful. He is Vanamālī (The Wearer of the Forest Garland) as he wears a garland that hangs down to his feet.


Shloka 67: Manomaya (मनोमय)
मनोमयः स्वसंकल्पकल्पितैककलेवरः ।
यदाभिमुख्यराहि-त्यान्नष्टा संपत्सुरद्विषाम् ॥ ६७ ॥
He is Manomaya (Made of Mind) because his entire body is fashioned by his own will. (It is repeated that) from the lack of his favor, the prosperity of the enemies of the gods perishes.


Shloka 68: Vaimukhya-hata-daitya-śrīḥ (वैमुख्यहतदैत्यश्रीः) & Pādāhati-jita-kṣiti (पादाहतिजितक्षिति)
यत्पादाघातमात्रेण पृथ्वी नम्रा बभूव सः ।
वैमुख्यहतदैत्यश्रीः पादाहतिजित-क्षितिः ॥ ६८ ॥
He at whose mere stamp of the foot the earth became bowed down; he by whose disfavor the glory of the demons is destroyed is Vaimukhya-hata-daitya-śrīḥ (The One by whose Disfavor the Glory of Demons is Destroyed), and he whose kick of the foot conquers the earth is Pādāhati-jita-kṣiti (The One who Conquered the Earth with a Kick).




सद्योजातः स्वर्णमुञ्जमेखली दुर्निमित्तहृत् ।
दुःस्वप्नहृत्प्रसहनो गुणी नादप्रतिष्ठितः ॥ १८॥


Shloka 69: Sadyojāta-svarṇa-muñja-mekhalī (सद्योजातस्वर्णमुञ्जमेखली)
मुञ्जाख्यहैमतृणजां मौञ्जीं तात्कालिकीं दधत् ।
सद्यो-जातस्वर्णमुञ्जमेखलीति निगद्यते ॥ ६९ ॥
He who, immediately upon birth, wore the sacred girdle made of golden Muñja grass is proclaimed as Sadyojāta-svarṇa-muñja-mekhalī (The One who at Birth Wore the Girdle of Golden Muñja Grass).


Shloka 70: Durnimittahṛt (दुर्निमित्तहृत्)
मूर्तिस्फोटन-भूकम्पमहोल्कापतनादिभिः ।
संसूचितान्यरिष्टानि हन्त्यतो दुर्निमित्तहृत् ॥ ७० ॥
He destroys the calamities portended by evil omens such as the cracking of idols, earthquakes, and the falling of great meteors. Therefore, he is Durnimittahṛt (The Remover of Evil Omens).


Shloka 71: Duḥsvapnahṛt (दुःस्वप्नहृत्) & Prasahana (प्रसहन)
दुःस्वप्नमपि सुस्वप्नं कुर्वन् दुःस्वप्न-हृन्मतः ।
भक्तापराधान् क्षमते तेन प्रसहनः स्मृतः ॥ ७१ ॥
He is considered Duḥsvapnahṛt (The Remover of Bad Dreams) because he turns even a bad dream into a good dream. He is remembered as Prasahana (The Forgiver) because he forgives the offenses of his devotees.


Shloka 72: Guṇī (गुणी) & Nādapratiṣṭhita (नादप्रतिष्ठित)
विशोकविजिघत्सत्वप्रमुख्यैः स्यात् गुणैर्गुणी ।
अस्ति प्रणव-बिन्द्वन्तेऽर्धचन्द्राद्यक्षराष्टकम् ॥ ७२ ॥
तत्र नादस्तृतीयस्त-द्वाच्यो नादप्रतिष्ठितः ।
He is Guṇī (The Embodiment of Qualities) because he possesses qualities such as freedom from sorrow and an indomitable spirit. There exists the group of eight letters, beginning with 'Ardhacandra', at the end of the Praṇava (Oṁ) followed by the Bindu. Among them, the third is Nāda. He, who is denoted by that, is Nādapratiṣṭhita (Established in the Primordial Sound).


सुरूपः सर्वनेत्राधिवासो वीरासनाश्रयः ।
पीताम्बरः खण्डरदः खण्डवैशाखसंस्थितः ॥ १९॥
Shloka 73: Surūpa (सुरूप)
सुरूपो भूरिलावण्याद्यद्वा तत्त्वनिरूपणात् ॥ ७३ ॥
He is Surūpa (The Beautiful-One) because of his abundant loveliness, 
or because he is ascertained (Nirūpaṇa) as the ultimate reality (Tattva).


Conclusion of the First Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ ।
गणेशसहस्त्रनाम खद्योते प्रथमं शतम् ॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), composed by Bhāskara, 
Also known as Agnicit, ends the first hundred of the Ganesha Sahasranama.


Shloka 74: Sarvanetrādhivāsaka (सर्वनेत्राधिवास:)
सर्वनेत्रेषु वसति य एष क्षीणपूरुषः ।
एष आत्मेति वचनात् सर्वनेत्राधिवासकः ॥ ७४ ॥
This being, who is the Inner Controller (Kṣīṇapuruṣa), dwells in all eyes. Because of the Vedic statement, "This is the Self," he is Sarvanetrādhivāsaka (The One who Resides in All Eyes).


Shloka 75: Vīrāsanāśraya (वीरासनाश्रय)
वामजानूपरिगतदक्षपादोपवे-शनं ।
वीरासनं तथा स्थानादेष वीरासनाश्रयः ॥ ७५ ॥
The posture where the left heel is placed at the base and the right foot is placed upon it (for meditation) is called the Vīrāsana. Because he remains in that posture, he is Vīrāsanāśraya (The One Seated in the Hero's Pose).


Shloka 76: Pītāmbara (पीतांबर) & Khaṇḍarada (खण्डरद)
निपीतं गगनं येन यस्य पीतोऽथ वा पटः ।
स तु पीतांबरः खण्डरदो दन्तार्धधारणात् ॥ ७६ ॥
He by whom the sky is consumed, or he who wears a yellow (Pīta) garment (Paṭa), is Pītāmbara (The Yellow-Robed One). He is Khaṇḍarada (The One with a Broken Tusk) because he holds (only) half (Ardha) of a tusk (Danta).


चित्राङ्गः श्यामदशनो भालचन्द्रो हविर्भुजः ।
योगाधिपस्तारकस्थः पुरुषो गजकर्णकः ॥ २०॥


Shloka 77: Citrāṅkaśyāmadaśana (चित्रांकश्यामदशन) & Bhālacandra (भालचन्द्र)
अर्धचन्द्रं दधन्मूर्भा खण्डेन्दुकृतशेखरः ।
श्यामैकप्रचुरैश्चित्रैरंकिते रदने सति ॥ ७७ ॥
चित्रां-कश्यामदशन इत्येवं स्तूयसे बुधैः।
भालचन्द्र इति ख्यातो भाले चंद्रस्य धारणात् ॥ ७८ ॥
He who bears the half-moon on his forehead, making it his crest jewel; 
he whose teeth are marked with various and distinct black lines
—is praised by the wise as Citrāṅkaśyāmadaśana (The One with Uniquely Marked, Dark Teeth). 
He is famous as Bhālacandra (The One with the Moon on his Forehead) 
because he wears the moon (Candra) on his forehead (Bhāla).


Shloka 79: Aṣṭamīcandra (अष्टमीचंद्र) & Caturbhuja (चतुर्भुज)
अष्टमीचंद्रसदृशं ललाटं यस्य सोऽथवा ।
चत्वारो बाहवो यस्य विग्रहे स चतुर्भुजः ॥ ७९ ॥
He whose forehead resembles the moon on the eighth lunar day (Aṣṭamīcandra), or he who has four arms in his form, is Caturbhuja (The Four-Armed One).


Shloka 80: Yogādhipa (योगाधिप)
योगाचार्यावतारा ये लैंगेऽष्टाविंशतिः स्मृताः ।
लकुलीशादयस्तत्तद्रूपो योगाधिपो मतः ॥ ८० ॥
The twenty-eight incarnations of Yoga teachers, like Lakulīśa, are remembered in the Liṅga Purāṇa. Since he assumes all those forms, he is considered Yogādhipa (The Lord of Yoga).


Shloka 81: Tārakastha (तारकस्थ) & Puruṣa (पुरुष)
प्रणवेना-भिषेयत्वात् तारकस्थ इति स्मृतः ।
पूर्षु सर्वासु शेतेऽसौ साक्षित्वेनेति पूरुषः ॥ ८१ ॥
He is remembered as Tārakastha (Established in the Liberating Syllable, Oṁ) because he is to be anointed/worshipped with the Praṇava (Oṁ). He is Puruṣa (The Supreme Being) because he "reposes" in all bodies as the witness.


गणाधिराजो विजयः स्थिरो गजपतिर्ध्वजी ।
देवदेवः स्मरः प्राणदीपको वायुकीलकः ॥ २१॥




Shloka 82: Gajakarṇaka (गजकर्णक) & Gaṇādhīrāja (गणाधिराज)
यस्य कर्णौ गजस्येव तेनासौ गजकर्णकः ।
आधीयंते यत्र काव्ये गणा मेयरसादयः ॥ ८२ ॥
गणा-धिराजस्तेनासौ राजते प्रतिपाद्यते ।
He whose ears are like those of an elephant is therefore Gajakarṇaka (The Elephant-Eared One). He in whom the "hosts" (Gaṇas)—such as the measurable elements (Meya) and the aesthetic sentiments (Rasa)—are established in poetry (Kāvya), is proclaimed as Gaṇādhīrāja (The Supreme King of Hosts).


Shloka 83: Vijayasthira (विजयस्थिर)
भक्तानां विजये स्थैर्ये शालित्वाद् विजयस्थिरः ॥ ८३ ॥
He is Vijayasthira (The Steady One in Victory) because he excels in granting victory and steadfastness to his devotees.


Shloka 84: Gajapatidhvajī (गजपतिध्वजी) & Devadeva (देवदेव)
गजेंद्राढ्यो ध्वजोऽस्येति मतो गजपतिध्वजी ।
देवदेवो वासवादिदैवतैरप्युपास्यते ॥ ८४ ॥
He is considered Gajapatidhvajī (The One with the Elephant-Lord on his Banner) because his banner is marked with a mighty elephant. He is Devadeva (The God of Gods) because he is worshipped even by deities like Vāsava (Indra).


Shloka 85: Smaraprāṇadīpaka (स्मरप्राणदीपक)
रुद्रेण दग्धदेहोऽपि प्राणानुज्जीवयन्नयम् ।
मन्मथस्य स्मरप्राणदीपकत्वेन कीर्त्यते ॥ ८५ ॥
Although his body was burnt by Rudra (Shiva), he revived him (the love-god, Manmatha). Therefore, he is celebrated as Smaraprāṇadīpaka (The Restorer of Life to Smara/Manmatha).


विपश्चिद्वरदो नादो नादभिन्नमहाचलः ।
वराहरदनो मृत्युञ्जयो व्याघ्राजिनाम्बरः ॥ २२ ॥


Shloka 86: Vāyukīlaka (वायुकीलक) & Vipraścidvarada (विपश्चिद्वरद)
नवद्वारि तनौ प्राणान् स्तंभयन् वायुकीलकः ।
राज्ञे विपश्चिदाख्याय चतुर्वा त्रीन् वरं ददत् ॥८६॥
अग्नया-त्मना त्वं वासिष्ठो विपश्चिद्वरदः स्मृतः ।
He is Vāyukīlaka (The One who Restrains the Wind) because he arrests the vital breaths (Prāṇa) in the body with its nine doors. He is remembered as Vipraścidvarada (The Boon-Giver to the Wise Vipraścit) because, in the form of Agni (fire), he granted three or four boons to the wise king Vipraścit.


Shloka 87: Nādonnāda (नादोन्नाद) & Bhinnabalāhaka (भिन्नबलाहक)
अल्पनादैर्महानादै-रपि येन विदारिताः ।
मेघाः स कथ्यते नादोन्नाद-भिन्नबलाहकः ॥ ८७ ॥
He by whom the clouds were torn asunder with both subtle and thunderous sounds (Nāda and Unnāda), is called Nādonnāda (The Source of Sound and Thunder) and Bhinnabalāhaka (The Splitter of Clouds).


Shloka 88: Varāhadaṃṣṭrā (वराहदंष्ट्रा) & Śobhājit (शोभाजित्)
वराहदंष्ट्राशोभाजिदेकदंतविराजितः ॥८८॥
He is adorned (Śobhājit) with tusks like those of a boar (Varāhadaṃṣṭrā) and splendid with his single tusk (Ekadanta).


Shloka 89: Varāharadana (वराहरदन) & Mṛtyuñjaya (मृत्युंजय)
इति भाष्यपुराणोक्तेर्वराहरदनो मतः ।
कालमृत्युं प्रमादं वा हरन् मृत्युंजयो मतः ॥ ८९ ॥
According to the statements of the Puranas and this commentary, he is considered Varāharadana (The Boar-Tusked One). He is considered Mṛtyuñjaya (The Conqueror of Death) because he removes (Haran) Time-Death (Kāla-Mṛtyu) or negligence (Pramāda).


Shloka 90: Vyāghrājināmbara (व्याघ्राजिनांबर)
प्रमादं वा मृत्युमहं ब्रवी-मीति तु भारतात् ।
वस्ते शार्दूलचर्मेति स्मृतो व्याघ्राजि-नांबरः ॥ ९० ॥
As stated in the Mahābhārata, "I declare myself to be death and negligence." He is remembered as Vyāghrājināmbara (The One Clad in a Tiger's Skin) because he wears the hide of a tiger (Śārdūla-carma).


इच्छाशक्तिभवो देवत्राता दैत्यविमर्दनः ।
शम्भुवक्त्रोद्भवः शम्भुकोपहा शम्भुहास्यभूः ॥ २३॥






Shloka 91: Icchāśaktidhara (इच्छाशक्तिधर) & Devatrāta (देवत्रात)
जगत्सिसृक्षाधारित्वात् इच्छाशक्तिधरो मतः । 
देव-नाताऽखिलान्देवांस्त्रायते दैत्यजाद्भयात् ॥ ९१ ॥
He is considered Icchāśaktidhara (The Holder of the Power of Will) because he is the basis for the desire to create the universe. He is Devatrāta (The Protector of the Gods) because he protects (Trāyate) all the gods from the fear born of demons.


Shloka 92: Daityavimardana (दैत्यविमर्दन) & Śivānanāt (शिवाननात्)
अत एव हरन् देवरिपून् दैत्यविमर्दनः ।
शंभुवक्त्रोद्भवो योऽयम-वतीर्णः शिवाननात् ॥ ९२ ॥
For this very reason, he is Daityavimardana (The Crusher of Demons) as he destroys the enemies of the gods. He is the one who originated from the mouth of Shambhu (Shiva) and descended from Shiva's face.


Shloka 93: Śambhukopahā (शंभुकोपहा) & Śambhuhāsya (शंभुहास्य)
पितुः क्रोधं हरन् बाल-लीलाभिः शंभुकोपहा ।
अज्ञवत् कतिचित् क्रीडाः कुर्वाणः शंसुहास्यभूः ॥ ९३ ॥
He is Śambhukopahā (The Remover of Shambhu's Anger) as he removes the anger of his father (Shiva) through his childish plays. He is Śambhuhāsya (The Cause of Shambhu's Laughter) as, by performing some playful acts like an ignorant child, he becomes the reason for Shiva's laughter.


शम्भुतेजाः शिवाशोकहारी गौरीसुखावहः ।
उमाङ्गमलजो गौरीतेजोभूः स्वर्धुनीभवः ॥ २४॥


Shloka 94: Śambhutejā (शंभुतेजा)
शंभुतेजा इति प्रोक्तः शिवतेजोऽस्ति यस्य सः ।
महिषादि-हतौ भग्नोत्साहायावर्धयन् बलम् ॥ ९४ ॥
He is proclaimed as Śambhutejā (The Splendor of Shambhu) because Shiva's splendor exists in him. He increased the strength of the gods when their enthusiasm was broken after being slain by (demons like) Mahisha.


Shloka 95: Dākṣāyaṇyaśokahā (दाक्षायण्याशोकहा) & Gaurīsukhāvaha (गौरीसुखावह)
दाक्षायण्याः शिवा-शोकहारीति परिगीयते ।
हर्ष गौर्यास्तपस्यंत्याः कुर्वन् गौरीसुखावहः ॥ ९५ ॥
He is celebrated in songs as Dākṣāyaṇyaśokahā (The Remover of the Sorrow of Dākṣāyaṇī, i.e., Parvati). He is Gaurīsukhāvaha (The Bringer of Joy to Gaurī) as he creates joy for Gaurī (Parvati) while she was performing penance.


Shloka 96: Umāṅgamalaja (उमाङ्गमलज)
निजांगोद्वर्तनोत्थेन मलेन प्रति-मामुमा ।
चक्रे तदंतः प्रविशन् उमाङ्गमलजो मतः ॥ ९६ ॥
With the dirt (Mala) arising from rubbing her own body, Uma (Parvati) created an image (of a child). He, entering into that image, is considered Umāṅgamalaja (Born from the Dirt of Uma's Body).


Shloka 97: Gaurītejobhū (गौरीतेजोभू)
चंडिकाभूयमात्मानं विष्णुब्रह्मादितेजसाम् ।
आधारत्वादसौ गौरीतेजोभूरिति कथ्यते ॥ ९७ ॥
Having assumed the form of Caṇḍikā, and being the support of the splendor of Vishnu, Brahma, and others, he is called Gaurītejobhū (Become the Splendor of Gaurī).


Shloka 98: Svardhanībhava (स्वर्धनीभव)
स्कन्दो गंगोद्भवस्तस्माद-भेदात् स्वर्धनीभवः ।
स्वर्धन्या उद्भवो यस्मादिति वा स्वर्धनीभवः ॥ ९८ ॥
Skanda (Kartikeya) was born from Gaṅgā. Due to non-difference from him (i.e., being one with his brother), Ganesha is Svardhanībhava (Born from Svadhānī/Gaṅgā). Or, he is so called because he was born from Svadhānī (another name for Parvati).


यज्ञकायो महानादो गिरिवर्ष्मा शुभाननः ।
सर्वात्मा सर्वदेवात्मा ब्रह्ममूर्धा ककुप्श्रुतिः ॥ २५॥


Shloka 99: Yajñakāya (यज्ञकाय) & Mahānāda (महानाद)
यज्ञकायोऽश्वमेधादिर्मखजातस्वरूपवान् ।
उच्चैस्तरां वषङ्कारोऽस्तुश्रौषड्येयजामहे ॥ ९९ ॥
आश्रावयेत्यादिरूपो महानाद इति स्मृतः ।
He is Yajñakāya (The One whose Body is Sacrifice) as his form is constituted by sacrifices like the Aśvamedha. He is remembered as Mahānāda (The Great Sound) because he is of the nature of the loud utterances "Vaṣaṭ," "Astu Śrauṣaṭ," "Yejāmabe," "Āśrāvaya," etc., (which are Vedic ritual calls).


Shloka 100: Girivarmā (गिरिवर्मा) & Śubhānana (शुभानन)
गिरिवर्माद्रयो यस्य विराजो देहतां गताः ॥ १०० ॥
मंगलनामानं प्राणं जनयन् स शुभाननः ।
The mountains, beginning with Girivarmā, have become the forms of his divine manifestations. Generating the life-force which is of an auspicious nature, he is Śubhānana (The Auspicious-Faced One).


Shloka 101: Sarvātmā (सर्वात्मा)
सर्वात्माऽप्येष सर्व खल्विदं ब्रह्मेति तु श्रुतेः ॥ १०१ ॥
He is also Sarvātmā (The Soul of All) because, as the Śruti says, "All this is indeed Brahman."


Shloka 102: Sarvadevātmā (सर्वदेवात्मा) & Brahmamūrdhā (ब्रह्ममूर्धा)
देहभूता यस्य सर्वदेवात्माखिलदेवताः ।
ब्रह्मव यस्य मूर्धा स ब्रह्ममूर्धेति कथ्यते ॥ १०२ ॥
He whose body consists of all the deities, who is the soul of all gods, is Sarvadevātmā (The Soul of All Gods). He whose head is Brahman, is called Brahmamūrdhā (The One whose Head is Brahman).


ब्रह्माण्डकुम्भश्चिद्व्योमभालःसत्यशिरोरुहः ।
जगज्जन्मलयोन्मेषनिमेषोऽग्न्यर्कसोमदृक् ॥ २६॥


Shloka 103: Brahmāṇḍakumbha (ब्रह्माण्डकुम्भ)
कर्णभूता दिशो यस्य कथितः स ककुश्रुतिः ।
ब्रह्मांडखर्परैंयुतं न्युजं सद्यस्य कुम्भति ॥ १०३॥
विश्वात्मनः स ब्रह्माण्डकुम्भ इत्यभिधीयते ।
He whose ears are the directions is called Kakuśruti (not the main name here). The universe (Brahmāṇḍa), which is like a pot (Kumbha), constitutes his body. Therefore, the Universal Soul is called Brahmāṇḍakumbha (The One whose Pot is the Universe).


Shloka 104: Cidyomabhāla (चिद्योमभाल)
ब्रह्माण्डखर्परांतर्यलग्नं खं तच्चिदंबरं ॥१०४॥
तदस्य ब्रह्मरंध्रं चियोमभालः स उच्यते ।
The space (Kha) within the shell of the universe is the consciousness-filled sky (Cidambara). That is his Brahmarandhra (the aperture at the crown of the head). Therefore, he is called Cidyomabhāla (The One whose Forehead is the Sky of Consciousness).
Shloka 105: Satyaśiroruh (सत्यशिरोरुह)
सत्यलोको यस्य कैश्यमसौ सत्यशिरोरुहः॥१०५॥
He whose head-dress (or cranial hair) is the world of Satya (the highest heaven) is Satyaśiroruh (The One on whose Head Grows the Satya Loka).


Shloka 106: Jagajjanmalayonmeṣanimeṣa (जगज्जन्मलयोन्मेषनिमेष)
उन्मेषाज्जायते यस्य निमेषाल्लीयते जगत् ।
जगज्जन्मलयोन्मेषनिमेष इति स स्मृतः ॥ १०६ ॥
From his opening of the eyes (Unmeṣa) the world is born, and by his closing of the eyes (Nimeṣa) it is dissolved. Therefore, he is remembered as Jagajjanmalayonmeṣanimeṣa (The One whose Opening and Closing of Eyes is the Creation and Dissolution of the World).


Shloka 107: Abhyarkasomadṛk (अभ्यर्कसोमदृक्)
ऊर्ध्वदक्षिणवामानि नेत्राणि जगदा-त्मनः ।
वह्निसूर्यैदवो यस्य क्रमात्सोऽभ्यर्कसोमदृक् ॥१०७॥
The upper, right, and left eyes of the Universal Soul are Agni (fire), Sūrya (sun), and Candra (moon) respectively. He, who possesses these in order, is Abhyarkasomadṛk (The One who has the Sun and Moon as his Eyes).
गिरीन्द्रैकरदो धर्माधर्मोष्ठः सामबृंहितः ।
ग्रहर्क्षदशनो वाणीजिह्वो वासवनासिकः ॥ २७॥


Shloka 108: Girīndraikarada (गिरींद्रैकरद)
एकस्थूलो बहिर्दतो गिरींद्रः कनकाचलः ।
विराड्ररूपजुषो यस्य गिरींद्रेकरदो हि सः ॥ १०८ ॥
He who has one large, external tusk and who is the Lord of Mountains (Girīndra), assuming the form of the cosmic being (Virāṭ), is indeed Girīndraikarada (The Lord of Mountains with a Single Tusk).


Commentary on a Previous Interpretation:
Sanskrit:
यत्तु-प्राचा गिरींद्रपदं हिमवत्परत्वेन व्याख्यातं, तत्सुमेरुकूटदंतायेति गणेशस्तव-राजविरुद्धत्वादनादेयम् ।
However, the previous interpretation which explains 'Girīndra' as referring to the Himalaya mountain is unacceptable, as it contradicts the description in the Ganesha-stava-rāja, which identifies him as the one whose tusk is the peak of Mount Meru.


Shloka 109: Dharma-adharmoṣṭha (धर्माधर्मोष्ठ)
पापपुण्ये यदोष्ठौ स धर्माधर्मोष्ठ उच्यते ।
धर्मोत्तरोष्ठो दुरिता-धरोष्ठ इति च स्मृतेः ॥ १०९ ॥
He whose two lips are righteousness and unrighteousness (Pāpa-Puṇya) is called Dharma-adharmoṣṭha (The One whose Lips are Dharma and Adharma). It is remembered that his upper lip is Dharma and his lower lip is sin.


Shloka 110: Sāmabṛṃhita (सामबृंहित)
यद्वं हितं सामवेदः स प्रोक्तः सामबृंहितः ।
स्तोत्रात्मकैः सामभिस्तोषित इति प्राचामर्थो...
सूर्योदयो ग्रहा यस्य कृत्तिकाद्याश्च तारकाः ॥ ११० ॥
He who is praised by the hymns of the Sāma Veda is proclaimed as Sāmabṛṃhita (The One Magnified by the Sāma Veda). (The commentator rejects an alternate interpretation as unsuitable.)


Shloka 111: Grahakṣedaśana (ग्रहक्षेदशन)
मुखांतःस्थाः सूक्ष्मदंतो ग्रहक्षेदशनो हि सः ॥ १११ ॥
He who has subtle teeth within his mouth, and who is the destroyer of the afflictions caused by planets, is Grahakṣedaśana (The Destroyer of Planetary Afflictions).


Shloka 112: Vāṇījihva (वाणीजिह्व) & Vāsavanāsika (वासवनासिक)
पुराणन्यायमीमांसाथर्वऋग्यजुषादिकाः ॥ १११ ॥
वाक्तती रसना यस्य स वाणीजिह्व उच्यते ॥
नासा यस्य शुनासीरः सोऽयं वासवनासिकः ॥ ११२ ॥
He whose tongue is the flow of speech—consisting of the Purāṇas, Nyāya, Mīmāṃsā, Atharva, Ṛg, and Yajur Vedas—is called Vāṇījihva (The One whose Tongue is Speech). He whose nose is the deity Śuna-sīra (Indra) is Vāsavanāsika (The One whose Nose is Indra).


कुलाचलांसः सोमार्कघण्टो रुद्रशिरोधरः ।
नदीनदभुजः सर्पाङ्गुलीकस्तारकानखः ॥ २८॥


Shloka 113: Kulācala (कुलाचल) & Somārkaghaṇṭa (सोमार्कघंट)
कुलाचलांसो विंध्याद्या यस्य स्कंधतया मताः ।
कंध-रोर्ध्वं मांसपिण्डावुन्नतौ घंटिके मते ॥ ११३ ॥
ते रवींदू यस्य सोमार्कघंटः कथितो हि सः।
He whose shoulders are the mountain ranges like the Vindhyas is Kulācala (The One whose Shoulders are Mountains). The two fleshy protuberances above his neck are considered to be the "bells" (Ghaṇṭike). He in whom the sun and moon are those two bells is declared as Somārkaghaṇṭa (The One with the Moon and Sun as Bells).


Shloka 114: Rudraśirodhara (रुद्रशिरोधर) & Nadīnadabhuja (नदीनदभुज)
यस्य ग्रीवा रुद्र एव सवै रुद्रशिरोधरः ॥ ११४ ॥
गंगाशोणादयो नद्यो नदा यस्य भुजाः स तु ॥ ११५ ॥
नदीनदभुजः सिंधुनदी नंदति वा भुजः ।
He whose neck is Rudra himself is Rudraśirodhara (The One who Bears Rudra as his Neck). He whose arms are rivers like the Ganga and Sonā is Nadīnadabhuja (The One whose Arms are Rivers). Or, he whose arms delight (Nandati) the ocean (Sindhu) and the rivers.


Shloka 116: Sarpāṅgulīka (सर्पाङ्गुलीक) & Tārakānakha (तारकानख)
सर्पागु-लीकः शेषाद्या अंगुल्यो यस्य पन्नगाः।
ध्रुवाद्या यन्नखास्ताराः स स्मृतस्तारकानखः ॥ ११६ ॥
He whose fingers are serpents like Śeṣa is Sarpāṅgulīka (The One whose Fingers are Serpents). He whose nails are the stars, beginning with Dhruva (the Pole Star), is remembered as Tārakānakha (The One whose Nails are Stars).


भ्रूमध्यसंस्थितकरो ब्रह्मविद्यामदोदकः ।
व्योमनाभिः श्रीहृदयो मेरुपृष्ठोऽर्णवोदरः ॥ २९॥


Shloka 117: Śaṇḍādaṇḍa (शंडादंड)
भ्रूमध्यसंस्थितकरो ध्रुवोर्घाणस्य संधिगम् ।
यत्तत्त्वं श्रुतिषूक्तं तच्छंडादंडात्म यस्य सः ॥ ११७ ॥
The principle (Tattva) stated in the Vedic scriptures (Śruti), which is situated between the eyebrows and is the support of the nose, is of the nature of Śaṇḍa and Daṇḍa. He whose essence is that is Śaṇḍādaṇḍa (The One whose Essence is Śaṇḍa and Daṇḍa).


Shloka 118: Brahmavidyāmadotkaṭa (ब्रह्मविद्यामदोत्कट)
प्रपंचमानापहृत्या ब्रह्मविद्यैव वै मद ।
तेनोद्भिन्नौ कटौ यस्य ब्रह्मविद्यामदो-त्कटः ॥ ११८ ॥
The intoxication (Mada) born from the knowledge of Brahman (Brahmavidyā) removes the sense of phenomenal reality. He whose hips (Kaṭi) are swollen with that (intoxication) is Brahmavidyāmadotkaṭa (The One Intoxicated with the Knowledge of Brahman).


Names 119-129: The Cosmic Body of Ganesha
The commentary continues to describe Ganesha's body as a microcosm of the universe:


119: Vyomanābhi (व्योमनाभि): 
यस्य व्योमबिलं नाभिर्योमनाभिरसौ मतः ।
ऋचः सामानि यजूंषि सा हि श्रीरमृता सतां ॥ ११९ ॥
He whose navel is the ethereal cavity (of space) is considered Vyomanābhi (The One whose Navel is the Sky). The Rig, Sāma, and Yajur Vedas are that (navel), which is the divine prosperity (Śrī) and the nectar (Amṛta) for the virtuous.


120: Śrīhṛdaya (श्रीहृदय) & Merupṛṣṭha (मेरुपृष्ठ): 
त्रैय्यर्थ एव यच्चित्तं स श्रीहृदय उच्यते ।
सुमेरुरेव यत्पृष्ठं मेरुपृष्ठः स उच्यते ॥ १२० ॥
He whose heart/mind is the very essence of the three Vedas is called Śrīhṛdaya (The One whose Heart is Śrī). He whose back is Mount Meru itself is called Merupṛṣṭha (The One whose Back is Meru).


कुक्षिस्थयक्षगन्धर्वरक्षःकिन्नरमानुषः ।
पृथ्वीकटिः सृष्टिलिङ्गः शैलोरुर्दस्रजानुकः ॥ ३०॥


Shloka 121: Arṇavodara (अर्णवोदर) & Kukṣiṣṭha-yakṣa-gandharva-rakṣaḥ-kinnara-mānuṣa (कुक्षिष्ठयक्षगन्धर्वरक्षःकिन्नरमानुष)
जठरांतर्जल-स्थानेऽर्णवा इत्यर्णवोदरः ।
अंत्राद्यवयवाः कुक्षौ यस्य यक्षादयः स तु ॥१२१॥
He is Arṇavodara (The Ocean-Bellied One) because the oceans (Arṇavāḥ) are in the watery region inside his belly. He in whose abdomen beings like Yakṣas, Gandharvas, Rākṣasas, Kinnaras, and humans reside as its internal organs is Kukṣiṣṭha-yakṣa-gandharva-rakṣaḥ-kinnara-mānuṣa (The One in whose Womb Dwell Yakṣas, Gandharvas, Rākṣasas, Kinnaras, and Humans).






122: Pṛthvīkaṭi (पृथ्वीकटि): 
कुक्षि-स्थयक्षगंधर्वरक्षः किन्नरमानुषः ।
पृथिव्येव कटिस्थानं यस्य पृथ्वीकटिस्तु सः ॥ १२२ ॥
He in whom the earth itself is the location of his waist/hips is Pṛthvīkaṭi (The Earth-Hipped One).


123: Śailorū (शैलोरु): 
सृष्टिलिंगस्तु यच्छेपः-स्थाने मैथुनजाः प्रजाः ।
शैलोरुः पर्वता एव यस्योरुस्थानतां गताः ॥ १२३ ॥
He is Śailorū (The Mountain-Thighed One) because the mountains themselves have become his thighs.


124: Dasrajānuka (दस्रजानुक) & Pātālajaṅgha (पातालजंघ): 
दस्रौ यस्याश्विनौ जानू स मतो दस्रजानुकः ।
पातालजंऋो यज्जंघात्मकं पातालसप्तकं ॥ १२४॥
He whose knees are the two Aśvini Kumaras (also called Dasra) is considered Dasrajānuka (The One with the Aśvinis as Knees). He is Pātālajaṅgha (The One whose Shanks are the Netherworlds) because the seven netherworlds (Pātāla-saptaka) constitute his shanks.




पातालजङ्घो मुनिपात्कालाङ्गुष्ठस्त्रयीतनुः ।
ज्योतिर्मण्डललाङ्गूलो हृदयालाननिश्चलः ॥ ३१॥


125: Muni-pāt (मुनिपात) & Kālāṅguṣṭha (कालांगुष्ठ): 
मुनि-पात् मुनिभिः पादलग्नैः पादात्मतां गतैः ।
कालांगुष्ठो महा-मृत्युर्यत्पादांगुलितां गतः ॥ १२५ ॥
He is Munipāt (The One whose Feet are the Sages) because the sages, who are attached to his feet, have become his feet. He is Kālāṅguṣṭha (The One whose Toes are Time) because Great Death (Mahāmṛtyu, a form of Time) has become his toes.


126: Trayītanu (त्रयीतनु)  & Jyotirmaṇḍalalāḍūla (ज्योतिर्मण्डललाडूल): 
अयुक्तावयवा यस्य श्रुतयः स त्रयीतनुः ।
शिंशुमाराभिधं ज्योतिश्चक्रं पुच्छं हि यस्य सः ॥ १२६ ॥
He whose body is the three Vedas (which have no physical limbs) is Trayītanu (The One whose Body is the Three Vedas). He is (also called Jyotirmaṇḍalalāḍūla) because the constellation known as Śiśumāra (the dolphin) is his tail.


127: Hṛdayālāna (हृदयालान): 
ज्योतिर्मण्डललाडूलो ब्रह्मात्रोक्तं गजाकृति ।
भक्तानां हृदयान्येवास्यालानं गजबंधनम् ॥१२७॥
The hearts of his devotees themselves are the post to which this 
elephant-form is tied (ālāna - the tying post for an elephant).


हृत्पद्मकर्णिकाशाली वियत्केलिसरोवरः ।
सद्भक्तध्याननिगडः पूजावारिनिवारितः ॥ ३२॥


128: Hṛdantaradaharakrīḍāsara (हृदन्तर्दहरक्रीडासर): 
तत्रापगतचाञ्चल्यो हृदयालाननिश्चलः । 
हृदंतर्दहराकाशं यस्य क्रीडासरः स तु ॥ १२८ ॥
He who, having abandoned all restlessness, is steadfastly fixed at the post of the heart. 
He for whom the subtle space (Daharakāśa) within the heart is a pleasure-lake (krīḍāsaraḥ).


129: Sadbhaktadhyānanigaḍa (सद्भक्तध्याननिगड): 
हृत्पद्म-कर्णिकाशालिवियत्केलिसरोवरः । 
सर्वगोऽपि परिच्छिन्नो भक्तैर्ध्यानेन यः स तु ॥ १२९ ॥
He for whom the lotus of the heart, with the sky in its pericarp, is a playful lake. 
Though all-pervading (sarvagaḥ), he is localized (paricchinnaḥ) and 
bound (nigaḍa - fettered) by the meditation (dhyānena) of his true devotees.


Shloka 130: Pūjāvārinivārita (पूजावारीनिवारित)
पूजैव कथिता वारी गजबंधनशृङ्खला ।
संनिरुद्धस्तया देवः पूजावारीनिवारितः ॥ १३० ॥
Worship (Pūjā) itself is said to be the water (Vārī) and the chain for binding the elephant. The Lord, who is restrained by that, is Pūjāvārinivārita (The One Restrained by the Water of Worship).


यत्तु -प्राचा वारीपदं ह्रस्वांतं पठता पूजां वारयंतीति पूजावारिणो राक्षसाः, तैरयं दूरीकृतोऽसेवित इति व्याख्यातं, तादृशार्थेन कीदृशी देवतास्तुतिः संपद्यत इति चिंत्यम् ।
Now, regarding this: The earlier commentators, reading the word 'vārī' with a short 'i' (as 'vāri'), have explained it (as follows): "The 'pūjā-vāriṇaḥ' are demons (Rākṣasas) who ward off (vārayanti) worship (pūjā). By them, this Lord (Ganesha) was driven away and not served." One must ponder, what kind of praise of the deity is accomplished by such a meaning?


The Commentator's Rebuttal and Correct Interpretation
ह्रस्वांत एव पाठ इति यदि शपथे प्रवर्तसे तदापि वारिपदं हि परिखाजलपरत्वेन व्याख्याय तदवगुंठितः पूजकैर्दुरधिगम इति व्याख्येयम्;
Even if you insist on taking an oath that the reading is indeed with a short 'i', even then, the word 'vāri' should be interpreted as meaning 'moat-water' (the water in a fortress trench). Thus, the meaning should be: "He who is concealed by a moat, is difficult for worshippers to approach."


पूजा-रंभे देवतायाः संनिरोधनावगुंठनविषयकप्रार्थनादेर्विधानात् ।
...because the ritual injunctions prescribe that at the beginning of worship, there are prayers, etc., for the confinement and veiling (saṃnirodhana, avaguṃṭhana) of the deity (to invite and secure the divine presence in the ritual area).


Conclusion of the Argument and the Next Name
संनिरोधनावगुंठनविषयकप्रार्थनादेर्विधानात् ।
...because the ritual injunctions prescribe that at the beginning of worship, there are prayers, etc., for the confinement (saṃnirodhana) and veiling (avaguṃṭhana) of the deity (to invite and secure the divine presence in the ritual area).


प्रतापी काश्यपो मन्ता गणको विष्टपी बली ।
यशस्वी धार्मिको जेता प्रथमः प्रमथेश्वरः ॥ ३३॥


Names 131-135: Various Glorious Names
Shloka 131: Pratāpī (प्रतापी): 
प्रतापी तापकत्वेन पराक्रमयुतोऽथवा ॥ १३१ ॥
He is Pratāpī (The One with Blazing Splendor) because of his power to 
scorch (his enemies), or because he is endowed with valor.
Shloka 132: Kaśyapasuta (कश्यपसुत): 
कश्य-पस्य मुनेः कुक्षाववतीर्ण इति स्मृतः । 
कथापुराणे गाणेशे स कश्यपसुतो मतः ॥१३२॥
He is remembered as one who descended into the womb of the sage Kaśyapa. 
In the Ganesha Purāṇa, he is considered the son of Kaśyapa.
Shloka 133: Gaṇapa (गणप), Viṣṭapī (विष्टपी) & Balī (बली)
गणपोऽध्वर्युहोत्रादीन् पातीति चतुरो गणान् ।
विष्टपी भुवनाधारो बलसामान्यवान् बली ॥ १३३ ॥
He is Gaṇapa (The Protector of the Hosts) because he protects the four hosts: the Adhvaryu, Hotṛ, and other priests. He is Viṣṭapī (The Supporter of the Worlds) because he supports the worlds. He is Balī (The Powerful One) because he possesses common and extraordinary strength.


Shloka 134: Yaśasvī (यशस्वी), Dhārmika (धार्मिक) & Svojas (स्वोजस्)
यशस्वी पुण्यकीर्त्याढ्यो धार्मिको धर्मवर्धकः ।
अष्टमो धातुरोजः स्यात् खोजास्तद्यस्य शोभनं ॥ १३४ ॥
He is Yaśasvī (The Glorious One), abundant with pious fame. He is Dhārmika (The Righteous One), the augmenter of Dharma. The eighth derivative of the root 'dhā' is 'ojas', meaning splendor. He whose splendor (Ojas) is splendid (Sva) is Svojas (The One of Innate Splendor).


Shloka 135: Prathama (प्रथम) & Prathameśvara (प्रथमेश्वर): 
प्रथमः सर्वकार्यादौ पूज्यत्वात्सर्वदेहिनाम् । 
प्रथमा ब्रह्मविष्ण्वी-शास्तदीशः प्रथमेश्वरः ॥ १३५ ॥
