He is Prathama (The First) because he is worshipped at the beginning of all undertakings by all beings. He is Prathameśvara (The First Lord) because he is the lord of the first (primary) deities: Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Īśa (Shiva).


चिन्तामणिर्द्वीपपतिः कल्पद्रुमवनालयः ।
रत्नमण्डपमध्यस्थो रत्नसिंहासनाश्रयः ॥ ३४॥


Names 136-138: Cintāmaṇi-dvīpapatiḥ, Kalpadruma-vanālayaḥ, Ratnamaṇḍapa-madhyasthaḥ, Ratnasiṃhāsanāśrayaḥ (चिन्तामणिर्द्वीपपतिः, कल्पद्रुमवनालयः, रत्नमण्डपमध्यस्थ:, रत्नसिंहासनाश्रयः)
चिंतामणिद्वीपपूर्व रत्नसिंहासनावधि ।
यस्योपभोग्यं संप्रोक्तश्चतुर्भिरिह नामभिः ॥ १३६ ॥
His enjoyable realm, described by four names here, extends from the "Isle of the Wish-Fulfilling Gem" in the east to the "Jeweled Throne." He is: Cintāmaṇi-dvīpapatiḥ (The Lord of the Isle of the Wish-Fulfilling Gem), Kalpadruma-vanālayaḥ (The One whose Grove is the Wish-Fulfilling Trees), Ratnamaṇḍapa-madhyasthaḥ (The One Seated in the Middle of the Jeweled Pavilion), and Ratnasiṃhāsanāśrayaḥ (The One Abiding on the Jeweled Throne).


तीव्राशिरोद्धृतपदो ज्वालिनीमौलिलालितः ।
नन्दानन्दितपीठश्रीर्भोगदो भूषितासनः ॥ ३५॥
सकामदायिनीपीठः स्फुरदुग्रासनाश्रयः ।
तेजोवतीशिरोरत्नं सत्यानित्यावतंसितः ॥ ३६॥


Tīvrāśiroddhṛtapadaḥ, Jvālinīmaulilālitaḥ, Nandānanditapīṭhaśrīḥ, Bhogadābhūṣitāsanaḥ, Sakāmadāyinīpīṭhaḥ, Sphuradugrāsanāśrayaḥ (तीव्राशिरोद्धृतपद:, ज्वालिनीमौलिलालित:, नन्दानन्दितपीठश्री:, भोगदाभूषितासन:, सकामदायिनीपीठः, स्फुरदुग्रासनाश्रय:)
तीव्रा च ज्वालिनी नंदा भोगदा कामदायिनी ।
उग्रा तेजोवती सत्या नवमी विघ्ननाशिनी ॥ १३७ ॥
पत्राष्टके कर्णिकायां संत्येताः पीठशक्तयः।
नवैताः पद्मसहिताः कथिता दशनामभिः ॥ १३८ ॥
Tīvrā, Jvālinī, Nandā, Bhogadā, Kāmadāyinī, Ugrā, Tejovatī, Satyā, and the ninth, Vighnanāśinī—these (nine) Śaktis reside on the eight petals and the pericarp of the (lotus) seat. These nine, along with the lotus itself, are described by ten names. These names are: Tīvrāśiroddhṛtapadaḥ (The One whose Seat is Elevated by Tīvrā), Jvālinīmaulilālitaḥ (The One Adorned by Jvālinī on His Head), Nandānanditapīṭhaśrīḥ (The One whose Seat is Delighted by Nandā), Bhogadābhūṣitāsanaḥ (The One whose Seat is Adorned by Bhogadā), Sakāmadāyinīpīṭhaḥ (The One whose Seat is Kāmadāyinī), and Sphuradugrāsanāśrayaḥ (The Abode of the Radiant and Terrible Seat). Here, with this last name, the second hundred is completed.


Conclusion of the Second Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ ॥
गणेशनामखद्योते द्वितीयमभवच्छतम् ॥ २०० ॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), composed by Bhāskara, 
also known as Agnicit, ends the second hundred of the Ganesha Thousand Names.


सविघ्ननाशिनीपीठः सर्वशक्त्यम्बुजाश्रयः ।
लिपिपद्मासनाधारो वह्निधामत्रयाश्रयः ॥ ३७॥






Shloka 139: Sarvaśakty-ambujāśrayaḥ (सर्वशक्त्यम्बुजाश्रयः)
सर्वशत्क्त्तयंबुजाश्रय इति ॥
अत्र पूर्वोक्तनवकपरं सर्वपद-मज्ञात्वा यत्प्राचोक्तं सर्वशक्तिर्नाम काचिदन्वर्थनामा दशमी शक्तिरेतादृशशक्तयो दशदिक्स्था इति, तदिदं मंत्रशास्त्र-ज्ञानाभावप्रयुक्तप्रलपनमित्युपेक्ष्यं ।
(The name is) Sarvaśakty-ambujāśrayaḥ (The Abode of the Lotus of All Powers). Here, not understanding that the word "Sarva" refers to the previously mentioned group of nine Śaktis, some previous commentators have said that "Sarvaśakti" is the name of a tenth Śakti... This is mere babbling born from a lack of knowledge of Mantra Śāstra and is to be disregarded.
Names 140-141: Lipipadmāsanādhāraḥ (लिपिपद्मासनाधार:) & Vahnidhāmatrayāśrayaḥ (वह्निधामत्रयाश्रयः)
द्विद्विस्वरककिंजल्कं पद्म-प्रासादकर्णिकं ।
कादिवर्गाष्टकदलं मातृकापद्ममुच्यते ॥१४०॥
तत्रस्थो लिपिपद्मासनाधार इति कथ्यते ।
कर्णिकोपर्युपर्यकैं-द्वन्याख्यं तेजसां त्रयं ॥ १४१ ॥
आस्तीर्ण तत्र निवसन् वह्निधामत्रयाश्रयः।
The "Mātṛkā Padma" (Lotus of the Letters) is described as having the vowels as its filaments, the palace (of consciousness) as its pericarp, and the eight groups of consonants as its eight petals. He, being situated there, is called Lipipadmāsanādhāraḥ (The Support of the Seat of the Lotus of Letters). Above the pericarp is the triad of lights known as Ka, E, and Dvanda. He, spread out and residing there, is the Vahnidhāmatrayāśrayaḥ (The Abode of the Three Dwellings of Fire).


यत्तु प्राचा मातृकाचक्रोपरि पद्मासनं वह्निसूर्यसोममया अंतरंतस्त्रयः प्राकारास्तदंतर्गणेशस्य गृह-मिति नामद्वयं व्याख्यातं, तदसांप्रदायिकत्वादुपेक्ष्यम् ।
"However, what has been explained by previous commentators—that the 'Lotus Seat' upon the matrix of letters (Mātṛkācakra), along with the three fortifications (Prākāra) within, made of fire, sun, and moon, constitutes the house of Ganesha, and they have interpreted the two names (Lipi-padmāsanādhāra and Vahnidhāmatrayāśraya) accordingly—that, due to its non-conformity with the established tradition (Asāmpradāyikatvāt), is to be disregarded."


उन्नतप्रपदो गूढगुल्फः संवृतपार्ष्णिकः ।
पीनजङ्घः श्लिष्टजानुः स्थूलोरुः प्रोन्नमत्कटिः ॥ ३८॥


Shloka 142: Unnataprapada (उन्नतप्रपद)
पादाग्रे उन्नते यस्य सोन्नतप्रपदो मतः ॥ १४२ ॥
कूर्मपृष्ठसमाकारभाव औन्नत्यमीरितम् ।
He whose forepart of the foot is raised is considered Unnataprapada (The One with Raised Forefeet). The raised part is said to be shaped like a tortoise's back.
Shloka 143: Gūḍhagulpha (गूढगुल्फ)
पादमूलगतावस्थि-ग्रंथी आच्छादितौ यदि ॥ १४३ ॥
नोन्नतत्वेन दृश्येते गूढ-गुल्फस्तदोच्यते ।
If the bony knots (the ankle bones) at the base of his feet are covered and 
not visible as protrusions, he is then called Gūḍhagulpha (The One with Concealed Ankles).


Shloka 144: Saṃvṛttapārṣṇika (संवृत्तपार्ष्णिक)
गुल्फाधोभागयोर्मासपूर्त्या संवृत्तपा-ष्णिकः ॥१४४॥
He whose heel-portion is rounded due to fullness of flesh below 
the ankles is Saṃvṛttapārṣṇika (The One with Rounded Heels).


Shloka 145: Pīnajaṅgha (पीनजङ्घ) & Śliṣṭajānuka (श्लिष्टजानुक)
पिंडिके मांसले यस्य पीनजङ्घः स कथ्यते ।
जंघोरुसंधियुग्मं चेदस्पष्टं श्लिष्टजानुकः ॥१४५॥
He whose calves (Piṇḍikā) are fleshy is called Pīnajaṅgha (The One with Fleshy Calves). If the pair of joints between his calves and thighs are indistinct, he is Śliṣṭajānuka (The One with Indistinct Knees).


Shloka 146: Sthūloru (स्थूलोरु) & Pronnamatkaṭi (प्रोन्नमत्कटि)
स्थूलोरु-रूरुयुगुलं यस्य स स्मृतः ।
आनघोन्नमत्कटिर्यस्य स प्रोक्तः प्रोन्नमत्कटिः ॥ १४६ ॥
He who has a pair of thick thighs is remembered as Sthūloru (The One with Thick Thighs). He whose hips are prominently raised is declared as Pronnamatkaṭi (The One with Protruding Hips).


निम्ननाभिः स्थूलकुक्षिः पीनवक्षा बृहद्भुजः ।
पीनस्कन्धः कम्बुकण्ठो लम्बोष्ठो लम्बनासिकः ॥ ३९॥


Shloka 147: Nimnanābhi (निम्ननाभि), Sthūlakukṣi (स्थूलकुक्षि), Pīnavakṣā (पीनवक्षा), Vṛhadbhuja (बृहद्भुज)
गांभीर्यतो निम्ननाभिः स्थूलकुक्षिः पिचंडिलः।
व्यूढो-रस्कः पीनवक्षा आजानुत्वाद्वृहद्भुजः ॥ १४७॥
Due to his profundity, he is Nimnanābhi (The One with a Deep Navel). He is Sthūlakukṣi (The Large-Bellied One). He is Pīnavakṣā (The Full-Breasted One). Because his arms reach his knees, he is Vṛhadbhuja (The Long-Armed One).






Shloka 148: Pīnaskandha (पीनस्कंध) & Kambukaṇṭha (कम्बुकण्ठ)
पीनस्कंधो मांसलांसः कम्बुकण्ठोऽनकंधरः ।
वलित्रितयसंयुक्तः कण्ठो यस्य स कीर्तितः ॥१४८॥
He is Pīnaskandha (The Broad-Shouldered One). He is Kambukaṇṭha (The One with a Conch-Neck), whose neck is adorned with three folds.


Shloka 149: Lamboṣṭha (लम्बोष्ठ) & Lambanāsika (लम्बनासिक)
लंबमानाधरोष्ठत्वाल्लं बोष्ठः स्याङ्गजा-ननः ।
शुण्डादण्डो लंबमानो यस्यासौ लम्बनासिकः ॥१४९॥
Because his lower lip is hanging down, the elephant-faced one (Aṅgajānana) is Lamboṣṭha (The Long-Lipped One). He whose trunk (Śuṇḍādaṇḍa) is hanging down is Lambanāsika (The Long-Nosed One).


भग्नवामरदस्तुङ्गसव्यदन्तो महाहनुः ।
ह्रस्वनेत्रत्रयः शूर्पकर्णो निबिडमस्तकः ॥ ४०॥


Shloka 150: Bhagnavāmarada (भग्नवामरद) & Tuṅgasavyadanta (तुङ्गसव्यदन्त)
भग्नवामरदो यस्य छिन्नाग्रो बामदन्तकः ।
दन्तोऽस्य दक्षिणस्तुंगः सव्यदन्तो यदुन्नतः ॥ १५० ॥
He whose left tusk is broken is Bhagnavāmarada (The One with a Broken Left Tusk). He whose right tusk is lofty and raised is Tuṅgasavyadanta (The One with a Lofty Right Tusk).


Shloka 151: Mahāhannu (महाहनु)
कपोलपरभा-गस्य महत्त्वेन महाहनुः ।
गजस्य यादृशं वक्रं तद्योग्यपरि-माणकम् ॥१५१॥
Due to the largeness of the posterior part of his cheeks, he is Mahāhannu (The Large-Jawed One). 
(His face is) curved like an elephant's and of a fitting proportion.


Shloka 152: Hrasvanetra (ह्रस्वनेत्र) & Śūrpakarṇa (शूर्पकर्ण)
न लोचनमनेनासौ ह्रस्वनेत्रत्रयः स्मृतः ।
यस्य शूर्पाकृती कर्णौ शूर्पकर्णः स उच्यते ॥ १५२ ॥
He is not (just) a being with (two) eyes; he is remembered as Hrasvanetra (The Three-Small-Eyed One). He whose ears are winnowing-basket-shaped (śūrpākṛtī) is called Śūrpakarṇa (The Winnowing-Basket-Eared One).


स्तबकाकारकुम्भाग्रो रत्नमौलिर्निरङ्कुशः ।
सर्पहारकटीसूत्रः सर्पयज्ञोपवीतवान् ॥ ४१॥


Shloka 153: Nibiḍamastaka (निबिडमस्तक) & Stabakākāra-kumbhāgra (स्तबकाकारकुम्भाग्र)
घन-त्वादतिकाठिन्यान्मूर्भो निबिडमस्तकः ।
स्तबकाकारकुंभाग्रो गुच्छोपमशिरोञ्चलः ॥ १५३ ॥
Due to its density and great hardness, his head is Nibiḍamastaka (The Dense-Headed One). The front of his temple is shaped like a flower-cluster, hence He is Stabakākāra-kumbhāgra (The One whose Temple-Front is like a Flower-Cluster).


Shloka 154: Ratnamaulī (रत्नमौलि) & Niraṅkuśa (निरंकुश)
रत्नोत्कीर्णकिरीटाढ्यो रत्नमौलिरुदीरितः ।
कुंभयोरंकुशा-स्पर्शात्स्वातंत्र्याद्वा निरंकुशः ॥ १५४ ॥
Adorned with a crown engraved with jewels, he is declared as Ratnamaulī (The Jewel-Crowned One). Because the goad (Aṅkuśa) touches his temples, or due to his absolute independence, he is Niraṅkuśa (The Unrestrained One).


सर्पकोटीरकटकः सर्पग्रैवेयकाङ्गदः ।
सर्पकक्षोदराबन्धः सर्पकक्ष्योदराबन्ध: ॥ ४२॥


Shloka 155: Sarpahāra-kaṭisūtra (सर्पहारकटिसूत्र), Sarpayajñopavītavān (सर्पयज्ञोपवीतवान्) & Sarpakoṭīra-kaṭaka (सर्पकोटीरकटक)
सर्पहारकटीसूत्रः सर्पयज्ञोपवीतवान् ।
सर्पकोटीरकटकः सर्पों यन्मौलिकंकणे ॥ १५५ ॥
He who wears serpents as his garland and his girdle is Sarpahāra-kaṭisūtra (The One with Serpents as His Garland and Girdle). He who has a serpent as his sacred thread is Sarpayajñopavītavān (The One with a Serpent Sacred Thread). He who wears serpents as his crown and his bracelets is Sarpakoṭīra-kaṭaka (The One with Serpents as His Diadem and Bracelets).


Shloka 156: Sarpagrāiveyakāṅgada (सर्पग्रैवेयकाङ्गद) & Sarpakakṣyodarābandha (सर्पकक्ष्योदराबन्ध)
सर्पात्मकण्ठदोर्भूषः सर्पग्रैवेयकांगदः ।
लक्ष्या च कटि-बंधाख्या कक्ष्याबंधश्च पन्नगौ ॥१५६॥
He who wears serpents as his necklace and his armlets is Sarpagrāiveyakāṅgada (The One with Serpents as His Necklace and Armlets). He for whom the two serpents known as the waist-girdle and the hip-girdle are visible is Sarpakakṣyodarābandha (The One with Serpents as His Girdle and Belly-Band).


Shloka 157: Sarpottarīyaka (सर्पोत्तरीयक)
यस्यासाते सर्पकक्ष्यो-दराबन्धः स कीर्तितः ।
'उदराबंध' इति दैर्ध्यमार्षमिति प्राञ्चः ।
सोत्तरीयो वासुकिना सर्पराजोत्तरीयकः ॥ १५७ ॥
He for whom these serpent ornaments are present is celebrated as such. (The commentator notes that previous scholars interpreted 'Udarābandha' based on its literal length). He who has the serpent-king Vāsuki as his upper garment is Sarpottarīyaka (The One with a Serpent-Shawl).


रक्तो रक्ताम्बरधरो रक्तमालाविभूषणः ।
रक्तेक्षणो रक्तकरो रक्तताल्वोष्ठपल्लवः ॥ ४३॥


Shloka 158: Raktaḥ (रक्तः), Raktāmbaradharaḥ (रक्ताम्बरधरः), Raktamālāvibhūṣaṇaḥ (रक्तमालाविभूषणः), Rakteṣaṇaḥ (रक्तेक्षणः), Raktakaraḥ (रक्तकरः) & Raktatālvoṣṭhapallavaḥ (रक्तताल्वोष्ठपल्लवः)
रक्तः कान्त्या वस्त्रमाल्य भूषादृक्करतालुना।
ओष्ठा-ग्रस्य च शोणत्वान्नामभिः पंचभिः स्तुतः ॥ १५८ ॥
He is praised by five names indicating a red hue: He is Raktaḥ (The Red One) due to his splendor, Raktāmbaradharaḥ (The Red-Robed One) due to his garments, Raktamālāvibhūṣaṇaḥ (The One Adorned with a Red Garland) due to his garland, Rakteṣaṇaḥ (The Red-Eyed One) due to his eyes, Raktakaraḥ (The Red-Handed One) due to the palms of his hands, and Raktatālvoṣṭhapallavaḥ (The One with a Red Palate and Lips) due to his palate and the tips of his lips.


श्वेतः श्वेताम्बरधरः श्वेतमालाविभूषणः ।
श्वेतातपत्ररुचिरः श्वेतचामरवीजितः ॥ ४४॥


Shloka 159: Śvetaḥ (श्वेतः), Śvetāmbaradharaḥ (श्वेताम्बरधरः), Śvetamālāvibhūṣaṇaḥ (श्वेतमालाविभूषणः), Śvetātapatraruciraḥ (श्वेतातपत्ररुचिरः) & Śvetacāmaravījitaḥ (श्वेतचामरवीजितः)
विद्याकामादिभिर्येयं रूपं बोधयितुं पुनः ।
प्रवृत्तं श्वेत इत्यादि स्पष्टार्थ नामपंचकम् ॥ १५९ ॥
In order to indicate again that form (which bestows) knowledge, desires, etc., the five names beginning with Śveta are employed; their meaning is clear. They are: Śvetaḥ (The White One), Śvetāmbaradharaḥ (The White-Robed One), Śvetamālāvibhūṣaṇaḥ (The One Adorned with a White Garland), Śvetātapatraruciraḥ (The One Resplendent with a White Canopy), and Śvetacāmaravījitaḥ (The One Fanned with a White Chowrie).


सर्वावयवसम्पूर्णसर्वलक्षणलक्षितः ।
सर्वाभरणशोभाढ्यः सर्वशोभासमन्वितः ॥ ४५॥


Shloka 160: Sarvāvayavasampūrṇa-sarvalakṣaṇalakṣitaḥ (सर्वावयवसम्पूर्णसर्वलक्षणलक्षितः)
सर्वागेषु समुद्रोक्तलक्षणैः पूरणादयम् ।
सर्वावयवसंपूर्ण-सर्वलक्षणलक्षितः ॥ १६० ॥
Because all his limbs are filled with all the characteristics mentioned in the scriptures, he is Sarvāvayavasampūrṇa-sarvalakṣaṇalakṣitaḥ (The One with All Limbs Complete and Endowed with All Auspicious Marks).


Shloka 161: Sarvābharaṇaśobhākhya (सर्वाभरणशोभाख्य) & Sarvaśobhā-samanvita (सर्वशोभासमन्वित)
सर्वाभरणशोभाख्योऽ-लंकारालंकृतत्वतः ।
लावण्याख्याङ्गकान्त्याढ्यः सर्वशोभा-समन्वितः ॥ १६१ ॥
He is called Sarvābharaṇaśobhākhya (The One whose Splendor is his Every Ornament) because he is adorned by ornaments. He is Sarvaśobhā-samanvita (The One United with All Splendor) because he is full of the beauty known as loveliness (Lāvaṇya) of limb.


सर्वमङ्गलमाङ्गल्यः सर्वकारणकारणम् ।
सर्वदैककरः शार्ङ्गी बीजपूरी गदाधरः ॥ ४६॥


Shloka 162: Sarvamaṅgalamāṅgalyaḥ (सर्वमङ्गलमाङ्गल्यः)
सर्वमंगलमांगल्यः शुभानां च शुभावहः ।
सर्वमंगला-ख्याया देव्या मंगलावह इति प्राञ्चः, तत्पक्षे आबन्तस्य पुंव-द्भावः केनेति विचार्यम् ।
सर्वस्य जगतो व्यक्तं परमाण्वादि-कारणम् ॥ १६२ ॥
He is the auspiciousness of all auspicious things and the bringer of all that is propitious. He is Sarvamaṅgalamāṅgalyaḥ (The Auspiciousness of All Auspiciousness). (The commentary discusses an alternate interpretation regarding the goddess Sarvamaṅgalā, concluding it is a subject for inquiry.)


Shloka 163: Sarvakāraṇakāraṇa (सर्वकारणकारण) & Sarvadaika-kara (सर्वदैककर)
सर्वदैककरः सर्व धत्ते मुख्यः करोऽस्य यत् ।
तेषामपि च हेतुत्वात् सर्वकारणकार-णम् ॥ १६३ ॥
He is Sarvadaika-kara — the one whose supreme, all-performing hand (or power) upholds and governs everything. Since he is the cause even of those causes, he is also Sarvakāraṇakāraṇa, the Cause of All causes.


Shloka 164: Śārṅgī (शार्ङ्गी)
शृंगोद्भवं धनुः शार्ङ्ग तद्वान् शाङ्गर्थथ नामभिः ।
दशभिदेश-हस्तस्यायुधान्यत्र गणेशितुः ॥ १६४ ॥
He is the one whose principal hand holds everything, always. The bow named Śārṅga, born from a horn, and he who holds it, is Śārṅgī (The Holder of the Śārṅga Bow). Here, the ten weapons in the ten directional hands of the Lord of Ganas are described by ten names.


इक्षुचापधरः शूली चक्रपाणिः सरोजभृत् ।
पाशी धृतोत्पलः शालिमञ्जरीभृत्स्वदन्तभृत् ॥ ४७॥


Shloka 165: Bījapūrī (बीजपूरी), Gadādharaḥ (गदाधरः), Ikṣucāpadharaḥ (इक्षुचापधरः), Śūlī (शूली), Cakrapāṇiḥ (चक्रपाणिः), Sarojabhṛt (सरोजभृत्), Pāśī (पाशी), Dhṛtotpalaḥ (धृतोत्पलः), Śālīmañjarībhṛt (शालीमञ्जरीभृत्) & Svadantabhṛt (स्वदन्तभृत्)
मातुलिंगं गदेक्षूत्थं धनुः शूलं सुदर्शनं ।
(शंख) पद्मप त्पलब्रीहिकणिशानि रदार्धकम् ॥ १६५ ॥
The ten weapons are: 1) Bījapūrī (The Holder of the Citron Fruit), 2) Gadādharaḥ (The Mace-Bearer), 3) Ikṣucāpadharaḥ (The Holder of the Sugarcane Bow), 4) Śūlī (The Trident-Bearer), 5) Cakrapāṇiḥ (The Discus-Bearer), 6) Sarojabhṛt (The Lotus-Bearer), 7) Pāśī (The Noose-Bearer), 8) Dhṛtotpalaḥ (The Blue Lotus-Bearer), 9) Śālīmañjarībhṛt (The Rice-Spike Bearer), and 10) Svadantabhṛt (The Holder of His Own Tusk).






A Scholarly Refutation
यत्तु प्राचा बीजा-पूरपदं दाडिमपरत्वेन व्याख्याय, इक्षुचापधरादि नामपंचकं मदनरुद्रविष्णुब्रह्मपरत्वेन व्याख्याय तदुत्तरनामद्वयं वर्षासु जलस्थलशोभाकरत्वाद्वरुणपरमेव व्याख्यातं, तदशभुजाया गणेशमूर्तेः 'बीजापूरगदेक्षुकार्मुकरुचा चक्राब्जपाशोत्पलव्रीह्य-ग्रस्वविषाणरत्न कलशप्रोद्यत्करांभोरुहः' इति ध्यानश्लोकस्थ-पदानां परशुरामकल्पसूत्रोक्तसांप्रदायिकार्थस्य स्वप्नेऽप्यश्रवणा-देवेत्युपेक्ष्यं ॥
However, what has been explained by previous commentators—interpreting 'Bījāpūra' as a pomegranate, and the five names beginning with 'Ikṣucāpadhara' (Holder of the Sugar-cane Bow) as referring to Madana, Rudra, Vishnu, and Brahma, and the next two names as referring to Varuṇa because he beautifies land and water in the rainy season—is to be disregarded. This is because they have never even heard in their dreams the traditional meaning found in the Paraśurāma-Kalpa-Sūtra for the words in the meditation verse describing the fourteen-armed Ganesha form: "He who holds in his lotus-hands the Bījāpūra (citron), mace, sugar-cane bow, discus, lotus, noose, blue-lotus, rice, a small tusk, a jewel, and a water-vessel..."


कल्पवल्लीधरो विश्वाभयदैककरो वशी ।
अक्षमालाधरो ज्ञानमुद्रावान् मुद्गरायुधः ॥ ४८॥


Shloka 166: 
अथ मंत्रांतरंगानां ध्याननामायुधान्यपि ।
सांकर्येण विनि-देंष्टुं नामान्यत्र त्रयोदश ॥ १६६ ॥
Now, in order to indicate collectively the names of the meditative forms, weapons, and other aspects intrinsic to the mantra, thirteen names are given here.


Shloka 167: Kalpavallīdharaḥ (कल्पवल्लीधरः) & Viśvābhayadaikakaraḥ (विश्वाभयदैककरः)
कल्पवल्लीधरः कल्प-लतां धत्ते करांबुजे ।
विश्वस्मादभयं धत्ते यस्य मुख्यः करः स तु ॥ १६७ ॥
महागणपतिर्विश्वाभयदैककरः स्मृतः ।
He holds the wish-fulfilling creeper in his lotus-hand and is Kalpavallīdharaḥ (The Holder of the Wish-fulfilling Creeper). He whose principal hand grants freedom from fear to the world is Viśvābhayadaikakaraḥ (The One whose Single Hand Gives Safety to the Universe).


Shloka 168: Vaśī (वशी)
वशेऽस्य विश्वं सर्वस्य वशीति श्रुतितो वशी ॥ १६८ ॥
The whole world is under his control; as the Śruti declares, he is Vaśī (The Supreme Controller) of all.
Shloka 169: Akṣamālādhara (अक्षमालाधर)
अकारादिक्षकारांतमातृकात्मकमालिका ।
उक्ताक्षमाला तद्धारी त्वक्षमालाधरो मतः ॥ १६९ ॥
The garland (Mālikā) consisting of the letters of the alphabet, from 'A' to 'Kṣa', is called the Akṣamālā (Rosary of Letters). He who bears that is considered Akṣamālādhara (The Bearer of the Rosary).


Shloka 170: Jñānamudrāvān (ज्ञानमुद्रावान्)
तर्जन्यंगुष्ठसंयोगो ज्ञान-मुद्रा हृदि स्थिताः ।
तादृशैककरो ज्ञानमुद्रावानिति कथ्यते ॥ १७० ॥
The joining of the index finger and the thumb is the Jñānamudrā (Gesture of Knowledge), and it is situated (symbolically) in the heart. He who has a single hand in such a gesture is called Jñānamudrāvān (The One Displaying the Gesture of Knowledge).


Shloka 171: Mudgarāyudha (मुद्गरायुध)
मुदं गिरंति यन्मत्रसाधनान्युरुबुन्नकं ।
मुसलं वायुधं यस्य स देवो मुद्गरायुधः ॥ १७१ ॥
He whose weapon (Āyudha) is a large, heavy club (Mudgara) that crushes (Giranti) the joy (Muda) of mantra-practices (of evil forces), is the Lord Mudgarāyudha (The One with the Club as his Weapon).


पूर्णपात्री कम्बुधरो विधृताङ्कुशमूलकः ।
मातुलिंगधरश्रूतकलिकाभृत् कुठारवान् ॥४९॥


Shloka 172: Pūrṇapātrī (पूर्णपात्री)
पूर्णपात्रं याज्ञिकेषु प्रसिद्धं तद्युतो मखः ।
यद्वा सुधा-घटयुतः पूर्णपात्रीति कीर्त्यते ॥ १७२ ॥
The Pūrṇapātra (Full Vessel) is well-known in Vedic rituals. He whose sacrifice is endowed with that, or he who holds a pot (Pātrī) full of nectar (Sudhā), is celebrated as Pūrṇapātrī (The One with the Full Vessel).


Shloka 173: Kambudhara (कंबुधर)
शंखस्य धारणा-द्धस्ते देवः कंबुधरः स्मृतः।
मद्यसंपुटकं धत्ते भृंगाणां संपुटायुते ॥ १७३ ॥
Because he holds a conch in his hand, the Lord is remembered as Kambudhara (The Conch-Bearer). He holds a casket of intoxicating perfume, which is a container for bees.
Shloka 174: Vidhṛtālisamudgakaḥ (विधृतालिसमुद्गकः), Mātuliṅgadharaḥ (मातुलिंगधरः) & Cūtakalikābhṛt (चूतकलिकाभृत्)
यो मदस्तं कटे धत्ते विधृतालिसमुद्ङ्गकः ।
बीजा-पूरफलं चाम्रमंजरीं च करे दधत् ॥ १७४ ॥
मातुलिंगधर-श्रुतकलिकाभृच्च कथ्यते ।
He who holds a casket of bees at his hip is Vidhṛtālisamudgakaḥ (The Bearer of the Bee-Casket). He who holds a citron fruit and a mango blossom in his hand is Mātuliṅgadharaḥ (The Holder of the Citron) and is also described as Cūtakalikābhṛt (The Holder of the Mango Blossom).


Shloka 175: Kuṭhāravān (कुठारवान्)
योगिज्ञानं केवलाख्यं कुठारं तस्य साक्ष्यसौ ।
ज्ञानार्थकात्तु शत्रंताद्धातोः सिद्धः कुठारवान् ।
'कुठारं केवले रत' इति हैमकोशः ॥ १७५ ॥
The knowledge of the Yogis, called "Kevala" (Absolute), is his axe (Kuṭhāra), and he is its witness. Alternatively, etymologically, from the root 'Śatṛ' (to know), the word 'Kuṭhāra' is derived, meaning "possessing an axe" (Kuṭhāravān). The Haima Kośa lexicon states, "Kuṭhāra is one who is devoted to the Absolute."


पुष्करस्थस्वर्णघटीपूर्णरत्नाभिवर्षकः ।
भारतीसुन्दरीनाथो विनायकरतिप्रियः ॥५०॥


Shloka 176: Puṣkarastha-svarṇaghaṭīpūrṇaratnābhivarṣakaḥ (पुष्करस्थस्वर्णघटीपूर्णरत्नाभिवर्षकः)
गुंडाग्रस्थ -स्वर्णकुंभपूर्णरत्नप्रदानतः ॥ १७६ ॥
पुष्कर स्थस्वर्णघटीपूर्णरत्नाभिवर्षकः ।
Because he gives from a golden pot full of jewels situated at the tip of his trunk, he is the one who showers jewels from a golden pot situated in Puṣkara (or from a lotus). Thus, He is Puṣkarastha-svarṇaghaṭīpūrṇaratnābhivarṣakaḥ (The One who Showers Jewels from a Golden Pot Situated in Puṣkara).


Shloka 177: Bhāratīsundarīnāthaḥ (भारतीसुंदरीनाथः)
भारतीसुंदरी-नाथो ब्रह्मविष्णुशिवात्मकः ॥ १७७ ॥
He is the Lord of Bhāratī-Sundarī (the goddess of speech and beauty) and is of the nature of Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Śiva. Thus, He is Bhāratīsundarīnāthaḥ (The Lord of Bhāratī-Sundarī).


Shloka 178: Vināyakaratipriya (विनायकरतिप्रियः)
लक्ष्मीवाचकमीकारं प्रश्लिष्यार्थोऽयमूह्यतां ।
विनायका ग्रहाः केचित्तैः सह क्रीडनं प्रियं ॥ १७८ ॥
पक्षिराजेन वाऽस्येति विनायकरतिप्रियः ।
The meaning of the word 'Lakṣmī' should be understood by joining it with the word 'Vācaka' (indicating). He finds delight (Rati) in playing with certain Vināyakas (Gana-deities) and Grahas (planetary deities), or with the king of birds (Garuḍa). Therefore, he is Vināyakaratipriya (The One who Finds Delight in the Vināyakas).


महालक्ष्मीप्रियतमः सिद्धलक्ष्मीमनोरमः ।
रमारमेशपूर्वाङ्गो दक्षिणोमामहेश्वरः ॥५१॥


Shloka 179: 
महालक्ष्मीसिद्धलक्ष्म्यावस्य द्वे वल्लभे इति ॥ १७९ ॥
For him, the two—Mahālakṣmī (the great goddess of prosperity) and Siddhalakṣmī (the goddess of spiritual accomplishment)—are his beloved (Vallabhe). Thus, he is Mahālakṣmīvallabha (The Beloved of Mahālakṣmī) and Siddhalakṣmīvallabha (The Beloved of Siddhalakṣmī).
Shloka 180: Mahālakṣmīpriyatama (महालक्ष्मीप्रियतम) & Siddhalakṣmīmanorama (सिद्धलक्ष्मीमनोरम)
महालक्ष्मीप्रियतमः सिद्धलक्ष्मीमनोरमः ।
अथ नामचतुष्केन देवस्यावरणात्मिकाः ॥ १८० ॥
He is the most beloved (Priyatama) of Mahālakṣmī and the delightful one (Manorama) to Siddhalakṣmī. Now, with the next four names, (are described) the deities constituting his protective enclosure (Āvaraṇa).


Shloka 181: Rāmārameśa-pūrvāṅgaḥ (रमारमेशपूर्वाङ्गः), Dakṣiṇomāmaheśvaraḥ (दक्षिणोमामहेश्वरः), Mahīvarāha-vāmāṅgaḥ (महीवराहवामाङ्गः) & Ratikandarpa-paścimaḥ (रतिकन्दर्पपश्चिमः)
प्राच्याम-वाच्यां चोदीच्यां प्रतीच्यां च स्थिताः क्रमात् ।
रमादामोदरौ गौरीशंकरौ भूमिसूकरौ ॥ १८१ ॥
रतिमाराविति द्वंद्वदेवताः कीर्तिताः स्फुटाः ।
Situated in the East, South, North, and West respectively are the divine pairs. 
* He who is the eastern pair, Rāma (Lakṣmī) and Rameśa (Viṣṇu), is Rāmārameśa-pūrvāṅgaḥ (The One whose Eastern Form is Lakṣmī and Viṣṇu). 
* He who is the southern pair, Umā and Maheśvara (Pārvatī and Śiva), is Dakṣiṇomāmaheśvaraḥ (The One whose Southern Form is Pārvatī and Śiva). 
* He who is the northern pair, Mahī (Earth) and Varāha, is Mahīvarāha-vāmāṅgaḥ (The One whose Northern Form is the Earth and Varāha). 
* He who is the western pair, Rati and Kandarpa (Kāma), is Ratikandarpa-paścimaḥ (The One whose Western Form is Rati and Kāma).


महीवराहवामाङ्गो रतिकन्दर्पपश्चिमः ।
आमोदमोदजननः सम्प्रमोदप्रमोदनः ॥५२॥
समेधितसमृद्धश्रीऋद्धिसिद्धिप्रवर्तकः ।
दत्तसौमुख्यसुमुखः कांतिकंदलिताश्रयः ॥५३॥


Shloka 183: Āmodamodajananah (आमोदमोदजननः), Sampramodapramodanaḥ (सम्प्रमोदप्रमोदनः), Samedhitasamṛddhiśrīḥ (समेधितसमृद्धिश्रीः) & Ṛddhisiddhipravartakaḥ (ऋद्धिसिद्धिप्रवर्तकः)
छंदोवशादक्रमेण प्रोक्ता द्वादशनामभिः ।
ऋद्ध्यामोदौ समृद्धि-प्रमोदौ च मिथुनी उभे ॥१८३॥
Through metrical arrangement, twelve names are presented. Ṛddhi and Āmoda form the first divine couple; Samṛddhi and Pramoda form the second. He who generates the delight of Āmoda is Āmodamodajananah (The Generator of Delight). He who causes great joy (Pramoda) is Sampramodapramodanaḥ (The Causer of Great Joy). He whose glory is increased by prosperity is Samedhitasamṛddhiśrīḥ (The One whose Splendor is Enhanced by Prosperity). He who sets in motion (bestows) Ṛddhi and Siddhi is Ṛddhisiddhipravartakaḥ (The Initiator of Prosperity and Accomplishment).


Shloka 184: Dattasaumukhyasumukhaḥ (दत्तसौमुख्यसुमुखः), Kāntikaṇḍalitāśrayaḥ (कांतिकंदलिताश्रयः), Madanāvatyāśritāṅghriḥ (मदनावत्याश्रिताङ्घ्रिः), Kṛtavaimukhyadurmukhaḥ (कृतवैमुख्यदुर्मुखः), Vighnasampallavopaghnaḥ (विघ्नसम्पल्लवोपघ्नः), Sarvonnatamadadravaḥ (सर्वोन्नतमदद्रवः), Vighnakṛnnimnacaraṇaḥ (विघ्नकृन्निम्नचरणः) & Drāviṇīśaktisatkṛtaḥ (द्राविणीशक्तिसत्कृतः)
सुमुखः कांतिरित्येकं दुर्मुखो मदनावती मदद्रवेत्येकं ।
विघ्नो विघ्नकृद्राविणीति च ॥१८४॥
Sumukha and Kānti constitute one pair. Durmukha and Madanāvatī form another. Madadravā is paired with Vighna; Vighnakṛt is joined with Drāviṇī. He who, being gracious (Sumukha), has bestowed benevolence is Dattasaumukhyasumukhaḥ (The Benevolent One who has Bestowed Grace). He who is the refuge of unfolding splendor (Kānti) is Kāntikaṇḍalitāśrayaḥ (The Abode of Unfolding Splendor). He at whose feet Madanāvatī has taken refuge is Madanāvatyāśritāṅghriḥ (The One at whose Feet Madanāvatī has Taken Refuge). He who has made Durmukha (The Ill-Faced One) turn away is Kṛtavaimukhyadurmukhaḥ (The One who Caused Durmukha to Turn Away). He who destroys the very sprout of obstacles is Vighnasampallavopaghnaḥ (The Destroyer of the Sprout of Obstacles). He whose intoxicating power flows forth, elevating all, is Sarvonnatamadadravaḥ (The One from whom the Intoxicating Power that Elevates All Flows). He whose feet are bowed to by Vighnakṛt (The Obstacle-Creator) is Vighnakṛnnimnacaraṇaḥ (The One whose Feet are Bowed to by the Obstacle-Creator). He who is honored by the power of Drāviṇī (The Melting One) is Drāviṇīśaktisatkṛtaḥ (The One Honored by the Power of Drāviṇī).


Verse 185 — The Role of Āmoda, Pramoda, and Samṛddhi
आमोदं मोदयत्येष प्रमोदस्य प्रमोदनैः ।
सहितश्च समृद्ध्या श्रीरनेनैवाभिवर्धिता ॥ १८५ ॥
He (Ganesha) brings forth joy through Āmoda, and delights Pramoda with his own delight-giving acts.
United with Samṛddhi, the goddess Śrī (Fortune) is enhanced and expanded by him alone.


Verse 186 — Siddhi’s Activity and the Grace of Sumukha
ऋद्धिदेव्यां स्थिता सिद्धिः प्रवर्तयति चाप्यसौ ।
सुमुखः स्वेषु सौमुख्यं यत्प्रसादात् प्रतिच्छति ॥ १८६॥
Siddhi, who dwells within the goddess Ṛddhi, is set into action by him. And Sumukha (the Auspicious-Faced One) receives his beauty and charm through the grace of Ganesha.


मदनावत्याश्रिताङ्घ्रिः कृतवैमुख्यदुर्मुखः ।
विघ्नसम्पल्लवः पद्मः सर्वोन्नतमदद्रवः ॥५४॥


Verse 187 — Kānti, Madanāvatī, and Durmukha’s Nature
यद्वशादाश्रयः कांतेर्देव्या अंकुरितोऽभवत् ।
धृतवान् मदनावत्या दौर्मुख्यं दुर्मुखे स्थितं ॥ १८७॥
By his power, the supporting essence of the goddess Kānti (Splendor) emerged into manifestation.
And through Madanāvatī, Durmukha sustains the harsh and fierce quality
that abides naturally within him.


Verse 188 — The Cutting of Obstacles and Protective Power
कृत्तं छिन्नमनेनैव सम्यग्विघ्नस्य पल्लवः ।
विस्तारस्तस्य चोपघ्न आश्रयो यस्य सेवने ॥१८८॥
By him alone, the tender shoots of obstacles are properly cut down and severed. For the one who takes refuge in his worship, the growth of opposing forces diminishes.


विघ्नकृन्निम्नचरणो द्राविणीशक्तिसत्कृतः ।
तीव्राप्रसन्ननयनो ज्वालिनीपालितैकदृक् ॥५५॥
मोहिनीमोहनो भोगदायिनीकान्तिमण्डनः ।
कामिनीकान्तवक्त्रश्रीरधिष्ठितवसुन्धरः ॥५६॥


Verse 189 — Madadravā and the Devotional Submission of Vighnakṛt
उन्निद्रानलसैवास्ते देवी नान्ना मदद्रवा ।
भक्त्या विघ्नकृतोऽधीने यस्य तञ्चरणांबुजे ॥ १८९॥
The goddess Madadravā remains ever-alert and never sluggish. And Vighnakṛt (the Remover of Obstacles) becomes submissive through devotion to the one devoted to his lotus-feet.




A Final Scholarly Refutation
यत्त्वत्र-प्राचां षोडशसु नामस्वर्थांतरवर्णनं पाठांतरावलंबनं, उपन्नपदे विसर्गापलापनेनोद्राविणीति पदकल्पनादिना च, कचिन्नामैक्य-करणं च, तत्सर्वमसांप्रदायिकत्वात् परशुरामकृत कल्पसूत्रा-दिविरोधाच्च मनसापि न स्मर्तव्यम् ।
However, whatever has been done by previous commentators here regarding these sixteen names—such as describing different meanings, relying on alternate readings, inventing the word 'Udrāviṇī' by dropping the Visarga in 'Upanṇa', and sometimes combining names into one—all of that, being non-traditional and contradicting texts like the Paraśurāma-Kalpa-Sūtra, should not be remembered even in the mind.


Shloka 190: Ṣaḍdiś (षड्दिश्)
अथ नामानि षट् प्राह षड्डिस्तीत्रादिशक्तिभिः ॥१९०॥
Now, he proclaims six names, (related to) the six directions, with the powers like Tīvrā, etc.


Shloka 191: Tīvrāprasannanayanaḥ (तीव्राप्रसन्ननयनः) & Jvālinīpālitāikadṛk (ज्वालिनीपालितैकदृक्)
तीव्रायां यस्य नयने प्रसाद-पिशुनेक्षणे ।
ज्वालिन्या रक्षणे मुख्या यदृष्टिर्मोहिनीमपि ॥ १९१ ॥
He on whom the gaze of Tīvrā, whose eyes are keen with grace, falls is Tīvrāprasannanayanaḥ (The One with the Graciously Keen Gaze of Tīvrā). He whose single gaze is protected by the principal gaze of Jvālinī is Jvālinīpālitāikadṛk (The One whose Gaze is Protected by Jvālinī).


Shloka 192: Mohinīmohanaḥ (मोहिनीमोहनः) & Bhogadāyinīkāntimaṇḍanaḥ (भोगदायिनीकान्तिमण्डनः)
यो मोहयति तस्मै श्रीगणाधिपतये नमः ।
त्वं भोगदायिनी शक्तेः कान्त्या मंडितपादुकः ॥ १९२ ॥
Salutations to that glorious Lord of Ganas who bewilders even Mohinī (the enchantress); He is Mohinīmohanaḥ (The Bewilderer of even Mohinī). "You are Bhogadāyinī (The Bestower of Enjoyment)," (saying this) the power of his splendor adorns his footwear; thus He is Bhogadāyinīkāntimaṇḍanaḥ (The One Adorned by the Splendor of Bhogadāyinī).


Conclusion of the Third Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ ॥
गणेशनामखद्योते तृतीयमभवत् शतम् ॥ ३००॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), composed by Bhāskara, 
also known as Agnicit, ends the third hundred of the Ganesha Sahasranama.


वसुधारामदोन्नादो महाशङ्खनिधिप्रियः ।
नमद्वसुमतीमाली महापद्मनिधिः प्रभुः ॥५७॥


Shloka 193: Kāminīkāntavaktraśriyaḥ (कामिनीकान्तवक्त्रश्रियः), Adhiṣṭhitavāsundharā (अधिष्ठितवासुन्धरा), Vasundharāmadonnamahāśaṅkhanidhiprabhave (वसुन्धरामदोन्नमहाशङ्खनिधिप्रभवे) & Namadvasumatīmaulimahāpadmanidhiprabhuḥ (नमद्वसुमतीमौलिमहापद्मनिधिप्रभुः)
कामिन्याः शोभने वक्रे या शोभातत्स्वरूपकः ।
वसुंधरा-भिधा देवी जाता त्वत्तः प्रतिष्ठिता ॥ १९३ ॥
अथ द्वे नामनी प्राह मिथुनद्वयबोधिके ।
महाशंखमहा-पद्माभिधे द्वे निधिदेवते ॥ १९४ ॥
वसुंधरा वसुमतीत्येते द्वे तत्तदंगने ।
He is the very form of the beauty which is in the lovely face of Kāminī; thus He is Kāminīkāntavaktraśriyaḥ (The One who is the Splendor of Kāminī's Lovely Face). The goddess named Vasundharā (The Earth) is born from you and is established (by you); thus She is Adhiṣṭhitavāsundharā (The Established Earth). He from whom the great treasure (Nidhi) named Mahāśaṅkha, belonging to the earth (Vasundharā), arises is Vasundharāmadonnamahāśaṅkhanidhiprabhave (The Source of the Great Śaṅkha Treasure of the Earth). He who is the lord (Prabhu) of the great treasure (Nidhi) named Mahāpadma, to which the bowing earth (Vasumatī) is the crest-jewel, is Namadvasumatīmaulimahāpadmanidhiprabhuḥ (The Lord of the Great Padma Treasure, the Crest-Jewel of the Bowing Earth).


सर्वसद्गुरुसंसेव्यः शोचिष्केशहृदाश्रयः ।
ईशानमूर्धा देवेन्द्रशिखः पवननन्दनः ॥५८॥


Shloka 195: Sarvasadgurusaṃsevya (सर्वसद्गुरुसंसेव्य)
गणेशादिस्वनाथान्तैः सम्यगाराधितो यतः ॥ १९५ ॥
सर्वसद्गुरुसंसेव्य इति तेन निगद्यसे ।
"Vasundharā" and "Vasumatī" are the two (consorts) in their respective courts. Because he is properly worshipped by (all teachers) from Gaṇeśa to one's own Guru (Svanātha), he is called Sarvasadgurusaṃsevya (The One Served by All True Gurus).

Shloka 196: Śociṣkeśa-hṛdāśraya (शोचिष्केशहृदाश्रय)
गार्हपत्यादयः पंच शोचिष्केशास्तदीयहृत् ।
आश्रयो यस्य तद्ध्येयः शोचिष्केशहृदाश्रयः ॥ १९६॥
The five fires beginning with the Gārhapatya are the "Śociṣkeśa" (Fire-haired ones). He who is the abode of their hearts (i.e., their inner essence) is to be meditated upon as Śociṣkeśa-hṛdāśraya (The Abode in the Heart of the Fires).


Shloka 197: Īśānamūrdhā (ईशानमूर्धा) & Devendraśikhaḥ (देवेन्द्रशिखः)
ईशानस्यापि मूर्धा त्वं देवेन्द्र-स्यापि वै शिखा ॥ १९७ ॥
शक्रशंकरयोर्मान्य इति नाम-द्वयाशयः ।
"You are the head of Īśāna (Śiva) and the crest-jewel of Devendra (Indra)." This is the intent of the two names. Thus, He is Īśānamūrdhā (The Head of Īśāna) and Devendraśikhaḥ (The Crest-Jewel of Indra).


Shloka 198: Pavananandanaḥ (पवननन्दनः)
जीवनान्नान्य आनंदस्तत्प्राणपवनात्मकः ॥१९८॥
प्राणस्यापि च यः प्राणः स स्यात्पवननंदनः ।
The bliss of life is none other than the vital air (Prāṇa). He whose nature is that vital air and breath, and he who is the very life of the life-force, is Pavananandanaḥ (The Son of the Wind).


अग्रप्रत्यग्रनयनो दिव्यास्त्राणां प्रयोगवित् । 
ऐरावतादिसर्वाशावारणावरणप्रियः ॥५९॥


Shloka 199: Agrapratyagranayana (अग्रप्रत्यग्रनयन)
अग्रं सूक्ष्मं चोत्तमं वा प्रत्यग्रं जीर्णतोज्झितम् ॥ १९९ ॥
तादृग्लोचनवानग्रप्रत्यग्रनयनो मतः ।
"Agra" means subtle or supreme; "Pratyagra" means fresh, cast off from decay. He who possesses such a vision is considered Agrapratyagranayana (The One with Fresh and Supreme Vision).


Shloka 200: Divyāstrāṇāṃ Prayogavit (दिव्यास्त्राणां प्रयोगवित्)
Sanskrit:
अस्त्रविद्याप्रवर्तृत्वा-द्दिव्यास्त्राणां प्रयोगवित् ॥ २०० ॥
Because he is the propounder of the science of weapons, 
he is Divyāstrāṇāṃ Prayogavit (The Knower of the Use of Divine Weapons).


Shloka 201: Airāvatādi-sarvāśā-vāraṇāvaraṇa-priya (ऐरावतादिसर्वाशावारणावरणप्रिय)
क्रीडयैवावृणोत्येष युगपद्दिग्गजानपि ।
ऐरावतादिसर्वाशावारणावरण-प्रियः ॥ २०१ ॥
Merely through play, he encompasses even the Diggajas (the elephants of the directions) simultaneously. He who takes delight in the enclosure formed by all the directional elephants like Airāvata is Airāvatādi-sarvāśā-vāraṇāvaraṇa-priya (The One who Delights in the Enclosure of All Directional Elephants like Airāvata).


वज्राद्यस्त्रपरीवारो गणचण्डसमाश्रयः ।
जयाजयापरिवार: विजयाविजयावहः ॥ ६०॥


Shloka 202: Vajrādyastra-parivāra (वज्राद्यस्त्रपरीवार)
वज्रादीनि यदस्त्राणि तैरिंद्रादिभिरावृतः ।
वज्राद्यस्त्र-परीवार आयुधावरणोऽपि वा ॥ २०२ ॥
He is surrounded by Indra and other deities who hold weapons (astras) starting with the Vajra. He whose entourage consists of weapons like the Vajra is Vajrādyastra-parivāra (The One whose Entourage Consists of Weapons like the Vajra).


Shloka 203: Gaṇacaṇḍa-samāśraya (गणचण्डसमाश्रय)
गणाश्चंडादयो यद्वा ये प्रचंडा गणेष्वपि ।
तेषां बलप्रदातृत्वाद्गणचंडसमाश्रयः ॥ २०३॥
Alternatively, the groups (Gaṇas) like Caṇḍa and others, or those who are the most fierce (Pracaṇḍa) even among the groups—because He is the bestower of strength to them, He is Gaṇacaṇḍa-samāśraya (The One who is the Refuge of Fierce Attendants like Caṇḍa).


अजयार्चितपादाब्जो नित्यानन्दवनस्थितः ।
विलासिनीकृतोल्लासः शौण्डीसौन्दर्यमण्डितः ॥ ६१॥
अनन्तानन्तसुखदः सुमङ्गलसुमङ्गलः ।
इच्छाशक्तिज्ञानशक्तिक्रियाशक्तिनिषेवितः ॥ ६२॥


Shloka 204-206: Jayājayāparivāraḥ (जयाजयापरिवारः), Vijayāvijayāvahaḥ (विजयाविजयावहः), Ajayārcitapādābjaḥ (अजयार्चितपादाब्जः), Nityānandavanasthitaḥ (नित्यानन्दवनस्थितः), Vilāsinīkṛtollāsaḥ (विलासिनीकृतोल्लासः) & Śauṇḍīsaundaryamaṇḍitaḥ (शौण्डीसौन्दर्यमण्डितः)
अथाष्टनामभिः प्राह प्राणशक्त्या अभिन्नतां ।
जया च विजया पश्चादजया चापराजिता ॥ २०४॥
नित्या विलासिनी शौंडी ततोऽनंताथ मंगला ।
प्राणशक्त्यादिदेवीनामेताः स्युः पीठशक्तयः ॥२०५॥
जया चाप्यजयेत्येतत्समाहारो जयाजयं ।
ते एवास्य परीवारो द्वितीयं नाम तु स्फुटं ॥ २०६ ॥
तृतीयनामन्यजितापदेनोक्ता पराजिता ।
Now, with eight names, He is declared to be non-different from His Prāṇa-Śakti. The combination of "Jayā" and "Ajayā" is "Jayājayam". They themselves are His attendants. Thus, He whose entourage consists of Jayā and Ajayā is Jayājayāparivāraḥ (The One Attended by Jayā and Ajayā). He who brings about both victory (Vijayā) and the absence of victory (Avijayā) is Vijayāvijayāvahaḥ (The Bringer of Victory and its Opposite). He whose lotus feet are worshipped by Ajayā is Ajayārcitapādābjaḥ (The One whose Lotus Feet are Worshipped by Ajayā). He who abides in the forest of eternal bliss is Nityānandavanasthitaḥ (The One Situated in the Forest of Eternal Bliss). He for whom Vilāsinī creates exuberance is Vilāsinīkṛtollāsaḥ (The One whose Exuberance is Caused by Vilāsinī). He who is adorned by the beauty of Śauṇḍī is Śauṇḍīsaundaryamaṇḍitaḥ (The One Adorned by Śauṇḍī's Beauty).




Shloka 207: Anantānantasukhadaḥ (अनन्तानन्तसुखदः)
सप्तमे नाम्यनंताया अनंतसुखदायकः ॥ २०७ ॥
In the seventh name, pertaining to Anantā (The Infinite One), He is the giver of infinite happiness. Thus, He is Anantānantasukhadaḥ (The Giver of Infinite Happiness to the Infinite One / The Infinite Giver of Happiness).


Shloka 208: Sumaṅgala-sumaṅgalaḥ (सुमङ्गलसुमङ्गलः)
शोभना मंगला यस्मिंस्तत्तु पीठं सुमंगलम् ।
तादृकू सुमंगलं यस्मात् सुमंगलसुमंगलः ॥ २०८ ॥
Because the auspicious Maṅgalā resides in Him, that seat (Pīṭha) is supremely auspicious (Sumaṅgala). Because He possesses such supreme auspiciousness, He is Sumaṅgala-sumaṅgalaḥ (The Supremely Auspicious One who is the very essence of Auspiciousness).


Commentary on the Names (209-210)
प्राचा त्वेतानि नामा-न्यन्यथा व्याख्यातानि ।
प्रथमनामनि जय इत्यस्य भिन्न-पदत्वं च स्वीकृतं, तदसांप्रदायिकत्वादुपेक्ष्यं ।
या ब्रह्मण्युगता वृत्तिः स्वर्गादावीक्षणात्मिका ।
ज्ञानेच्छाकृतिरूपा सेत्य-स्माभिः श्रुतियुक्तिभिः ॥ २०९ ॥
वामकेश्वरतंत्रस्य व्याख्यायां प्रतिपादितं ।
तादृवृत्तियुतं ब्रह्मनाम्नैकेनेह कथ्यते ॥ २१०॥
However, previous (commentators) have explained these names differently. In the first name, they have accepted "Jayā" and "Ajayā" as separate words. That is non-traditional and should be disregarded. As established by us through Vedic statements and reasoning, and as propounded in the commentary on the Vāmakeśvara-tantra, the disposition (Vṛtti) which is inherent in Brahman, which is of the nature of witnessing (enjoyment) in states like heaven, and which has the form of Knowledge, Will, and Action—He, who is endowed with such a disposition, is spoken of here by the single name "Brahma".


Shloka 211: Icchā-śakti-jñāna-śakti-kriyā-śakti-niṣevita (इच्छाशक्तिज्ञानशक्तिक्रियाशक्तिनिषेवित)
इच्छाशक्तिज्ञानशक्तिक्रियाशक्तिनिषेवितः ।
सैव वृत्तिर्भिद्यमाना तत्तद्धर्मैकयोगतः ॥ २११ ॥
He is constantly attended by the powers of Will (Icchā), Knowledge (Jñāna), and Action (Kriyā). That very (supreme) disposition (Vṛtti), when differentiated and connected with specific properties, becomes these (three) powers. He is Icchā-śakti-jñāna-śakti-kriyā-śakti-niṣevita (The One Served by the Powers of Will, Knowledge, and Action).


सुभगासंश्रितपदो ललिताललिताश्रयः ।
कामिनीपालनः कामकामिनीकेलिलालितः ॥ ६३॥


Shloka 212: Subhagā-saṃśrita-pada (सुभगासंश्रितपद)
सुभगा-दिकरौ ह्येते नामभागित्यपीरितं ।
तत्र या सुभगादेवी तया सेव्यांघ्रिरप्यसौ ॥ २१२॥
It is stated that these (names) are also parts of names like "Subhagādi-kara". He, whose feet are served by that very goddess Subhagā, is Subhagā-saṃśrita-pada (The One at whose Feet the Auspicious Goddess Subhagā Resides).


Shloka 213: Lalitā-lalitā-śrayaḥ, Kāminī-pālanaḥ, Kāma-kāminī-keli-lālitaḥ (ललिताललिताश्रयः, कामिनीपालनः, कामकामिनीकेलिलालितः)
गणेशसुंदरीमंत्राललिताललिता-श्रयः ।
कामिनी या कामकला कामेशी काममालिनी ॥ २१३ ॥
He is the abode of the goddesses Lalitā and Alalitā (Lalitā-lalitā-śrayaḥ). He is the protector of the goddesses Kāminī, Kāmakalā, Kāmeśī, and Kāmamālinī (Kāminī-pālanaḥ). He who is adorned by the playful sports with Kāma (desire) and the Kāminīs (desirous goddesses) is Kāma-kāminī-keli-lālitaḥ.


सरस्वत्याश्रयो गौरीनन्दनः श्रीनिकेतनः ।
गुरुगुप्तपदो वाचासिद्धो वागीश्वरीपतिः ॥ ६४ ॥


Shloka 214: Sarasvaty-āśrayaḥ, Gaurī-nandanaḥ, Śrī-niketanaḥ (सरस्वत्याश्रय:, गौरीनन्दनः, श्रीनिकेतनः)
सरस्वत्याश्रयादीनि त्रीणि स्पष्टार्थकान्यथ ।
गणक्रीडादिगुरुभिर्ग्रथादौ प्रतिपादितैः ॥ २१४ ॥
The next three names, starting with "Sarasvaty-āśraya", have clear meanings, as propounded by revered teachers like Gaṇakrīḍa. They are: Sarasvaty-āśrayaḥ (The Abode of Sarasvatī), Gaurī-nandanaḥ (The Son of Gaurī), and Śrī-niketanaḥ (The Abode of Śrī).


Shloka 215: Gurugupta-padaḥ, Vācā-siddha (गुरुगुप्तपद:, वाचासिद्ध)
गोपित-त्वात्पदस्यास्य गुरुगुप्तपदो मतः ।
वाचा सिद्ध्यति यद्भक्त्या स वाचासिद्ध इष्यते ॥ २१५ ॥
Because this word ("Vācāsiddha") is concealed (in meaning), He is known as Gurugupta-padaḥ (The One whose true nature is hidden even from the Guru). He who is attained (Siddha) through devotion accompanied by (recitation of) Vāk (speech/mantra) is Vācā-siddha.


नलिनीकामुको वामारामो ज्येष्ठामनोरमः ।
रौद्रीमुद्रितपादाब्जो हुम्बीजस्तुङ्गशक्तिकः ॥ ६५ ॥


Shloka 216: Vāgīśvarī-patiḥ, Nalinī-kāmukaḥ (वागीश्वरीपतिः, नलिनीकामुक:)
वागीश्वरी तु नकुली नलिनी तु सुरापगा ।
तयोः पतिः कामुकोऽथ वामा ज्येष्ठा च रौद्रिका ॥ २१६ ॥
Vāgīśvarī is (also called) Nakulī, and Nalinī is (another name for) Surāpagā (the celestial river, Gaṅgā). He is the master (Pati) of these two and the desirous lover (Kāmuka) of them. Thus, He is Vāgīśvarī-patiḥ (The Lord of Vāgīśvarī) and Nalinī-kāmukaḥ (The Lover of Nalinī/Gaṅgā).


Shloka 217: Vāmā-rāmaḥ, Jyeṣṭhā-manoramaḥ (वामारामः, ज्येष्ठामनोरमः)
ब्रह्मविष्णुहरा एव शक्तिरूपधरा यदि ।
वामैव यस्य रामा स ज्येष्ठा यस्य मनोरमा ॥ २१७॥
If Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Hara (Śiva) themselves assume the form of Śaktis, then He whose (Śakti) is Vāmā (who is also) Rāmā, is Vāmā-rāmaḥ (The One whose Vāmā is delightful). And He whose (Śakti) is Jyeṣṭhā (who is also) Manoramā, is Jyeṣṭhā-manoramaḥ (The One whose Jyeṣṭhā is enchanting).

Shloka 218: Raudrī-mudrita-pādābja (रौद्रीमुद्रितपादाब्ज)
रौद्रीमुद्रितपादाब्जस्तदंजलिधृतांत्रिकः ।
हुंकार एव चरमो वक्रतुंडषडक्षरे ॥ २१८ ॥
He whose lotus feet are sealed (worshipped) by Raudrī, and who holds the three (worlds) in his cupped hands. The final (syllable) is indeed the Huṃkāra in the Ṣaḍakṣara (six-syllabled) mantra of Vakratuṇḍa (Gaṇeśa). He is Raudrī-mudrita-pādābja (The One whose Lotus Feet are Sealed by Raudrī).


Shloka 219: Huṃ-bīja (हुंबीज)
बीजं सर्वपुमर्थानां हुंबीजस्तेन गीयसे ।
तत्रत्य एव तुंकारोऽष्टाविंशत्यर्णगं च कं ॥ २१९ ॥
The seed-syllable (Bīja) of all supreme goals is the Huṃ-bīja; thus, you are praised as Huṃ-bīja. The "Tuṃ" sound therein, and the "Ka" which resides in the twenty-eight letters (of the alphabet)... He is Huṃ-bīja (The Seed-Syllable 'Huṃ').
Shloka 220: Tuṅga-śaktika (तुङ्गशक्तिक)
शक्तीस्ताः सकलौन्नत्यशक्त्या वान् तुंगशक्तिकः ।
हिरण्यगर्भो विश्वादिस्तदुत्पादनपालने ॥ २२० ॥
He possesses (Vān) the power of complete elevation (Sakalaunnati-śakti) for those Śaktis; thus, He is Tuṅga-śaktika (The One with Lofty Powers). (He is) Hiraṇyagarbha (the cosmic womb), Viśva, etc., in the acts of their creation and preservation.


विश्वादिजननत्राणः स्वाहाशक्तिः सकीलकः ।
अमृताब्धिकृतावासो मदघूर्णितलोचनः ॥ ६६॥


Shloka 221: Viśvādi-janana-trāṇa (विश्वादिजननत्राण)
यस्मात् स्यातां स विश्वादिजननत्राण उच्यते ।
जननत्राणवच्छक्तो होमे कंबलने प्रियः ॥२२१॥
Because from Him, the creation and protection (Janana-trāṇa) of the universe, etc., occur, He is called Viśvādi-janana-trāṇa (The Source of Creation and Protection for the Universe, etc.). He is powerful, as implied by "Janana-trāṇa," and is fond of offerings in fire (Homa) and of blankets (Kambala).


Shloka 222: Svāhā-śakti (स्वाहाशक्ति)
स्वाहेति मंत्रशक्तिर्वा स स्वाहा-शक्तिरीरितः ।
मंत्रे मृत्युंजये सौरे स इत्यर्णः सकीलकः ॥ २२२ ॥
Or, the power of the mantra is "Svāhā"; thus, He is declared as Svāhā-śakti (The Power of Svāhā). In the Mṛtyuñjaya and Saura mantras, the letter "Sa" with its Kilaka (a grammatical marker) denotes Him.


Shloka 223: Sakīlaka (सकीलक), Amṛtābdhi-kṛtāvāsa (अमृताब्धिकृतावास) & Madaghūrṇita-locana (मदघूर्णितलोचन)
वाचको यस्य मंत्रांगसाहित्याद्वा सकीलकः ।
सुरासुधेक्षुसिंधुस्थो यस्तत्पानरतश्च सः ॥ २२३ ॥
अमृता-ब्धिकृतावासो मदघूर्णितलोचनः ।
He is called Sakīlaka either because His mantra includes the letter "Sa" with its Kilaka, or because He is situated in the nectar, sugarcane, and ocean (i.e., the essence of delights) and is devoted to drinking that (essence). He who has His abode in the ocean of nectar is Amṛtābdhi-kṛtāvāsa (The One who has Made the Ocean of Nectar His Abode). He whose eyes are rolling due to (divine) intoxication is Madaghūrṇita-locana (The One with Eyes Rolling in Divine Intoxication).


उच्छिष्टगण उच्छिष्टगणेशो गणनायकः । 
सार्वकालिकसंसिद्धिर्नित्यशैवो दिगंबरः ॥६७॥


Shloka 224: Ucchiṣṭa-gaṇa (उच्छिष्टगण)
उच्छिष्टगण उत्कृष्टा गणाः शिष्टाश्च यस्य सः ॥ २२४ ॥
He who has the Ucchiṣṭa Gaṇa (a group of beings associated with remnants) as well as the excellent and the righteous Gaṇas (hosts) as His own, is Ucchiṣṭa-gaṇa (The One with the Ucchiṣṭa Gaṇa as His Host).


Shloka 225: Ucchiṣṭa-gaṇeśa (उच्छिष्टगणेश)
उच्छिष्टं नामरूपं चेत्याद्युचः सप्तविंशतिः ।
आथर्वणो यं वदंति स गणेशो न चापरः ॥ २२५ ॥
मोदकं भक्षयन्देवो ध्येयो मंत्रो नवाक्षरैः ।
साधकेनेति वोच्छिष्टगणेश इति कथ्यसे ॥ २२६ ॥
The utterances beginning with "Ucchiṣṭa, Nāma, Rūpa, etc." are twenty-seven in number. The followers of the Atharva Veda proclaim Him (as the lord of these); He is that Gaṇeśa and no other. He is Ucchiṣṭa-gaṇeśa (The Lord of the Ucchiṣṭa Gaṇas).
The deity who is eating the Modaka sweet, who is to be meditated upon with a nine-syllabled mantra by the sādhaka—thus, you are called Ucchiṣṭa-gaṇeśa (The Lord of the Ucchiṣṭa Gaṇas).


Shloka 227: Gaṇanāyaka (गणनायक) and Sārvakālikasaṃsiddhi (सार्वकालिकसंसिद्धि)
गणनं गुणसंख्यानमयते गणनायकः ।
सार्वकालिकसंसिद्धिः शाश्वत्यो यस्य सिद्धयः ॥ २२७ ॥
He who seeks out (Ayate) the counting (Gaṇana) and enumeration of qualities is Gaṇanāyaka (The Lord of Reckoning). His perfections (Siddhis) are eternal and ever-accomplished.


Shloka 228: Nityaśaiva (नित्यशैव) and Digambara (दिगम्बर)
नित्यशैवः कदाप्यस्य शिवभावनया यतः ।
दिशमंबरयत्या-वृणोति स दिगंबरः कथितः ॥ २२८ ॥
He is eternally one with Śiva (Nityaśaiva); because His nature is always that of Śiva, and because He covers (Āvṛṇoti) the directions (Diś) as His garment (Ambara), He is declared as Digambara (The Sky-Clad One).




अनापायोऽनन्तदृष्टिरप्रमेयोऽजरामरः ।
अनाविलोऽप्रतिहतिरच्युतोऽमृतमक्षरः ॥ ६८॥
अप्रतर्क्योऽक्षयोऽजय्योऽनाधारोऽनामयोऽमलः ।
अमेयसिद्धिरद्वैतमघोरोऽग्निसमाननः ॥ ६९॥ 


Shloka 229: Anapāya (अनपाय) & Anantadṛṣṭi (अनन्तदृष्टि)
अनपगतशुभावहविधिरनपायो नाशविरहाद्वा ।
अगणित-चक्षुर्नाशविरहित ज्ञानोऽप्यनंतदृष्टिरिति ॥ २२९॥
His rule of bestowing auspiciousness never departs; He is without decline, hence He is Anapāya (The One Without Decline). His eyes are countless, and His knowledge is without destruction; thus, He is Anantadṛṣṭi (The One of Infinite Vision).


Shloka 230: Aprameya (अप्रमेय) & Ajara-amara (अजरामर)
ज्ञानेंद्रियवामनसावगम्यत्वादप्रमेय इति ।
विजेरो विमृत्युरित्याम्नानादजरामरो गदितः ॥ २३० ॥
Because He is beyond the grasp of the senses and the mind, He is Aprameya (The Immeasurable). Based on the scriptural declaration "Vijero Vimṛtyuḥ" (free from old age and death), He is proclaimed as Ajara-amara (The One Free from Old Age and Death).


Shloka 231: Anāvila (अनाविल) & Apratiratha (अप्रतिरथ)
अविघ्नकधर्मततिभिरकलुषि-तत्वादनाविलोऽपि भवान् ।
अप्रतिरथ इति गदितो नास्ति यतस्ते प्रतिद्वंद्वी ॥ २३१ ॥
O Lord, You are unstained by the multitude of obstructive qualities, and thus unpolluted; You are Anāvila (The Unpolluted One). You are called Apratiratha (The Unopposed Warrior) because You have no rival or opponent.


Shloka 233: Akṣara (अक्षर) & Apratarvya (अप्रतर्व्य)
अक्षरमश्नोतेर्व्याप्यर्थादथवा क्षयाभावात् ।
वेदाननुमततर्कागम्यत्वादप्रतर्व्यस्त्वम् ॥ २३३ ॥
You are Akṣara (The Imperishable) because You pervade all, or because You are without decay. You are Apratarvya (The Unassailable) because You are unattainable by logic that is not sanctioned by the Vedas.


Shloka 234: Akṣaya (अक्षय), Ajayya (अजय्य) & Anādhāra (अनाधार)
न क्षतिरपि न च वसतिर्जेतुमशक्योऽक्षयोऽजय्यः ।
जगदाधारस्य तवा-धारो नास्तीत्यनाधारः ॥ २३४ ॥
You are without loss and without a fixed abode; unable to be conquered, You are Akṣaya (The Indestructible) and Ajayya (The Unconquerable). For You, the support of the universe, there is no support; thus, You are Anādhāra (The Supportless One).


Shloka 235: Anāmaya (अनामय), Amala (अमल), Amoghasiddhi (अमोघसिद्धि) & Advaitam (अद्वैतम्)
आमयमालिन्याभ्यामगृहीतत्वादनामयोऽस्यमलः ।
सिद्धिर्भवत्यर्थवती नामोघा भक्तस्य तेन त्वममोघसिद्धिः ।
द्वैतप्रपञ्श्चोपशमाच्चतुर्थमद्वैतमित्युच्यस ईशरूपः ॥ २३५ ॥
You are untouched by disease and impurity, hence You are Anāmaya (The Diseaseless) and Amala (The Stainless). The fulfillment of Your devotee is meaningful and unfailing; therefore, You are Amoghasiddhi (The One of Unfailing Fulfillment). Duality is a mere expansion, and with its cessation, the fourth state is called Non-duality; You are that Advaitam (The Non-dual) form of the Lord.


Shloka 236: Aghora & Apramitānana (अघोर & अप्रमितानन)
अघोरमूर्तिः शिव इत्यघोरो न मज्जनानामभयंकरो वा ।
असंख्यवक्रोऽस्यमितानि यस्मात् संजीवनान्यप्रमिताननस्त्वम् ॥ २३६ ॥
Your form is non-terrifying (Aghora) and auspicious (Śiva), hence You are Aghora. You are not fearsome to those who take refuge in You. Because Your faces are innumerable and Your life-giving powers are immeasurable, You are Apramitānana (The One with Immeasurable Faces).


अनाकारोऽब्धिभूम्यग्निबलघ्नोऽव्यक्तलक्षणः ।
आधारपीठमाधार आधाराधेयवर्जितः ॥ ७०॥


Shloka 237: Anākāra (अनाकार)
भक्ततोषार्थमाकाराः सगुणाः संति यद्यपि ।
तथापि त्वमनाकारः परमार्थदृशा विभो ॥ २३७॥
O Lord, although You assume forms with qualities to please Your devotees, in reality, from the absolute point of view, You are Anākāra (The Formless One).






Shloka 238: Andhibhūmyagnibalamna (अन्धिभूम्यग्निबलम्न)
भूवारितेजोमरुतां बलानि संस्तंभनक्लेदनदाहशोषाः ।
त्वयि क्षमास्ते न भवंति तेन त्वमन्धिभूम्यग्निबलम्न उक्तः ॥ २३८ ॥
The powers of earth, water, fire, air, and ether—solidifying, liquefying, burning, and drying—do not exist in You as limitations. Therefore, You are called Andhibhūmyagnibalamna (The One Unaffected by the Powers of the Elements).


Shloka 239: Avyaktalakṣaṇa (अव्यक्तलक्षण)
सच्चित्सुखैकमयता तु तव स्वरूपलक्ष्मप्रपंचकलना तु तटस्थलक्ष्म ।
न व्यज्यते तदु-भयं हि बहिर्मुखानां अव्यक्तलक्षण इति प्रथितोऽसि तेन ॥ २३९ ॥
Your true nature is an indivisible mass of Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss. The manifestation of the universe is a mere external attribute. Both of these are not revealed to those whose minds are turned outward. Therefore, You are renowned as Avyaktalakṣaṇa (The One with an Unmanifest Nature).


Shloka 240: Ādhārapīṭha (आधारपीठ)
आङ् व्यापनं वक्ति धरैव धारं शिवावधि क्ष्माव-धितत्त्वपंक्तेः ।
षट्त्रिंशतः पीठ इवासनं त्वमाधारपीठोऽसि ततो नमस्ते ॥ २४० ॥
The prefix "Ā" indicates pervasion; "Dhāra" means support. You are the support (Ādhāra) for the entire series of principles, from earth (Pṛthvī) up to Śiva, numbering thirty-six. Like a base (Pīṭha) for them, You are the seat. Therefore, You are the Ādhārapīṭha (The Foundational Seat). Obeisance to You!


Shloka 241: Ādhāra (आधार)
अकारो वासुदेवः स्यादाकारस्तु पिता-महः ।
तावुभौ यो धारयति स आधारो गणाधिपः ॥ २४१ ॥
The letter "A" is Vāsudeva, and the letter "Ā" is the Grandfather (Brahmā). He who sustains both of them is the Ādhāra (The Support), the Lord of the Gaṇas.


Shloka 242: Ādhārādheya-varjita (आधाराधेयवर्जित)
सर्वाधारस्याधारांतरविरहादतात्त्विकत्वाच्च ।
एषामाधेयाना-माधाराधेयवर्जितोऽसि ततः ॥ २४२ ॥
Because You are the support of all, yet are without any other support for Yourself, and because You are not a material substance, You are therefore free from the relationship of being a support (Ādhāra) and that which is supported (Ādheya). Thus, You are Ādhārādheya-varjita (The One Devoid of the Support-Supported Duality).


आखुकेतन आशापूरक आखुमहारथः ।


Shloka 243: Ākhu-ketana, Āśāpūraka, Ākhu-mahāratha (आखुकेतन, आशापूरक, आखुमहारथ)
आखुवाहं ध्वजीकुर्वन् दिशस्तृष्णां च पूरयन् ।
आखु-केतन आशापूरक आखुमहारथः ॥ २४३ ॥
He who has the mouse as His banner, who fulfills the desires (thirst) of the directions, is Ākhu-ketana (The One with the Mouse as His Emblem), Āśāpūraka (The Fulfiller of Hopes), and Ākhu-mahāratha (The Great Charioteer with the Mouse).


इक्षुसागरमध्यस्थ इक्षुभक्षणलालसः ॥ ७१॥
इक्षुचापातिरेकश्रीरिक्षुचापनिषेवितः ।
इन्द्रगोपसमानश्रीरिन्द्रनीलसमद्युतिः ॥ ७२॥


Shloka 244 - 245: Ikṣusāgara-madhyastha (इक्षुसागरमध्यस्थ), Ikṣubhakṣaṇa-lālasaḥ (इक्षुभक्षणलालसः), Ikṣucāpātirekaśrī (इक्षुचापातिरेकश्री) & Ikṣucāpa-niṣevita (इक्षुचापनिषेवितः)
इक्षुशब्दा-दिमं नामयुगलं प्रकटार्थकं ।
इक्षुचापातिरेकश्रीर्मदनाधिककांतिमान् ॥२४४॥
चाप-देवतया मूर्तिमत्या पंचशरेण वा ।
नित्यं यः सेव्यते सोऽय-मिक्षुचापनिषेवितः ॥ २४५ ॥
एतस्यैवारक्तरूपं भास्वरा-भास्वरासिते ।
This pair of names, beginning with the word "Ikṣu" (sugarcane), has a clear meaning. He who is situated in the ocean of sugarcane is Ikṣusāgara-madhyastha (The One Standing in the Ocean of Sugarcane). He who is eager to eat sugarcane is Ikṣubhakṣaṇa-lālasaḥ (The One Eager to Eat Sugarcane). He whose sugarcane-bow is exceptionally splendid is Ikṣucāpātirekaśrī (The One with the Superbly Splendid Sugarcane Bow). He who is constantly attended by the deity of the bow or by Cupid is Ikṣucāpa-niṣevita (The One Served by the Sugarcane Bow). His form is red, brilliantly white, black...


इन्दीवरदलश्याम  इन्दुमण्डलनिर्मलः ।
इध्मप्रिय इडाभाग इडावानिन्दिराप्रियः ॥ ७३॥


Shloka 246-247: Indragopa-samāna-śrī (इन्द्रगोपसमानश्री), Indranīla-sama-dyuti (इन्द्रनीलसमद्युति), Indīvara-dala-śyāma (इन्दीवरदलश्याम) & Indumaṇḍala-nirmala (इन्दुमण्डलनिर्मल)
शुक्लं च भास्वरं ध्येयं कामनाभेदतो जनैः ॥ २४६ ॥
युगभेदेन भेदस्तत्पुराणेऽपि निरूपितः ।
इति ध्वनयितुं नाम-चतुष्कं प्रकटार्थकं ॥ २४७ ॥
His form is to be meditated upon by people according to their desires. This distinction based on the different Ages has also been described in the Purāṇas. To suggest this, the following four names are given: He who has the lustre of the Indragopa insect is Indragopa-samāna-śrī (The One with the Splendour of an Indragopa). He who has the radiance of a blue sapphire is Indranīla-sama-dyuti (The One with the Radiance of a Sapphire). He who is dark blue like an Indīvara (blue lotus) petal is Indīvara-dala-śyāma (The One Dark as a Blue Lotus Petal). He who is as pure as the orb of the moon is Indumaṇḍala-nirmala (The One as Spotless as the Moon's Disc).


Shloka 248: Idhma-priya (इध्मप्रिय)
श्रौतस्मार्ताग्निरूपत्वाद्भवा-निध्मप्रियो मतः।
इडा मही हविः शेषो गौश्च भागस्तवैव यत् ॥ २४८ ॥
Because You are of the nature of the Vedic (Śrauta) and traditional (Smārta) fires, You are considered Idhma-priya (The One Fond of Sacrificial Fuel). This is because the offering (Ilā), the clarified butter (Haviḥ), the oblation (Idhma), and the cow (Go) are all portions of You.


Shloka 249: Iḍā-bhāga (इडाभाग)
ऋत्विग्याज्यादिरूपत्वादिडाभाग इतीर्यसे ॥
इरेति पृथ्वी तद्धामा तदंतर्यमवस्थितः ॥२४९॥
Because You exist in the forms of the priest, the sacrificer, etc., You are proclaimed as Iḍā-bhāga (The Portion of the Oblation). "Ilā" means the Earth; You are its abode and the Inner Controller residing within it.


Shloka 250: Īradhāma (ईरधाम) & Indirā-priya (इन्दिराप्रिय)
यः पृथिव्यां तिष्ठतीति बृहदारण्यकश्श्रुतेः ।
लक्ष्मीपूज्योऽथवा विष्णुरूप-त्वादिंदिराप्रियः ॥ २५० ॥
According to the Śruti (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad), "He who dwells in the earth..." (from the root √īr, to possess), thus He is Īradhāma (The One whose Abode is the Earth). Alternatively, because You are worthy of being worshipped by Lakṣmī or because of Your form as Viṣṇu, You are Indirā-priya (The Beloved of Indirā/Lakṣmī).




इक्ष्वाकुविघ्नविध्वंसी इतिकर्तव्यतेप्सितः ।
ईशानमौलिरीशान ईशानसुत ईतिहा ॥ ७४॥


Shloka 251: Ikṣvāku-vighna-vidhvaṃsī (इक्ष्वाकुविघ्नविध्वंसी)
इक्ष्वाकुः कटुतुंबी तत्तुल्यप्रत्यूहनाशनात् ।
इक्ष्वाकुविघ्न-विध्वंसी राज्ञो वा विघ्ननाशनात् ॥ २५१॥
"Ikṣvāku" is the bitter Tumbī gourd; because He destroys obstacles just like it (i.e., effectively), He is Ikṣvāku-vighna-vidhvaṃsī (The Destroyer of Obstacles like the Ikṣvāku [gourd]). Alternatively, because He destroys the obstacles of a king (Ikṣvāku), He is so called.


Shloka 252: Itikartavyata-epṣita (इतिकर्तव्यतेप्सित)
इतिकर्तव्यताशब्दः क्रत्वंगस्तोमवाचकः ।
तामपेक्ष्य ददत्कामानितिकर्तव्यते-प्सितः ॥ २५२ ॥
The word "Itikartavyatā" refers to the parts (Aṅga) and praises (Stoma) of a ritual (Kratu). He who, being depended upon (for the ritual's success), grants desires, is Itikartavyata-epṣita (The One Sought After for the Performance of Duties/Rituals).


Shloka 253: Īśāna-mauli (ईशानमौलि)
नरभूतसुरेशाद्या ईशाना वहवोऽमिताः ।
तेषामेषोऽधिको मौलिरमितैश्वर्ययोगतः ॥ २५३॥
The lords (Īśāna) such as Nara, Bhūta, and Sureśa are many and boundless. He is the foremost (Mūli) among them, being endowed with immeasurable lordship. Thus, He is Īśāna-mauli (The Crest-Jewel among all Lords).


Shloka 254: Īśāna (ईशान) & Īśāna-suta (ईशानसुत)
अनयन् जीवयन्नीशानीशान इति कीर्त्यसे ।
ईशानेन सुतः सोमरूप-स्तस्य सुतोऽथवा ॥ २५४ ॥
You are called Īśāna (The Supreme Ruler) because You lead (beings in) and enliven (the universe). Alternatively, because You are the son of Īśāna (Śiva), You are Īśāna-suta (The Son of Īśāna).


Shloka 255: Ītihā (ईतिहा)
अतिवृष्टिमनावृष्टिं मूषकान् शल-भान् शुकान् ।
स्वचक्रं परचक्रं च विनिघ्नन्नीतिहा मतः ॥२५५॥
He who destroys calamities (Īti) such as excessive rain, drought, mice, locusts, parrots, internal revolt (one's own circle), and external invasion (enemy's circle) is considered Ītihā (The Destroyer of Calamities).


ईषणात्रयकल्पान्त ईहामात्रविवर्जितः । 
उपेन्द्र उडुभृन्मौलिरुडुनाथकरप्रियः ॥ ७५॥


Shloka 256: Īṣaṇātraya-kalpānta (ईषणात्रयकल्पान्त) & Īhāmātra-vivarjitaḥ (ईहामात्रविवर्जितः)
लोकपुत्रधनेच्छानां तिसृणां प्रलयात्मकः ।
ईषणात्रय-कल्पांतो नृणां वैराग्यदायकः ॥ २५६ ॥
निश्चेष्टचित्स्वरूपत्वादीहामात्रविवर्जितः ।
He who is the great dissolution (Pralaya) for the threefold desires (Īṣaṇā) for worldly pleasure, progeny, and wealth, and who is the final conflagration (Kalpānta) for this triad of desires, bestowing dispassion upon men, is Īṣaṇātraya-kalpānta (The Destructive End for the Triad of Desires). Because His essential nature is Consciousness without activity, He is devoid of even the slightest effort (Īhā); thus He is Īhāmātra-vivarjitaḥ (The One Devoid of Mere Effort).


Shloka 257: Upendra (उपेन्द्र)
वामनेन सहाभेदादुपेंद्र इति कीर्तितः ॥ २५७ ॥
Due to His non-difference from Vāmana (the dwarf incarnation), He is proclaimed as Upendra (The Younger Brother of Indra).


Conclusion of the Fourth Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ ॥
गणेशनामखद्योते चतुर्थमभवच्छतम् ॥ ४०० ॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), composed by Bhāskara,
also known as Agnicit, ends the fourth hundred of the Ganesha Sahasranama.


Shloka 258: Uḍubhṛn-mauliḥ (उडुभृन्मौलिः)
बिभ्रत्युडूनि या द्यौः सा यस्य विश्वात्मनः शिरः । सत्त्व-मेवोडुभृन्मौलिर्धा मूर्धानमिति श्रुतेः ॥ २५८ ॥ यद्वा चंद्रा-वतंसत्वादुड्डुभृन्मौलिरुच्यसे ।
The firmament (Dyauḥ) which bears the stars (Uḍu) is His head, since He is the Soul of the Universe. According to the Śruti, "The essence itself is the head-bearer of stars." 
Or, because He wears the moon as His crest-jewel, You are proclaimed as Uḍubhṛn-mauliḥ (The One who has the Moon, the Bearer of Stars, as His Crest-Jewel).


Shloka 259: Uṇḍeraka-bali-priyaḥ (उण्डेरकबलिप्रियः)
उंडेरकाः पूषभेदा वर्तुला-कृतयोऽल्पकाः ॥ २५९ ॥ उपहारास्तवेष्टा इत्युंडेरकबलि-प्रियः ।
Uṇḍerakās are a type of small, round-shaped cake (Puṣpa-bheda, a kind of offering). They are offerings dear to You. Hence, He is Uṇḍeraka-bali-priyaḥ (The One Fond of Uṇḍeraka Offerings). Previous commentators, accepting a variant reading with the letter 'ra', have explained it as "He who is the mover and lord of insentient cosmic eggs (Brahmāṇḍa)" and "He who is dear to the collective life-force." However, since that interpretation is incongruent with the series of names beginning with 'U', it is to be disregarded.


उन्नतानन उत्तुंग उदारत्रिदशाग्रणीः ॥ 
ऊर्जस्वानूष्मलमद ऊहापोहदुरासदः ॥ ७६ ॥


Shloka 260: Unnatānana (उन्नतानन)
उत्कृष्टानामजादीनां प्राणनादुन्नताननः ॥ २६० ॥
He is Unnatānana (The Lofty-Faced One) because he is the vital breath (Prāṇa) and the source of the call (Nāda) of the foremost beings like Brahmā (Aja).


Shloka 261: Uttuṅga (उत्तुंग)
तुंगा नाम नदी काचित् वराहवपुषस्तव । दंष्ट्राया उद्गता तस्मा-द्भवानुत्तुंग ईरितः ॥ २६१॥
There is a certain river named Tuṅgā which originated from the tusk of your Varāha (boar) form. From that (Tuṅgā), you are called Uttuṅga (The Elevated One / One who Lifted [the Earth]).


Shloka 262: Udāra-tridaśāgraṇī (उदारत्रिदशाग्रणी)
उदारो दातृमहतोस्त्रिदशो देव उच्यते । महादेवादपि श्रेष्ठ उदारत्रिदशाग्रणीः ॥२६२॥
"Udāra" means great giver or magnificent. "Tridaśa" means god (deva). Being superior even to Maheśvara (Śiva), He is Udāra-tridaśāgraṇī (The Magnificent Leader of the Gods).


Shloka 263: Ūrjasvān (ऊर्जस्वान्) & Uṣmalamada (ऊष्मलमद)
ऊर्जस्वानपि तेजोवानत एव मदोदकं । ऊष्मं स्रवति गंडाभ्यां तेनोष्मलमदो भवान् ॥ २६३ ॥
He is Ūrjasvān (The Energetic / Vigorous One), full of splendor. From that very (vigor), the intoxicating fluid (Mada) flows with warmth from his temples; therefore, you are Uṣmalamada (The One with Warm Intoxicating Fluid).


Shloka 264: Ūhāpoha-durāsada (ऊहापोहदुरासद)
ऊहो वितर्कस्तत्त्वस्यापोह-स्तात्त्विकबाधनम् । ताभ्यां दुरासदोऽप्राप्य ऊहापोहदुरासदः ॥ २६४ ॥
Ūha (hypothetical reasoning) is conjecture about the Truth; Apoha (exclusion) is the refutation of such speculative truth. Unattainable by both (reasoning and refutation), He is Ūhāpoha-durāsada (The Unapproachable One, Beyond Conjecture and Refutation).


ऋग्यजुः सामसं भूतिऋद्धिसिद्धिप्रवर्तकः ॥ 
ऋजुचित्तैकसुलभ ऋणत्रयविमोचकः ॥ ७७ ॥


Shloka 265: Ṛgyajuḥsāmasaṁbhūti (ऋग्यजुःसामसंभूति) & Ṛddhisiddhipravartakaḥ (ऋद्धिसिद्धिप्रवर्तकः)
ऋग्यजुःसामसंभूतिर्यस्य निःश्वसितं त्रयी । राज्याणि-मादिदातृत्वादृद्धिसिद्धिप्रवर्तकः ॥ २६५॥
He whose exhalation (Nissvasita) is the three Vedas (Ṛg, Yajus, Sāma) is Ṛgyajuḥsāmasaṁbhūti (The Source of the Ṛg, Yajus, and Sāma Vedas). Because he is the bestower of sovereignty (Rājya) and other powers from the very beginning, He is Ṛddhisiddhipravartakaḥ (The Initiator of Prosperity and Accomplishment).


Shloka 266: Ṛjucittaikasulabha (ऋजुचित्तैकसुलभ)
दृश्यते त्वग्र्यया बुद्ध्येत्युक्तेर्यद्विमलं मनः । तद्ब्राह्यऋजुचित्तैकसुल भस्तेन गीयसे ॥२६६॥
That pure mind is attained through the highest intellect, as stated. Since that (mind) is to be attained, you are praised as Ṛjucittaikasulabha (The One Easily Attained by a Straightforward Mind Alone).


Shloka 267: Ṛṇatrayavimocakaḥ (ऋणत्रयविमोचकः)
पुत्रीयंति यियक्षंति वेदानिच्छंति ये द्विजाः । तत्कामना पूरकत्वादृणत्रयविमोचकः ॥ २६७ ॥
Because he fulfills the desires of those brahmins who wish for progeny (Putrīyanti), who wish to perform sacrifices (Yiyakṣanti), and who wish to study the Vedas (Vedānicchanti), He is Ṛṇatrayavimocakaḥ (The Liberator from the Three Debts).






लुप्तविघ्नः स्वभक्तानां लुप्तशक्तिः सुरद्विषाम् । 
लुप्तश्रीर्विमुखार्चानां लूताविस्फोटनाशनः ॥ ७८ ॥


Shloka 268: Svabhaktānāṁ Luptavighnaḥ (स्वभक्तानां लुप्तविघ्नः), Suradviṣāṁ Luptaśaktiḥ (सुरद्विषां लुप्तशक्तिः), Vimukhārcānāṁ Luptaśrīḥ (विमुखार्चानां लुप्तश्रीः) & Lūtā-Visphoṭanāśanaḥ (लूता-विस्फोटनाशनः)
लुप्तशब्दादिकं नामत्रितयं प्रकटार्थकं । दंतलूतादयो रोगा आंतरा बहिरामयाः ॥ २६८ ॥ स्फोटाद्यास्तान्निहंतीति लूताविस्फोटनाशनः ।
The first three names, beginning with the word 'Lupta' (destroyed), have a clear meaning. He for whom obstacles are destroyed for his own devotees is Svabhaktānāṁ Luptavighnaḥ (The One who Destroys Obstacles for His Devotees). He who destroys the power of the enemies of the gods is Suradviṣāṁ Luptaśaktiḥ (The One who Destroys the Power of the Enemies of the Gods). He who destroys the prosperity of those who worship him insincerely is Vimukhārcānāṁ Luptaśrīḥ (The One who Destroys the Prosperity of Insincere Worshippers). Internal and external diseases, such as tooth-ache (Dantalūtā) and eruptions (Sphoṭa), are destroyed by him; therefore, He is Lūtā-Visphoṭanāśanaḥ (The Destroyer of Diseases like Lūtā and Eruptions).


एकारपीठमध्यस्थ एकपादकृतासनः ॥  
एजिताखिलदैत्यश्रीरेधिताखिलसंश्रयः ॥७९॥ 
ऐश्वर्यनिधिरैश्वर्यमैहिकामुष्मिकप्रदः ॥ 
ऐरंमदसमोन्मेष ऐरावतनिभाननः ॥ ८० ॥


Shloka 269: Ēkārapīṭhamadhyasthaḥ (एकारपीठमध्यस्थ)
एकारपीठमध्यस्थस्त्रिकोणांतःस्थबिंदुगः ॥ २६९ ॥
He who is situated in the middle of the single seat (of the monosyllable oṁ), within the triangle, and is the possessor of the Bindu (dot) is Ēkārapīṭhamadhyasthaḥ (The One Seated in the Middle of the Syllabic Seat 'E').




Shloka 270: Ēkapādakṛtāsanaḥ (एकपादकृतासनः) & Ējitākhiladaityaśrīḥ (एजिताखिलदैत्यश्रीः)
काश्यामेकपदा तिष्ठन्नेकपादकृतासनः । एजिताखिल-दैत्यश्रीः कंपितासुरसंषदः ॥ २७० ॥
He who stands on a single foot in Kāśī, having made a single foot his seat, is Ēkapādakṛtāsanaḥ (The One who has Made a Single Foot His Seat). He whose entire assembly of demons is made to tremble and whose glory of demons is agitated is Ējitākhiladaityaśrīḥ (The One who Agitates the Entire Glory of Demons).


Shloka 271: Ēdhitākhilasaṁśrayaḥ (एधिताखिलसंश्रयः)
स्वाश्रिता वर्धिता ये-नैधिता चाखिलसंश्रयः । ऐश्वर्यनिधिरैश्वर्य भक्तेषु निदधाति यः ॥ २७१ ॥
He by whom all his dependents are increased and nourished is Ēdhitākhilasaṁśrayaḥ (The One who Nourishes All who Seek Refuge). He who places lordship (Aiśvarya) in his devotees (is the treasure of sovereignty).


Shloka 271-272: Aiśvaryanidhiḥ (ऐश्वर्यनिधिः) & Aiśvaryam (ऐश्वर्यम्)
ऐश्वर्यनिधिरैश्वर्य भक्तेषु निदधाति यः ॥ २७१ ॥ ईश्वरेषु यदैश्वर्य संपत्तिः सा त्वमेव हि । एषाऽस्य परमा संपदिति बोधयति श्रुतिः ॥ २७२ ॥
He who places the treasure of sovereignty and lordship in his devotees is Aiśvaryanidhiḥ (The Treasure of Sovereignty). That supreme prosperity and sovereignty which is in all lords (Īśvaras) is indeed you. As the Śruti indicates, "This is his supreme wealth."


Shloka 273: Aihikāmuṣmikapradaḥ (ऐहिकामुष्मिकप्रदः)
दत्से सुतादीन्स्वर्गे चेत्यैहिकामुष्मिकप्रदः । विद्युत्तुल्यस्त-वोन्मेषः क्षणाद्भासितभासि च ॥ २७३ ॥
You grant sons, etc., in this world and heaven in the next; thus you are Aihikāmuṣmikapradaḥ (The Bestower of this-Worldly and Other-Worldly Rewards). Your manifestation is like lightning—in an instant you illuminate the illuminator.


Shloka 273: Airamadasamonmeṣaḥ (ऐरंमदसमोन्मेषः)
विद्युत्तुल्यस्त-वोन्मेषः क्षणाद्भासितभासि च ॥ २७३ ॥ ऐरंमदसमोन्मेषः स्वार्थे ज्ञेयोऽणिरंमदात् ।
Your manifestation is like lightning. Therefore, you are to be understood in its own meaning (as the syllable ai) from 'Aṇiramada'. Thus, He is Airamadasamonmeṣaḥ (The One whose Flashing Forth is Equal to the Splendor of 'Airamada' / Lightning).


Shloka 274: Airāvatanibhānanaḥ (ऐरावतनिभाननः)
ऐरावतसमास्यत्वादैरावतनिभा-ननः ॥ २७४ ॥
Because his face is equal to Airāvata (the divine elephant), He is Airāvatanibhānanaḥ (The One whose Face is Like Airāvata).


ॐकारवाच्य ॐकार ओजस्वानोषधीपतिः ॥ 
औदार्यनिधिरौद्धत्यधुर्य औन्नत्यनिस्वनः॥ ८१ ॥


Shloka 275: Oṁkāravācyaḥ (ॐकारवाच्य)
क्लेशादिरहितमीश्वरमुक्तत्वाग्रे तस्य वाचकः प्रणवः । इति योगशास्त्रसूत्रे कथनादोंकारवाच्यस्त्वम् ॥ २७५ ॥ इति शब्दोऽपि त्वंभेदाभावेन वाच्यवाच्यवाचकयोः ।
According to the statement in the Yoga Śāstra Sūtra that "the sacred syllable Praṇava (Oṁ) denotes the Lord who is free from afflictions, etc.," you are denoted by Oṁ. Therefore, you are Oṁkāravācyaḥ (The One Signified by the Syllable Oṁ).


Shloka 276: Oṁkāraḥ (ॐकार)
अत एव त्वयि वाक्यैराकारोऽपि प्रत्ययो युक्तः ॥ २७६ ॥
For this very reason, through scriptural statements, the word itself (Oṁ) is also a valid cognition for you. Thus, He is Oṁkāraḥ (The Sacred Syllable Oṁ).


Shloka 277: Ojasvān (ओजस्वान्)
ओजोऽष्टम-धातुरिति प्रोक्तं यद्वेदभाष्यकृता । शौर्योत्कर्षनिदानं तेज-स्तद्वांस्त्वमोजस्वान् ॥ २७७ ॥
It is stated by the commentator of the Vedas that "Ojas is the eighth dhātu (constituent element)." That which is the source of the excellence of valor and is splendor—you possess that. Therefore, you are Ojasvān (The Vigorous / Powerful One).


Shloka 277: Oṣadhīpatiḥ (ओषधीपतिः)
ओषधीपतिरौदार्यनिधि-रित्याह्वयौ स्फुटौ ॥
The names Oṣadhīpati (Lord of Herbs) and Audāryanidhi (Treasure of Magnanimity) are clearly two appellations.


Shloka 277: Oṣadhīpatiḥ (ओषधीपतिः)
Thus, He is Oṣadhīpatiḥ (The Lord of Herbs).


Shloka 278: Audāryanidhiḥ (औदार्यनिधिः) & Auddhatyadhuryakaḥ (औद्धत्यधुर्यकः)
औद्धत्यमुद्धतत्वं यत् स्वभक्तानुजिघृ-क्षया ॥ २७८ ॥ स्वोत्कर्षद्योतनं तत्र श्रेष्ठ औद्धत्यधुर्यकः ।
Thus, He is Audāryanidhiḥ (The Treasure of Magnanimity). That which is haughtiness (Uddhatatva) for the sake of wishing to protect his own devotees, and which manifests his own superiority, is supreme in that. Therefore, He is Auddhatyadhuryakaḥ (The Supreme in Divine Haughtiness).
Shloka 279: Aunnatyanisvanaḥ (औन्नत्यनिस्वनः)
बृंहितं यस्य सर्वोच्चं सस्यादौन्नत्यनिस्वनः ॥ २७९ ॥
He whose roar (Bṛṁhita) is the highest of all, is Aunnatyanisvanaḥ (The One whose Sound is Supreme Elevation).


अङ्कुशः सुरनागानामङ्कुशः सुरविद्विषाम्। 
समस्तविसर्गान्तपदेषु परिकीर्तितः ॥ ८२ ॥


Shloka 280: Aṅkuśaḥ Suranāgānām (अंकुशः सुरनागानाम्)
अंकुशः सुरनागानां त्रैलोक्यस्य नियामकः । ऊर्ध्व सुरा अधो नागा मर्त्यलोकोपलक्षणं ॥ २८० ॥
He is the controller (Aṅkuśa) of the gods (Sura) and serpents (Nāga) and the regulator of the three worlds. The gods are above, the serpents below, with the mortal world in between. Thus, He is Aṅkuśaḥ Suranāgānām (The Goad for the Gods and Serpents).


Shloka 281: Aṅkuśaḥ Suravidviṣām (अंकुशः सुरविद्विषाम्)
द्विषः सुराणां च विदां सुष्ठु सम्यग्रविद्विषां । निग्राहकत्वान्निर्दिष्टोऽस्यं-कुशः सुरविद्विषाम् ॥ २८१ ॥
Because he restrains the enemies (Vidviṣ) of the gods, who know how to oppose properly, He is designated as Aṅkuśaḥ Suravidviṣām (The Goad for the Enemies of the Gods).


Shloka 282: Samastavisargāntapadeṣu Parikīrtitaḥ (समस्तविसर्गातपदेषु परिकीर्तितः)
अकाराद्येकपंचाशद्विसर्गा-तार्णबोधितः । अःसमस्तविसर्गातपदेषु परिकीर्तितः ॥ २८२ ॥ प्राचा-त्वसमस्तेति विसर्गनिर्मुक्तमाद्यक्षरं पठित्वा केवलविसर्गातप्रथमैकवचनपदैः कीर्तित इति व्याख्यातं, -तन्मातृकाक्रमाननुगणत्वादचिंत्यम् ।
He is known through all words ending in Visarga (the sound 'ḥ'), from the letter 'A' to the fifty-first (the Visarga). Therefore, He is Samastavisargāntapadeṣu Parikīrtitaḥ (The One Proclaimed in All Words Ending in Visarga). Some earlier commentators have interpreted this by reading the first letter without the Visarga, explaining it as "He is proclaimed by words in the singular form ending in Visarga alone." However, as this does not follow the sequence of the alphabet (Mātṛkā), it is not to be considered.


कमंडलुधरः कल्पः कपर्दी कलभाननः ॥ 
कर्मसाक्षी कर्मकर्ता कर्माकर्मफलप्रदः ॥ ८३ ॥




Shloka 283: Kamaṇḍaludharaḥ (कमंडलुधरः) & Kalpaḥ (कल्पः)
कमंडलुधरः शृंगेनामृतकुंभधृकू । कल्पः प्रलयकाला-त्मा समर्थः कल्पनेऽपि वा ॥ २८३ ॥
He who holds the water pot (Kamaṇḍalu) with his tusk is Kamaṇḍaludharaḥ (The Bearer of the Water Pot). He who is the very essence of the time of dissolution (Kalpa), or who is capable in creation (Kalpanā), is Kalpaḥ (The Aeon / The Creator).


Shloka 284: Kapardī (कपर्दी)
वराटधारी मल्लारिस्त्वं कपर्यथवा जटी ।
You wear the sacred skull-ornament (Varāṭa) or have matted locks. Thus, He is Kapardī (The One with Matted Hair / Skull-Ornament).


Shloka 284: Kalabhānanaḥ (कलभाननः)
कलो नादो भा तु दीप्तिरननं प्राणनं मतं ॥२८४॥ नादकांत्युज्जीवकत्वात्कथ्यते कलभाननः ।
'Kala' is sound (Nāda); 'Bhā' is light or splendor (Dīpti); 'Anana' is considered to be breathing (Prāṇana). Because he is the animator (Ujjīvakatva) of sound and splendor, He is called Kalabhānanaḥ (The One whose Face is Sound and Splendor).


Shloka 285: Karmasākṣī (कर्मसाक्षी)
कर्म-साक्षी त्वदृष्टानामपि द्रष्टासि कर्मणां ॥ २८५॥
You are the witness of the actions of even the unseen beings. Therefore, He is Karmasākṣī (The Witness of Actions).


Shloka 286: Karmakartā (कर्मकर्ता) & Karmākarmaphalapradaḥ (कर्माकर्मफलप्रदः)
कर्मठप्रेरणा-दन्तः कर्मकर्ता भवानपि । स्वर्गापवर्गदातृत्वात् कर्माकर्म-फलप्रदः ॥ २८६ ॥
You are also the inner instigator of activity, thus you are Karmakartā (The Doer of Actions). Because you bestow the fruits (heaven and liberation) for both action (Karma) and inaction (Akarma), you are Karmākarmaphalapradaḥ (The Bestower of the Fruits of Action and Inaction).


कदंबगोलकाकारः कूष्मांडगणनायकः ॥ 
कारुण्यदेहः कपिलः कथकः कटिसूत्रभृत् ॥  ८४ ॥


Shloka 287: Kadambagolakākāraḥ (कदंबगोलकाकारः)
सर्वनाड्युद्गमस्थानं कदंबकुसुमाकृतिः । परितः संवृतात्मा-भिर्हदि काप्यस्ति देवता ॥ २८७ ॥ 
कदंबगोलकाकार इति तद्रूप उच्यसे ।
There is a deity in the heart from which all the subtle channels (Nāḍīs) originate, having the shape of a Kadamba flower-bud. Since you are that form, you are called Kadambagolakākāraḥ (The One with the Form of a Kadamba Bud).


Shloka 288: Kūṣmāṇḍagaṇanāyakaḥ (कूष्मांडगणनायकः)
दुष्टग्रहाणां नेता त्वं कूष्मांडगण-नायकः ॥ २८८ ॥
You are the leader of the host of malignant planets (Kūṣmāṇḍa Gaṇa). Thus, He is Kūṣmāṇḍagaṇanāyakaḥ (The Leader of the Kūṣmāṇḍa Host).


Shloka 289: Kāruṇyadehaḥ (कारुण्यदेहः), Kapilaḥ (कपिलः), Kathakaḥ (कथकः) & Kaṭisūtrabhṛt (कटिसूत्रभृत्)
कारुण्यदेहः करुणामूर्तित्वात्कपिलो मुनिः । कर्णतो वाऽसि कथकः 
संप्रदायान्प्रवर्तयन् ॥२८९॥ रत्नप्रोतस्वर्णकांची धारणात्कटिसूत्रभृत् ।
Because your body is compassion itself, and because you are the sage Kapila (the embodiment of compassion), you are Kāruṇyadehaḥ (The One whose Body is Compassion). Thus, He is Kapilaḥ (The Sage Kapila / The Tawny-Colored One). Or, you are Kathakaḥ (The Narrator / Preacher) as you propagate traditions from ear to ear. Because you wear a golden girdle (Kañcī) strung with gems, you are Kaṭisūtrabhṛt (The Wearer of the Sacred Girdle).


खर्वः खङ्गप्रियः खङ्गखांतान्तस्थः खनिर्मलः ॥ 
खल्वाटशृंगनिलयः खड्यांगी खदुरासदः ॥८५॥


Shloka 290: Kharvaḥ (खर्वः) & Khaṅgapriyaḥ (खङ्गप्रियः)
खर्वो वामनरूपत्वात्खङ्गो गंडकसंज्ञकः ॥ २९०॥ मृगोऽ-सिर्वा प्रियो यस्य स तु खङ्गप्रियो मतः ॥
Because you are of the form of the dwarf (Vāmana), you are Kharvaḥ (The Dwarf / Short One). 'Khaṅga' is the name of a musk-deer or a sword (Asi). He to whom that is dear is considered Khaṅgapriyaḥ (The Lover of the Sword / Musk-Deer).


Shloka 291: Khaṅgakhāntāntasthaḥ (खङ्गखांतान्तस्थः)
खङ्गशब्दे खात्परो डस्तदंते गाक्षरे तव ॥ २९१ ॥ बीजे तिष्ठस्यतः खङ्गखांतां-तस्थ इतीर्यसे ।
In the word 'Khaṅga', the letter 'Ga' comes after 'Kha'. You abide in that Bīja (seed-syllable). Therefore, you are proclaimed as Khaṅgakhāntāntasthaḥ (The One Abiding at the End of the Letter 'Kha' in 'Khaṅga').


Shloka 292: Khanirmalaḥ (खनिर्मलः)
आकाशवत्सर्वगोऽपिनिर्लेपोऽतः खनिर्मलः ॥ २९२ ॥
Like space (Ākāśa), you are all-pervading and unsullied. Therefore, you are Khanirmalaḥ (The One Pure as Space).


Shloka 293: Khalvāṭaśṛṅganilayaḥ (खल्वाटशृंगनिलयः)
वृक्षा एव गिरेः केशाः खल्वाटस्तैर्विवर्जितः । खल्वाटशृंग निलयस्तच्छृंगेऽपि वसन् मतः ॥२९३॥
Trees are the hair of the mountain. Khalvāṭa (bald) is one devoid of them. He who resides on the bald peak (of the mountain) and dwells even on that peak is Khalvāṭaśṛṅganilayaḥ (The Abode on the Bald Peak).


Shloka 294: Khaḍyāṅgī (खड्यांगी) & Khadurāsadaḥ (खदुरासदः)
खङ्घां-गीति भवान् ख्यातः खङ्गांगाख्यायुधं दधत् । अमूर्तो व्योम-वद्धर्तुं अशक्यः खदुरासदः ॥ २९४ ॥
You are famed as Khaḍyāṅgī (The One with Limbs of Space / Wielding the Sword-Khaḍga) as you bear the weapon called Khaḍgāṅga. Being formless like the sky (Vyoma), you are impossible to grasp. Therefore, you are Khadurāsadaḥ (The Unassailable One).


गुणाढ्यो गहनो गस्थो गद्यपद्यसुधार्णवः ॥ 
गद्यगानप्रियो गर्जो गीतगीर्वाणपूर्वजः ॥८६॥


Shloka 295: Guṇāḍhyaḥ (गुणाढ्यः), Gahanaḥ (गहनः), Gasthaḥ (गस्थः) & Gadyapadyasudhārṇavaḥ (गद्यपद्यसुधार्णवः)
अनंतकल्याणगुणो गुणाढ्यो गंतुं न शक्यो गहनोऽसि गस्थः । बीजे निजे तिष्ठसि गद्यपद्यसुधार्णवः काव्यरसा-करत्वात् ॥ २९५॥
Endowed with infinite auspicious qualities, He is Guṇāḍhyaḥ (The Abundant in Virtues). He cannot be reached; He is impenetrable, thus He is Gahanaḥ (The Impenetrable / Profound). He abides in his own seed (syllable 'Ga'), thus He is Gasthaḥ (The Abider in 'Ga'). He is the ocean of the nectar of prose and poetry because He is the source of poetic sentiment; thus He is Gadyapadyasudhārṇavaḥ (The Ocean of the Nectar of Prose and Verse).


Shloka 296: Gadyagānapriyaḥ (गद्यगानप्रियः) & Garjaḥ (गर्जः)
गदितुं योग्यं गद्यं न निषिद्धं साम ते प्रियं गानं । तेनासि गद्यगानप्रियोऽथ गर्जोऽसि मेघनादात्मा ॥ २९६ ॥
Prose (Gadya) is that which is fit to be spoken; the Sāman (chant) is not forbidden. Song (Gāna) is dear to you. Therefore, you are Gadyagānapriyaḥ (The Lover of Prose and Song). 
Moreover, you are of the nature of the thunder of clouds, thus you are Garjaḥ (The Thundering One).


Shloka 297: Gītagīrvāṇapūrvajaḥ (गीतगीर्वाणपूर्वजः)
नादात् गीतादयः शब्दा नादार्थादमरादयः । जातास्तद्व्यरूपत्वाङ्गीतगीर्वाणपूर्वजः ॥ २९७ ॥
From sound (Nāda), words like 'Gīta' (song) arise; from the meaning of sound, the gods (Amara) and others arise. Since He is the very form of that, He is Gītagīrvāṇapūrvajaḥ (The Forefather of Song and Speech).


गुह्याचाररतो गुह्यो गुह्यागमनिरूपितः ॥ 
गुहाशयो गुहाब्धिस्थो गुरुगम्यो गुरोर्गुरुः ॥ ८७ ॥


Shloka 298: Guhyācārarataḥ (गुह्याचाररत)
गुहाप्रविष्टावात्मानौ गुह्यौ तौ मनसा चरन् । अंतर्मुखस्तत्र तुष्टो गुह्याचाररतो मतः ॥ २९८ ॥
The two selves (individual and supreme) entered into the cave (of the heart) are secret (Guhya). Dwelling on them with the mind, turned inward, and content therein, He is considered Guhyācārarataḥ (The One Delighting in Secret Practice).


Shloka 299: Guhyaḥ (गुह्यः) & Guhyāgamanirūpitaḥ (गुह्यागमनिरूपितः)
गुह्य एकांतवेद्यत्वा-द्धितकारी गुहाय वा । रहस्यतंत्रवेद्यत्वाद्गुह्यागमनिरूपितः ॥ २९९ ॥
He is secret (Guhya) because He is to be known in solitude or because He is beneficial to the cave (heart). He is Guhyāgamanirūpitaḥ (The One Defined by the Secret Scriptures) because He is to be known through the esoteric Tantras (Rahasyatantra).


Shloka 300: Guhāśayaḥ (गुहाशयः) & Guhābdhisthaḥ (गुहाब्धिस्थः)
गुहायां हृदयाकाशे शेते यः स गुहाशयः । अव्याकृतं नभोगूढमगाधं च गुहाब्धिवत् ॥ ३०० ॥
He who lies in the cave, the sky of the heart, is Guhāśayaḥ (The One who Dwells in the Cave). It is unmanifest, concealed by space, unfathomable like the cave-ocean.
अधि-तिष्ठसि तत्तेन गुहाब्धिस्थ इतीर्यसे ॥ ३०० ॥
You preside over that. Therefore, you are proclaimed as Guhābdhisthaḥ (The One Abiding in the Ocean-Cave).


Shloka 301: Gurugamyaḥ (गुरुगम्यः)
गुरुगम्यो गुरुप्रोक्त-योगोपायेन लभ्यसे ॥ ३०१ ॥
He is to be approached (Gamya) through the Guru; He is attainable only by the means of Yoga taught by the Guru. Thus, He is Gurugamyaḥ (The One Attainable through the Guru).


Shloka 302: Gurorguruḥ (गुरोर्गुरुः)
वेदा अध्यापिता येन ब्रह्मणे स गुरोर्गुरुः । शिवसूत्रे गुरुरूपाय इत्येवं हि वर्णितं ॥३०२॥
He who taught the Vedas to Brahmā is the Guru of the Guru (Brahmā). Thus, He is Gurorguruḥ (The Guru of the Guru). As described in the Śivasūtra, "To Him in the form of the Guru..."


घंटाघर्घरिकामाली घटकुंभो घटोदरः ॥ 
चंडचंडेश्वरसुहृच्चंडीशचंडविक्रमः ॥ ८८ ॥


Shloka 303: Ghaṇṭāghargharikāmālī (घंटाघर्घरिकामाली)
घंटेव मंजुनादा घर्घरिकाः किंकिणीभेदाः । तद्धारी घंटा-घर्घरिकामालीति बाललीला ते ॥ ३०३ ॥
Bells (Ghaṇṭā) are those with a sweet sound; Ghargharikās are a type of small tinkling bells (Kiṅkiṇī). He who wears a garland (Mālī) of such bells and ghargharikās, such is your childhood play. Thus, He is Ghaṇṭāghargharikāmālī (The One Wearing a Garland of Bells and Tinkling Ornaments).


Shloka 304: Ghaṭakumbhaḥ (घटकुंभः), Caṇḍaḥ (चंडः) & Ghaṭodaraḥ (घटोदरः)
कुंभौ न्युजाम्बु-घटाकारौ घटकुंभस्ततो मतः । घटोदरो बृहत्कुक्षिश्चंडो भूरिपराक्रमः ॥ ३०४ ॥
His two breasts are shaped like water-pots (Ghaṭā). Therefore, He is Ghaṭakumbhaḥ (The One whose Breasts are like Pots). He is large-bellied, thus He is Ghaṭodaraḥ (The Pot-Bellied One). He is fierce (Caṇḍa) with immense valor. Thus, He is Caṇḍaḥ (The Fierce One).


Shloka 305: Caṇḍeśvarasuhṛt (चंडेश्वरसुहृत्), Caṇḍīśaḥ (चंडीशः) & Caṇḍavikramaḥ (चंडविक्रमः)
