चंडेश्वरः सुहृद्यस्य शैवश्चंडेश्वरः सखा । चंडीराश्चंडिकानाथश्चंडा 
अत्यंतकोपनाः ॥ ३०५ ॥ तानाक्रम्य वशे कुर्वन्नाख्यातश्चंडविक्रमः ।
He whose friend is Caṇḍeśvara (the fierce lord, a form of Śiva), the Śaiva deity Caṇḍeśvara is his companion. Thus, He is Caṇḍeśvarasuhṛt (The Friend of Caṇḍeśvara). He who is the lord (Īśa) of Caṇḍikā, thus He is Caṇḍīśaḥ (The Lord of Caṇḍikā). The Caṇḍās are those of extreme wrath. He who overpowers them and brings them under control is proclaimed as Caṇḍavikramaḥ (The One with Fierce Prowess).






चराचरपतिचिंतामणिचर्वणलालसः ॥ 
छंदरछंदोवपुश्छंदोदुर्लक्ष्यश्छंदविग्रहः ॥ ८९ ॥


Shloka 306: Carācarapatiḥ (चराचरपतिः)
चराचरपतिः स्थास्तुश्चरिष्णुर्जगदीश्वरः ॥ ३०६ ॥
He is the lord of all that moves and does not move, of the stationary and the mobile, the Lord of the universe. Thus, He is Carācarapatiḥ (The Lord of the Moving and the Non-Moving).


Shloka 307: Cintāmaṇicarvaṇalālasaḥ (चिंतामणिचर्वणलालसः)
चिंतामणिकामदुघाकल्पद्रुमगर्वचर्वणतः । चिंतामणिचर्वण-लालस इति कथितोऽस्यचिन्त्यदानेन ॥ ३०७ ॥
He who is eager to chew (i.e., enjoy) the Cintāmaṇi (wish-fulfilling gem) and the Kalpadruma (wish-fulfilling tree) which yield all desires and thus crush their pride. Therefore, he is called Cintāmaṇicarvaṇalālasaḥ (The One Eager to Savour the Cintāmaṇi), due to his granting of inconceivable boons.


Shloka 308: Chandaraḥ (छंदरः), Chandovapuḥ (छंदोवपुः) & Chandodurlakṣyaḥ (छंदोदुर्लक्ष्यः)
गायत्र्या-द्यात्मना छन्दो भवान् वेदमयात्मना । छन्दो वपुस्तथा छन्दो दुर्लक्ष्यो वेददुर्ग्रहः ॥ ३०८ ॥
In the form of Gāyatrī and other metres (Chanda), you are of the nature of the Veda. Your body is Vedic metre (Chanda) and thus you are Chandovapuḥ (The One whose Body is Vedic Metre). You are difficult to perceive as the Veda is difficult to grasp; thus you are Chandodurlakṣyaḥ (The Inscrutable One of the Veda). (Chandaraḥ is implied as the enjoyer of the metres/forms).


Shloka 309: Chandavigrahaḥ (छंदविग्रहः)
स्वाभिप्रायानुसारीणि भक्तेः परवशानि वा । अवतारशरीराणि यस्य स च्छंद-विग्रहः ॥ ३०९ ॥
He whose incarnated bodies are either in accordance with his own will or subject to the will of his devotees is Chandavigrahaḥ (The One of Willful Incarnations).


जगद्योनिर्जगत्साक्षी जगदीशो जगन्मयः ॥ 
जपो जपपरो जप्यो जिह्वासिंहासनप्रभुः ॥ ९० ॥








Shloka 310: Jagadyoniḥ (जगद्योनिः), Jagatsākṣī (जगत्साक्षी), Jagadīśaḥ (जगदीशः) & Jaganmayaḥ (जगन्मयः)
जगद्योनिर्जगत्साक्षी जगदीशो जगन्मयः । विश्वस्य कारणं द्रष्टा नाथोऽधिष्ठानमेव च ॥ ३१० ॥
He is the womb (source) of the universe, the witness of the universe, the lord of the universe, and pervaded by the universe. He is the cause of the universe, its seer, its master, and its very substratum. Thus, He is Jagadyoniḥ (The Source of the Universe), Jagatsākṣī (The Witness of the Universe), Jagadīśaḥ (The Lord of the Universe), and Jaganmayaḥ (The One who Pervades the Universe).


Shloka 311: Japaḥ (जपः), Japaparaḥ (जपपरः), Japyaḥ (जप्यः) & Jihvāsiṃhāsanaprabhuḥ (जिह्वासिंहासनप्रभुः)
जपो जपपरो जप्यः कर्मकर्तृक्रियात्मकः । कीर्त्यमानं यस्य नाम जिह्वा-सिंहासनस्थितं ॥ ३११ ॥ ददाति पुरुषार्थान् स जिह्वा-सिंहासनप्रभुः ।
He is the act of muttering (Japa), devoted to muttering, and the object of muttering, being of the nature of action, the doer, and the act itself. He whose name, when recited, resides on the throne of the tongue and bestows the supreme goals of life is Jihvāsiṃhāsanaprabhuḥ (The Lord Seated on the Throne of the Tongue). Thus, He is also Japaḥ (The Japa), Japaparaḥ (The One Devoted to Japa), and Japyaḥ (The Object of Japa).


झलज्झल्लोल्लसद्दानझंकारिभ्रमराकुलः ॥ 
टंकारस्फारसंरावष्टंकारिमणिनूपुरः ॥ ९१ ॥


Shloka 312: Jhalajjhallollasadāna-jhaṅkāri-bhramarākulaḥ (झलज्झलोल्लसद्दानझङ्कारिभ्रमराकुलः)
झलज्झल्लोल्लस हान झंकारिभ्रमराकुलः ॥३१२॥ नदंति गंडयो भृंगात्स्फुरन्मदपिपांसवः ।
The intoxicated bees buzz with a jhalajjhal sound around the streaming ichor (Dāna). Thus, He whose temples resound with the buzzing of bees drunk on the flowing ichor is Jhalajjhallollasadāna-jhaṅkāri-bhramarākulaḥ (The One whose Streaming Ichor is Loud with the Buzzing of Drunken Bees).


Shloka 313: Ṭaṅkāra-sphāra-saṁrāvaḥ (टंकारस्फारसंरावः)
कांस्यटंकारवत्स्फारो यस्य मंजुतरो रखः ॥ ३१३ ॥ टंकारस्फारसंराव इति लोके स गीयते ।
He whose beautiful sound (Rava) is more pervasive and resonant than the clang of bronze is celebrated in the world as Ṭaṅkāra-sphāra-saṁrāvaḥ (The One with a Sound Pervasive as the Clang of Bronze).


Shloka 314: Ṭaṅkāri-maṇi-nūpuraḥ (टंकारिमणिनूपुरः)
नदद्रलांघिकटकष्टकारिमणिनूपुरः ॥ ३१४ ॥
He whose anklets (Nūpura), studded with tinkling gems, make a ṭaṅkāra sound is Ṭaṅkāri-maṇi-nūpuraḥ (The One with Gem-Anklets that Tinkle).


ठद्वयीपल्लवान्तः स्थसर्वमंत्रैकसिद्धिदः ॥ 
डिंडिगुंडो डाकिनीशो डामरो डिण्डिमप्रियः ॥ ९२ ॥


Shloka 315: Ṭhadvayī-pallavāntaḥstha-sarvamantraika-siddhidaḥ (ठद्वयीपल्लवान्तः स्थसर्वमंत्रैकसिद्धिदः)
ठद्वयीपल्लवांतःस्थसर्वमंत्रैकसिद्धिदः । स्वाहान्ता-खिलमंत्रोत्थमुख्यसिद्धिप्रदो यतः ॥ ३१५॥
He who abides within the tip of the two-leafed sprout (of the heart) is the bestower of all perfections (Siddhi) through all mantras. Since He grants the principal perfections arising from all mantras ending in svāhā, He is Ṭhadvayī-pallavāntaḥstha-sarvamantraika-siddhidaḥ (The One Residing in the Two-Leafed Sprout, Bestower of All Siddhis through Every Mantra).


Shloka 316: Ḍiṇḍimuṇḍaḥ (डिंडिमुंडः)
डिंडीनामैकदेशेन डिंडिमौ न्युब्जदुंदुभी । तावेव कुंभौ यन्मुण्डे डिंडिमुंडः स उच्यते ॥ ३१६ ॥
By a part of the word ḍiṇḍī, ḍiṇḍima refers to the two kettle-drums (Dundubhi). Those very two (drums) are the breasts on his head; therefore, he is called Ḍiṇḍimuṇḍaḥ (The One with the Drums [Breasts] on His Head).


Shloka 317: Ḍākinīśaḥ (डाकिनीशः)
डाकिन्यो नाम योगिन्यः काश्चित्सत्य-गणादिमाः । षोढा न्यासादिषु स्पष्टा डाकिनीशस्तदीश्वरः ॥ ३१७॥
The Ḍākinīs are certain yoginīs, like Satyā, Gaṇādi, etc., clearly known in the sixfold placement (Nyāsa) and other practices. He is their lord; thus He is Ḍākinīśaḥ (The Lord of the Ḍākinīs).


Shloka 318: Ḍāmaraḥ (डामरः) & Ḍiṇḍimapriyaḥ (डिण्डिमप्रियः)
डामरस्तु महांस्तन्त्रविशेषो वा तदात्मकः । दुंदुभौ पेटहध्वानेनानंदी डिण्डिमप्रियः ॥ ३१८ ॥
Ḍāmara is a great, special Tantra, or He is of that nature. He delights in the sound of the kettle-drum (Dundubhi) and the war-drum (Peṭaha); therefore, He is Ḍiṇḍimapriyaḥ (The Lover of the Drum-Sound).


ढक्कानिनादमुदितो ढौको ढुंढिविनायकः ॥ 
तत्त्वानां परमं तत्त्वं तत्त्वंपदनिरूपितः ॥ ९३ ॥




Shloka 319: Ḍhakkāninādamuditaḥ (ढक्कानिनादमुदितः)
ढक्कानिनादमुदित इति नामद्वयाशयः । गत्यर्थात् ढौकतेढौंकः सर्वगोवासिसर्ववित् ॥ ३१९ ॥
The intent is expressed by two names: delighted by the sound of the Ḍhakkā drum. He goes everywhere (from the root √ḍhauk, to move), thus He is Ḍhakkāninādamuditaḥ (The One Delighted by the Sound of the Ḍhakkā Drum).


Shloka 319: Ḍhaukaḥ (ढौकः)
Thus, He is Ḍhaukaḥ (The Pervader / Mover).


Conclusion of the Fifth Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ । 
गणेशनामसाहस्रखद्योते पंचमं शतम् ॥ ५०० ॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), composed by Bhāskara,
also known as Agnicit, ends the fifth hundred of the Ganesha Sahasranama.


Shloka 320: Ḍhuṇḍhivināyakaḥ (ढुंढिविनायकः)
काशीखंडे ढुंढिधातुः प्रथितोऽन्वेषणार्थकः । विशिष्टो नायकोऽन्वेष्य इति ढुंढिविनायकः ॥ ३२०॥
In the Kāśīkhaṇḍa, the root ḍhuṇḍh is famous in the sense of 'seeking'. He is the distinguished leader who is to be sought; therefore, He is Ḍhuṇḍhivināyakaḥ (The Leader who is Sought / The Sought-After Lord).


Shloka 321: Tattvānāṁ Paramam Tattvam (तत्त्वानां परमं तत्त्वं)
तत्त्वानां तत्त्वं पंचविंशतितः परम् । त्रयं सृष्ट्यादि स्वप्नादि वाच्यं धर्मत्वमा-सदा ॥३२१॥
He is the supreme reality beyond the twenty-five principles. The triad of creation, etc., and dream, etc., are to be spoken of as having the nature of Dharma, and so on.


Shloka 321: Tattvampadanirūpitaḥ (तत्त्वंपदनिरूपितः)
त्यत्क्त्वा धमीणि भक्त्यैव तत्त्वंपदनिरूपितः ।
Having abandoned all that, He is defined by the word 'Tat Tvam' (That Thou Art) through devotion alone. Thus, He is Tattvampadanirūpitaḥ (The One Defined by the Phrase 'That Thou Art').










तारकान्तरसंस्थानस्तारकस्तारकान्तकः स्थाणुः ॥ 
स्थाणुप्रियः स्थाता स्थावरं जङ्गमं जगत् ॥ ९४ ॥


Shloka 322: Tārakāntarasaṁsthānaḥ (तारकान्तरसंस्थानः)
तारकान्तर संस्थानस्तारकाक्ष्णः कनीनिका ॥ ३२२ ॥ तस्यां चिन्त्यो य एषोऽन्तरक्षिणीत्यादिकश्रुतेः ।
He resides within the pupil of the eye of the one looking at the stars. As the Śruti says, "He is to be meditated upon within the eye." Thus, He is Tārakāntarasaṁsthānaḥ (The One Dwelling Within the Star of the Eye).


Shloka 323: Tārakaḥ (तारकः)
तारको भव-पाथोधेस्त्वमेव प्रणवो यथा ॥ ३२३ ॥
You are the one who ferries across the ocean of existence, like the sacred syllable Praṇava. Thus, He is Tārakaḥ (The Ferryman / Deliverer).


Shloka 323: Tārakāntakaḥ (तारकान्तकः)
तारकासुरहंत्रात्मा कथितस्तारकान्तकः ।
He is the one whose soul is the slayer of the demon Tārakāsura. Therefore, He is proclaimed as Tārakāntakaḥ (The Destroyer of Tārakāsura).


Shloka 324: Sthāṇuḥ (स्थाणुः)
स्थाणुः कल्पान्तवाताग्निसलिलैरप्य-कंपितः ॥ ३२४ ॥
He is the immovable one (Sthāṇuḥ), unshaken even by the wind, fire, and water at the end of the cosmic age.


Shloka 324: Sthāṇupriyaḥ (स्थाणुप्रियः)
स्थाणोः शिवस्य पुत्रत्वात्स्थाणुप्रिय इतीर्यसे ।
Because you are the son of the immovable one (Sthāṇu, Śiva), you are proclaimed as Sthāṇupriyaḥ (The Beloved of the Immovable One).


Shloka 325: Sthātā (स्थाता)
स्थाता युद्धेऽपि कल्पेऽपि निष्कम्पो गगनादिवत् ॥ ३२५ ॥
He remains steady, even in battle and even during cosmic dissolution, unshakable like the sky. Thus, He is Sthātā (The Abiding / Steady One).


Shloka 325: SthāvaraṂ JaṅgamaṂ Jagat (स्थावरं जङ्गमं जगत्)
चराचरात्मकत्वेन स्थावरं जङ्गमं जगत् ।
Since He is the soul of all that moves and does not move, He is SthāvaraṂ JaṅgamaṂ Jagat (The Universe of Moveable and Immoveable Beings).


दन्तप्रभिन्नाभ्रमालो दैत्यवारणदारणः ॥
दंष्ट्रालग्नद्विपघटो देवार्थनृगजाकृतिः ॥ ९५॥


Shloka 326: Dakṣayajñapramathanaḥ (दक्षयज्ञप्रमथनः)
दक्षयज्ञप्रमथनो भवानेव शिवात्मना ॥ ३२६ ॥
You yourself, in the form of Śiva, are the destroyer (Pramathana) of Dakṣa's sacrifice. Thus, He is Dakṣayajñapramathanaḥ (The One who Destroyed Dakṣa's Sacrifice).


Shloka 327: Dātā (दाता), Deyaḥ (देयः) & Dānavamohanaḥ (दानवमोहनः)
दाता देयः शोधनार्थाद्भवान् पतितपावनः । दानवांस्तत्त्वविमुखान् कुर्वन् दानवमोहनः ॥ ३२७ ॥
He is the giver (Dātā) and the worthy oblation (Deyaḥ). He is the purifier of the fallen. He deludes the demons (Dānava) who are averse to the truth; thus He is Dānavamohanaḥ (The Deluder of Demons).


Shloka 328: Divyavibhavaḥ (दिव्यविभवः)
दद्यावान् परदुःखानि प्रहारयितुमिच्छसि । प्राप्त्यर्थाद्दीव्यतेर्दिव्यविभवः प्रापकः श्रियाम् ॥३२८॥
You desire to strike down the sorrows of others. From the root 'div' (to shine, to obtain), you are the divine splendor (Divyavibhavaḥ) and the bestower of prosperity.


Shloka 329: Daṇḍabhṛt (दण्डभृत्) & Daṇḍanāyakaḥ (दण्डनायकः)
दण्डभृत् कथितो दण्डनीतेर परिलोपतः । यमेन्द्रादिषु ये दण्डास्तन्नेता दण्डनायकः ॥ ३२९ ॥
He is called Daṇḍabhṛt (The Bearer of the Rod) because he never fails in the administration of justice. He is the leader (Netā) of the rods of punishment held by Yama, Indra, and others; thus He is Daṇḍanāyakaḥ (The Leader of Divine Punishment).










दंतप्रभिन्नाभ्रमालो दैत्यवारणदारणः ॥
दंष्ट्रालमद्विपघटो देवार्थनृगजाकृतिः ॥ ९६ ॥


Shloka 330: Dantaprabhinnābhramālaḥ (दंतप्रभिन्नाभ्रमालः)
यच्छिरःकंपमात्रेण मेघपंक्तिः प्रभिद्यते । दंतप्रभिन्ना-भ्रमाल इति स त्वं निगद्यसे ॥ ३३० ॥
By the mere shaking of your head, the row of clouds is split. Therefore, you are proclaimed as Dantaprabhinnābhramālaḥ (The One whose Tusk Splits the Row of Clouds).


Shloka 331: Daityavāraṇadāraṇaḥ (दैत्यवारणदारणः)
वारयन् दारयन् दैत्यान् दैत्यवारणदारणः । यस्य दंष्ट्रैकदेशेऽपि लग्ना रिपुगजच्छटाः ॥ ३३१ ॥
He who restrains (Vārayan) and tears apart (Dārayan) the demons is Daityavāraṇadāraṇaḥ (The Restrainer and Destroyer of Demons). Even on one part of his tusk, the multitudes of enemy elephants are impaled.


Shloka 331: Daṃṣṭrālagnadvipaghaṭaḥ (दंष्ट्रालग्नद्विपघटः)
दंष्ट्रालग्नद्विपघट इति नाम्ना स गीयते ॥ ३३१ ॥
He is sung by the name Daṃṣṭrālagnadvipaghaṭaḥ (The One on whose Tusk is Attached a Group of Elephants).


Shloka 332: Devārthanṛgajākṛtiḥ (देवार्थनृगजाकृतिः)
देवानां कार्यसिद्धय॑र्थ येन मर्त्यद्विपाकृतिः ॥ ३३२॥ स्वीकृता स स्मृतो नाम्ना देवार्थनृगजाकृतिः ।
He who assumed the form of a human-elephant (Nṛgaja, i.e., the elephant-headed form) for the sake of accomplishing the work of the gods is remembered by the name Devārthanṛgajākṛtiḥ (The One who Took the Human-Elephant Form for the Gods' Purpose).


धनधान्यपतिर्धन्यो धनदो धरणीधरः ॥ 
ध्यानैकप्रकटो ध्येयो ध्यानं ध्यानपरायणः ॥९७॥


Shloka 333: Dhanadhānyapatiḥ (धनधान्यपतिः)
धनधान्यप्रदत्वेन धनधान्यपतिर्भवान् ॥३३३॥
Because you are the bestower of wealth and grain, you are Dhanadhānyapatiḥ (The Lord of Wealth and Grain).


Shloka 334: Dhanyaḥ (धन्यः)
धन्य-स्त्वं धनर्लब्धृत्वात् पुण्यवान्वापि गीयसे ॥ ३३४ ॥
You are Dhanyaḥ (The Fortunate / Auspicious One) because you are the obtainer of wealth, or you are praised as the virtuous one.


Shloka 334: Dhanadaḥ (धनदः)
धनदो धनदानेन कुबेरात्मतयापि वा ॥
You are the bestower of wealth (Dhanadaḥ), either by giving riches or because you are of the nature of Kubera himself.


Shloka 334: Dharaṇīdharaḥ (धरणीधरः)
शेषेणादिद्वराहेणाप्यभिन्नो धरणीधरः ।
Being non-different from Śeṣa and from the Primeval Boar (Varāha), you are Dharaṇīdharaḥ (The Supporter of the Earth).


Shloka 335: Dhyānaikaprakaṭaḥ (ध्यानैकप्रकटः)
मनसैवानुद्रष्टव्यो ध्यानैकप्रकटो भवान् ॥ ३३५ ॥
You are to be perceived only by the mind; you are Dhyānaikaprakaṭaḥ (The One who is Revealed Only through Meditation).


Shloka 335: Dhyeyaḥ (ध्येयः), Dhyānam (ध्यानम्) & Dhyānaparāyaṇaḥ (ध्यानपरायणः)
त्वमेव त्रिपुटी ध्येयो ध्यानं ध्यानपरायणः ॥
You yourself are the triad: the object of meditation (Dhyeyaḥ), the act of meditation (Dhyānam), and the one devoted to meditation (Dhyānaparāyaṇaḥ).


नन्दो नन्दिप्रियो नादो नादमध्यप्रतिष्ठितः ॥ 
निष्कलो निर्मलो नित्यो नित्यानित्यो निरामयः ॥ ९८ ॥


Shloka 336: Nandaḥ (नन्दः) & Nandipriyaḥ (नन्दिप्रियः)
नंद आनंदनीयोऽसि नंदिकेश्वरवल्लभः ॥ ३३६ ॥ नंदि-प्रियोऽसि
You are adorable and blissful (Nandaḥ). You are the beloved of Nandikeśvara. Thus, you are Nandaḥ (The Blissful One) and Nandipriyaḥ (The Beloved of Nandi).




Shloka 336: Nādaḥ (नादः)
नादोऽसि योऽन्तर्दशविधध्वनिः
You are the primordial sound (Nāda) which is the tenfold inner vibration. Thus, He is Nādaḥ (The Primordial Sound).


Shloka 337: Nādamadhyapratiṣṭhitaḥ (नादमध्यप्रतिष्ठितः)
गम्यो नादानु-संधानान्नादमध्यप्रतिष्ठितः ॥ ३३७॥
He is attained through the contemplation of sound, and is established in the midst of that sound. Thus, He is Nādamadhyapratiṣṭhitaḥ (The One Established in the Center of Sound).


Shloka 338: Niṣkalaḥ (निष्कलः)
निष्कलोऽवयवैहींनो
He is without parts, devoid of any components. Thus, He is Niṣkalaḥ (The Partless One).


Shloka 338: Nirmalaḥ (निर्मलः)
निर्मलो दूषणोज्झितः
He is pure, free from all impurities. Thus, He is Nirmalaḥ (The Stainless One).


Shloka 338: Nityaḥ (नित्यः)
नित्यो नाशोज्झितो
He is eternal, free from destruction. Thus, He is Nityaḥ (The Eternal One).


Shloka 338: Nityānityaḥ (नित्यानित्यः)
नित्यानित्यो खोर्यादिरूपधृकू ॥ ३३८ ॥
He is both eternal and non-eternal (Nityānityaḥ), assuming forms such as the sky, etc.


Shloka 338: Nirāmayaḥ (निरामयः)
अविद्येव महारोगस्तदस्पृष्टो निरामयः ।
He is untouched by the great disease of ignorance. Thus, He is Nirāmayaḥ (The Diseaseless One).












परंव्योम परन्धाम परमात्मा परं पदम् ॥
परात्परः पशुपतिः पशुपाशविमोचकः ॥ ९९ ॥


Shloka 339: Paraṁ Vyoma (परं व्योम)
अव्याकृतं यदाकाशं परंव्योम तदात्मकः ॥ ३३९ ॥
He is the unmanifest ether (Ākāśa), whose nature is the supreme void. Thus, He is Paraṁ Vyoma (The Supreme Ether).


Shloka 339: Paraṁ Dhāma (परं धाम)
ज्योतिषामपि यज्ज्योतिस्तत्परन्धाम कथ्यते ।
He is the light of even all other lights; that is called the supreme abode. Thus, He is Paraṁ Dhāma (The Supreme Abode).


Shloka 340: Paramātmā (परमात्मा)
परमः सर्व-जीवेभ्यः परमात्मेति गद्यते ॥ ३४०॥
He is the highest of all living beings; therefore, He is declared as Paramātmā (The Supreme Self).


Shloka 340: Paraṁ Padam (परं पदम्)
कैलासादिपदेभ्योऽपि पैरमत्वात्परं पदम् ।
Because He is higher than even the state of Kailāsa and other realms, He is Paraṁ Padam (The Supreme State/Vishnu's Abode).


Shloka 341: Parātparaḥ (परात्परः)
ब्रह्मविष्णुमहेशादप्युत्तमत्वात् परात्परः ॥ ३४१ ॥
Because He is superior even to Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Maheśa (Śiva), He is Parātparaḥ (The One Greater than the Greatest).


Shloka 341: Paśupatiḥ (पशुपतिः)
ब्रह्मादिकीटकान्तानां पाता पशुपतिर्मतः ।
He is the protector (Pātā) of all beings from Brahmā down to the smallest insect; therefore, He is considered Paśupatiḥ (The Lord of Beings).


Shloka 342: Paśupāśavimocakaḥ (पशुपाशविमोचकः)
द्विपंचाशद्विधाः पाशाः सांख्यतंत्रादिषु स्फुटाः ॥ ३४२ ॥ ब्रह्मादीन्मोचयंस्तेभ्यः पशुपाशविमोचकः ।
The fifty-two types of bonds (Paśa) are clearly described in the Sāṅkhya, Tantra, and other scriptures. He who liberates even Brahmā and others from these bonds is Paśupāśavimocakaḥ (The Liberator from the Bonds of Fetters).


पूर्णानन्दः परानन्दः पुराणपुरुषोत्तमः ॥ 
पद्मप्रसन्ननयनः प्रणताज्ञानमोचनः ॥ १०० ॥


Shloka 343: Pūrṇānandaḥ (पूर्णानन्दः)
पूर्णानन्दः क्रियाकर्तृकर्मभेदोज्झितं सुखं ॥ ३४३ ॥
He is the bliss that is devoid of the distinctions of action, doer, and instrument. Thus, He is Pūrṇānandaḥ (The Complete / Perfect Bliss).


Shloka 344: Parānandaḥ (परानन्दः)
भूम्यादिब्रह्मपर्यन्तानन्दान् शतगुणोत्तरान् । सर्वानप्यधरी-कुर्वन् परानंदो महासुखम् ॥ ३४४ ॥
He surpasses all pleasures, from those of the earth up to those of Brahmā, each a hundred times greater than the previous. Therefore, He is Parānandaḥ (The Supreme Bliss).


Shloka 345: Purāṇapuruṣottamaḥ (पुराणपुरुषोत्तमः)
क्षरेभ्यः सर्वभूतेभ्यः कूटस्थादक्षरादपि । उत्तमत्वादनादित्वात् पुराणपुरुषोत्तमः ॥ ३४५ ॥
Because He is superior to all perishable beings, and also superior to the imperishable, changeless Self (Kūṭastha), and because He is without beginning, He is Purāṇapuruṣottamaḥ (The Ancient Supreme Person).


Shloka 346: Padmaprasannanayanaḥ (पद्मप्रसन्ननयनः)
पद्मप्रसन्ननयनो विकसत्कमलेक्षणः ।
He has eyes as gracious (Prasanna) and beautiful as the lotus; He is Padmaprasannanayanaḥ (The One whose Eyes are Gracious as Lotuses).


Shloka 346: Praṇatājñānamocanaḥ (प्रणताज्ञानमोचनः)
नमतां तत्त्वबोधेन प्रणताज्ञानमोचनः ॥ ३४६ ॥
He who liberates the ignorance (Ajñāna) of those who bow down (Praṇata) to Him, through the knowledge of the Truth, is Praṇatājñānamocanaḥ (The Liberator of the Ignorance of the Devout).




प्रमाणप्रत्ययातीतः प्रणतार्तिनिवारणः ॥ 
फलहस्तः फणिपतिः फेत्कारः फाणितप्रियः ॥ १०१ ॥


Shloka 347: Pramāṇapratyayātītaḥ (प्रमाणप्रत्ययातीतः)
प्रमाणप्रत्ययातीतो नित्यज्ञानैकविग्रहः । प्रमाकरण-जानां हि प्रमितीनामनित्यता ॥ ३४७॥
He transcends all proofs (Pramāṇa) and cognitions (Pratyaya), for He is the sole embodiment of eternal knowledge. Indeed, the cognitions (Pramiti) arising from instruments of knowledge are impermanent. Thus, He is Pramāṇapratyayātītaḥ (The One Beyond All Proofs and Cognitions).


Shloka 347: Praṇatārtinivāraṇaḥ (प्रणतार्तिनिवारणः)
नमतां नाशयन् पीडां प्रणतार्तिनिवारणः ।
He who destroys the afflictions of those who bow to Him is Praṇatārtinivāraṇaḥ (The Remover of the Afflictions of the Devout).


Shloka 348: Phalahastaḥ (फलहस्तः)
फलहस्तः स्वभक्तानामविलंबात्फलं ददत् ॥ ३४८ ॥
He who holds the fruit in His hand and bestows the results (of actions) upon His devotees without delay is Phalahastaḥ (The One with a Fruit in His Hand).


Shloka 348: Phaṇipatiḥ (फणिपतिः)
वासुकेरपि शेषस्य स्वामी फणिपतिर्मतः ।
He is the lord of Vāsuki and Śeṣa; thus He is considered Phaṇipatiḥ (The Lord of Serpents).


Shloka 349: Phetkāraḥ (फेत्कारः)
फेत्कार इति तन्नाम्ना तन्त्रं तत्र निरूप्यसे ॥ ३४९ ॥
You are described in the Tantra by the name Phetkāraḥ (The Exclamation Pheṭ).


Shloka 349: Phāṇitapriyaḥ (फाणितप्रियः)
खण्डाह्वयेक्षुविकृतौ प्रीतिमान् फाणितप्रियः ॥
He who delights in Phāṇita (sugarcane juice concentrate) – which is a preparation made from earth-colored sugar lumps – is Phāṇitapriyaḥ (The One Fond of Sugarcane Juice).






बाणार्चिताङ्ङ्गियुगलो बालकेलिकुतूहली ॥ 
ब्रह्म ब्रह्मार्चितपदो ब्रह्मचारी बृहस्पतिः ॥ १०२ ॥


Shloka 350: Bāṇārcitāṅghriyugalaḥ (बाणार्चिताङ्घ्रियुगलः) & Bālakelikutūhalī (बालकेलिकुतूहली)
बाणासुरेण पूज्यत्वाद्बाललीलासु कौतुकात् ॥ ३५० ॥ बाणार्चितांत्रियुगलो बालकेलिकुतूहली ।
Because He is worshipped by the demon Bāṇa, He is Bāṇārcitāṅghriyugalaḥ (The One whose Pair of Feet is Worshipped by Bāṇa). Due to His curiosity in child-like playful sports, He is Bālakelikutūhalī (The One Eager in Child-like Play).


Shloka 351: Brahma (ब्रह्म)
ब्रह्म त्वमेव वेदान्तवेद्यतत्त्वात्मकत्वतः ॥ ३५१ ॥
You are indeed Brahman (the Supreme Reality), being the essence of the Truth known through the Vedānta. Thus, He is Brahma (The Absolute).


Shloka 351: Brahmārcitapadaḥ (ब्रह्मार्चितपदः)
ब्रह्मार्चितपदो वेदैः श्लाघनीयं हि ते पदम् । प्राचा तु विष्णुविधिभ्यामर्चिते पदे यस्येत्य॑र्थकमेकमेवेदं 'ब्रह्मब्रह्मार्चितपदम्' इति नामेत्युक्तं, -तत्पक्षे सरूपैकशेषो दुर्वारः ।
He whose feet (or state) are worshipped by Brahmā (or the Vedas) is Brahmārcitapadaḥ (The One whose Feet are Worshipped by Brahmā). (The commentary notes an alternative interpretation from previous commentators, considering 'Brahma Brahmārcitapadam' as a single name, but then argues it leads to grammatical difficulty.)


Shloka 352: Brahmacārī (ब्रह्मचारी)
ब्रह्मचारी भवान् येनाकृतदार-परिग्रहः ॥ ३५२ ॥
He is Brahmacārī (The Celibate Student) because He has not accepted a wife.


Shloka 352: Bṛhaspatiḥ (बृहस्पतिः)
वाचस्पतिस्वरूपेण देवार्च्यत्वाद्वृहस्पतिः ।
Because He is worshipped by the gods in the form of Vācaspati (Bṛhaspati, the Lord of Speech), He is Bṛhaspatiḥ (The Lord of Prayer/Strength).








बृहत्तमो ब्रह्मपरो ब्रह्मण्यो ब्रह्मवित्प्रियः ॥ 
बृहन्नादाग्यचीत्कारो ब्रह्माण्डावलिमेखलः ॥ १०३ ॥


Shloka 353: Bṛhattamaḥ (बृहत्तमः)
बृहतो ब्रह्मविष्ण्वादीनतिशेषो बृहत्तमः ॥ ३५३ ॥
He who is greater than the great ones like Brahmā and Viṣṇu is Bṛhattamaḥ (The Greatest of the Great).


Shloka 353: Brahmaparaḥ (ब्रह्मपरः)
वेदैकतत्परो धातुः श्रेष्ठो ब्रह्मपरो मतः ।
He who is solely devoted to the Vedas, the creator (Dhātuḥ), the supreme, is considered Brahmaparaḥ (The One Whose Supreme Goal is Brahman / Devoted to Brahman).


Shloka 354: Brahmaṇyaḥ (ब्रह्मण्यः)
ब्रह्मण्य इति विख्यातो ब्राह्मणान् मानयन् स्वयम् ॥ ३५४ ॥
He who honors Brahmins is renowned as Brahmaṇyaḥ (The Favorable to Brahmins).


Shloka 354: Brahmavidpriyaḥ (ब्रह्मवित्प्रियः)
प्रिया ब्रह्म-विदो यस्य स मतो ब्रह्मवित्प्रियः ।
He to whom the knowers of Brahman (Brahmavit) are dear is considered Brahmavidpriyaḥ (The Beloved of the Knowers of Brahman).


Shloka 355: Bṛhannādāgyacītkāraḥ (बृहन्नादाग्यचीत्कारः)
चीत्कारो गजशब्दः स मेघाशन्यादितो महान् ॥ ३५५॥ तवास्तीति बृहन्नादाग्य-चीत्कार इति स्मृतः ।
The 'Cītkāra' is the great sound of the elephant, which is mightier than the sound of thunder and lightning. Because you possess that, you are remembered as Bṛhannādāgyacītkāraḥ (The One with the Great Sound and the Elephant's Trumpet).


Shloka 356: Brahmāṇḍāvalimekhalaḥ (ब्रह्माण्डावलिमेखलः)
कटिसूत्रे किंकिणीव ब्रह्माण्डान्येव गुंफ-यन् ॥ ३५६ ॥ तादृग्दधद्विश्वरूपं ब्रह्माण्डावलिमेखलः ।
He who strings together the multitude of cosmic eggs (Brahmāṇḍas) like tinkling bells (Kiṅkiṇī) on his girdle, thus bearing the universal form, is Brahmāṇḍāvalimekhalaḥ (The One Whose Girdle is a String of Cosmic Eggs).




भ्रूक्षेपदत्तलक्ष्मीको भर्गो भद्रो भयापहः ॥ 
भगवान् भक्तिसुलभो भूतिदो भूतिभूषणः ॥ १०४ ॥


Shloka 357: Bhrūkṣepadattalakṣmīkaḥ (भ्रूक्षेपदत्तलक्ष्मीकः)
भ्रूक्षेपद‌त्तलक्ष्मीको भक्तेभ्यः संपदो ददत् ॥ ३५७ ॥
He who bestows prosperity (Lakṣmī) upon His devotees merely by the movement (Kṣepa) of His eyebrow is Bhrūkṣepadattalakṣmīkaḥ (The One who Bestows Prosperity by a Glance of His Brow).


Shloka 357: Bhargaḥ (भर्गः)
भर्गो गायत्र्युदीरितः ।
He is the one praised as Bharga (the Radiant) in the Gāyatrī mantra. Thus, He is Bhargaḥ (The Effulgent Destroyer of Sins).


Shloka 358: Bhadraḥ (भद्रः)
भद्रा मन्द्रा मृगाश्चेति सन्ति द्विरदजातयः ॥ ३५८ ॥ तेषूत्तमा भद्रजाति-र्भद्रस्त्वं तादृशाननः ।
Among the three classes of elephants – Bhadra, Mandra, and Mṛga – the Bhadra class is the best. You, whose face is like that, are Bhadraḥ (The Auspicious/Excellent One).


Shloka 359: Bhayāpahaḥ (भयापहः)
द्वितीयाद्वै भयं यत्तदपन्नंस्त्वं भयापहः ॥ ३५९ ॥
The fear that arises from duality (Dvaita) – You remove that. Thus, You are Bhayāpahaḥ (The Remover of Fear).


Shloka 360: Bhagavān (भगवान्)
उत्पत्तिं च विनाशं च भूतानामगतिं गतिम् । वेत्सि विद्यामविद्यां चेत्युच्यसे भगवानिति ॥ ३६० ॥
You know the origin, destruction, the path and the wrong path of beings, as well as knowledge and ignorance. Therefore, You are called Bhagavān (The Lord possessing Supreme Opulences).


Shloka 361: Bhaktisulabhaḥ (भक्तिसुलभः)
भक्त्यैव लब्धुं यः शक्यः स भक्तिसुलभो महान् ।
He who is attainable only through devotion (Bhakti) is the great Bhaktisulabhaḥ (The Easily Attainable through Devotion).




Shloka 361: Bhūtidaḥ (भूतिदः) & Bhūtibhūṣaṇaḥ (भूतिभूषणः)
सिद्धीर्दत्से भस्म धत्से भूतिदो भूतिभूषणः ॥ ३६१ ॥
You bestow all powers (Siddhis) and you wear sacred ashes (Bhasma). Thus, You are Bhūtidaḥ (The Bestower of Prosperity/Powers) and Bhūtibhūṣaṇaḥ (The One Adorned with Sacred Ashes).


भव्यो भूतालयो भोगदाता भ्रूमध्यगोचरः ॥ 
मन्त्रो मन्त्रपतिर्मन्त्री मदमत्तमनोरमः ॥ १०५ ॥


Shloka 362: Bhavyaḥ (भव्यः)
भव्यो भावुकरूपत्वाद्भाविविश्वात्मकत्वतः ॥ ३६२ ॥
He is Bhavyaḥ (The Auspicious/Gracious One) because He is of the nature of benevolence and because He is the soul of the future universe.


Shloka 362: Bhūtālayaḥ (भूतालयः)
भूतानां भूजलादीनां पिशाचानां च देहिनाम् ॥ अतीतानाम-धिष्ठानमतो भूतालयो भवान् ॥
You are the substratum for beings such as the earth, water, etc., as well as for ghosts (Piśācas) and embodied beings, including those who have passed away. Therefore, You are Bhūtālayaḥ (The Abode of All Beings).


Shloka 363: Bhogadātā (भोगदाता)
भोगदाता दुःखसुखसाक्षा-त्कारप्रदत्वतः ॥ ३६३ ॥
He is Bhogadātā (The Bestower of Experience) because He grants the direct experience of both pleasure and pain.


Shloka 363: Bhrūmadhyagocaraḥ (भ्रूमध्यगोचरः)
अविभक्ते ध्रुवोर्मध्ये ध्येयो घूम-ध्यगो चरः ।
He is to be meditated upon in the indivisible, steady center between the eyebrows. Thus, He is Bhrūmadhyagocaraḥ (The One within the Range of the Mid-Brow).


Shloka 364-365: Mantraḥ (मन्त्रः), Mantrapatiḥ (मन्त्रपतिः) & Mantrī (मन्त्री)
मंत्राः सामर्थजूरूपा षडणैकाक्षरादयः ॥३६४॥ रहोविचाररूपाश्च राज्यभारोपयोगिनः । तद्रूपस्तदभिमानी देवता तस्य कारकः ॥ ३६५ ॥ तेन मन्त्रो मन्त्रपतिर्मन्त्री-त्यपि च गीयसे ।
Mantras are forms of power, such as six-syllabled or single-syllabled ones, or those useful for royal duties and consisting of secret deliberations. You are the very form of that, the deity who presides over and activates it. Therefore, You are sung as Mantraḥ (The Mantra Itself), Mantrapatiḥ (The Lord of Mantras), and Mantrī (The Possessor of Mantras).


Shloka 366: Madamattamanoramaḥ (मदमत्तमनोरमः)
मदः समाधिसंभूतध्येयोऽन्यविषयाग्रहः ॥ ३६६ ॥ रमसे तद्युते चित्ते मदमत्तमनोरमः ।
The divine intoxication (Mada) is born of deep meditation (Samādhi), is the object of contemplation, and is exclusive of other objects. You delight in a mind possessed by that intoxication. Thus, You are Madamattamanoramaḥ (Delighting in the Intoxicated Mind).


मेखलावान् मन्दगतिर्मतिमत्कमलेक्षणः ॥
महाबलो महावीर्यो महाप्राणो महामनाः ॥ १०६ ॥


Shloka 367: Mekhalāvān (मेखलावान्)
मेखलावान् रशनया मौञ्ज्या वा विलसत्कटिः ॥ ३६७॥
He whose waist shines with a girdle (Mekhalā) or a sacred Muñja-grass belt is Mekhalāvān (The One with a Girdle).


Shloka 368: Mandagatiḥ (मन्दगतिः)
येषां कापि गतिर्नास्ति मन्दानां ज्ञानकर्मणोः । अभावेन गतिस्तेषामपि मन्दगतिस्ततः ॥ ३६८ ॥
For those who are dull (Manda) and devoid of both knowledge and action, there is no progress. He becomes their progress (Gati); therefore, He is Mandagatiḥ (The Refuge of the Slow / The One with Slow Movement).


Shloka 369: Matimatkamaleksaṇaḥ (मतिमत्कमलेक्षणः)
यस्माज्ज्ञानयुता संप-त्तादृग् यस्य निरीक्षणम् । दृशैव दत्तधीश्रीको मतिमत्कम ले-क्षणः ॥ ३६९ ॥ प्राचा तु मतिमानिति भिन्ननामत्वेन पठितं, तत्कमलेक्षणनाम्न्नो मकारप्राये पठितुमयुक्तत्वादुपेक्ष्यम् ।
He whose mere glance bestows intelligence and prosperity is Matimatkamaleksaṇaḥ (The One with the Lotus-Eye of Intelligence). (The commentary notes that previous commentators read "Matimān" as a separate name, but disregards this as unsuitable.)








Shloka 370: Mahābalaḥ (महाबलः), Mahāvīryaḥ (महावीर्यः), Mahāprāṇaḥ (महाप्राणः) & Mahāmanāḥ (महामनाः)
बलवीर्यप्राणमनःसमष्ट्येकतनुर्भवान् । महाबलो महावीर्यो महाप्राणो महामनाः ॥ ३७० ॥
You are the collective embodiment of all strength, valor, life-force, and mind. Therefore, You are Mahābalaḥ (The Great in Strength), Mahāvīryaḥ (The Great in Valor), Mahāprāṇaḥ (The Great Life-Force), and Mahāmanāḥ (The Great of Mind).


Shloka 371: Yajñaḥ (यज्ञः) etc.
यज्ञ इत्यादिकं नामाष्टकं तु प्रकटार्थकं । रसः परम आनंदो रैसो वै स इति श्रुतेः ॥३७१॥
The eight names beginning with Yajña, etc., have clear meanings. He is the supreme essence (Rasa), the supreme Bliss, as stated in the Śruti: "He is indeed Rasa."


Conclusion of the Sixth Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ । 
गणेशनामखद्योते षष्ठं शतमबीभवत् ॥ ६०० ॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), composed by Bhāskara, 
also known as Agnicit, ends the sixth hundred of the Ganesha Sahasranama.


यज्ञो यज्ञपतिर्यज्ञगोप्ता यज्ञफलप्रदः ॥ 
यशस्करो योगगम्यो याज्ञिको याजकप्रियः ॥ १०७ ॥ 
रसो रसप्रियो रस्यो रञ्जको रावणार्चितः ॥ 
रक्षोरक्षाकरो रत्नगर्भो राज्यसुखप्रदः ॥ १०८ ॥ 


Shloka 372: Yajñaḥ (यज्ञः), Yajñapatiḥ (यज्ञपतिः), Yajñagoptā (यज्ञगोप्ता) & Yajñaphalapradaḥ (यज्ञफलप्रदः)
(From the context of verse 107, these names are clear. He is the sacrifice itself, the Lord of sacrifice, the protector of sacrifice, and the bestower of the fruits of sacrifice.)


Shloka 372: Yaśaskaraḥ (यशस्करः)
(He is the bestower of fame.)


Shloka 372: Yogagamyaḥ (योगगम्यः)
(He who is attainable through Yoga.)


Shloka 372: Yājñikaḥ (याज्ञिकः) & Yājakapriyaḥ (याजकप्रियः)
(He is the one devoted to sacrifice and is dear to the sacrificer.)


Shloka 372: Rasaḥ (रसः)
शांतादौ मधुरादौ वा रसे प्रीत्या रसप्रियः। रस्यते चर्व्यते प्राज्ञैर्भूयो भूयोऽनुभूयते ॥ ३७२ ॥ स रस्यस्तन्मनो-वृत्तिरञ्जनेनासि रञ्जकः ।
He is the essence (Rasa) itself, in peaceful or sweet forms. Thus, He is Rasaḥ (The Essence).


Shloka 372: Rasapriyaḥ (रसप्रियः)
He is dear to the essence, or He delights in the essence. Thus, He is Rasapriyaḥ (The Lover of Essence).


Shloka 372: Rasyaḥ (रस्यः)
He is relished and tasted again and again by the wise. Thus, He is Rasyaḥ (The One who is Relished).


Shloka 372: Rañjakaḥ (रञ्जकः)
You are the colorer (delighter) of the mind's modifications. Thus, He is Rañjakaḥ (The Delighter / One who Colors).


Shloka 373: Rāvaṇārcitaḥ (रावणार्चितः)
रावणैः शब्दनैर्वेदशास्त्रगीतादिपा-ठिभिः ॥ ३७३ ॥ दशास्येनापि सिद्धयर्थ पूजितो रावणार्चितः ।
He is worshipped by the roaring reciters of the Vedas, scriptures, songs, etc., and also by the ten-faced Rāvaṇa for attaining success. Thus, He is Rāvaṇārcitaḥ (The One Worshipped by Rāvaṇa).


Shloka 374: Rakṣorakṣākaraḥ (रक्षोरक्षाकरः)
रक्षांसि भस्मसात्कुर्वन् रक्षोरक्षाकरः स्मृतः ॥ ३७४॥
He who reduces the Rākṣasas (demons) to ashes is remembered as Rakṣorakṣākaraḥ (The Destroyer of Demons).


Shloka 375: Ratnagarbhaḥ (रत्नगर्भः)
रल-गर्भा मही तद्वान् रत्नगर्भस्तदाश्रये ।
The earth (Mahī) contains the womb of gems. He who possesses that (or is the support of that) is Ratnagarbhaḥ (The Womb of Jewels / One with Gems within).






Shloka 375: Rājyasukhapradaḥ (राज्यसुखप्रदः)
राज्यं तज्जन्मसौख्यं च ददद्राज्यसुखप्रदः ॥ ३७५ ॥
He who bestows kingdom and the happiness arising from it is Rājyasukhapradaḥ (The Bestower of Kingdom and Happiness).


लक्ष्यं लक्ष्यप्रदो लक्ष्यो लयस्थो लड्डुकप्रियः ॥ 
लानप्रियो लास्यपरो लोभकृल्लोकविश्रुतः ॥ १०९ ॥


Shloka 376: Lakṣyam (लक्ष्यम्)
तारेण धनुषा चित्तशरेण ब्रह्म वेधयेत् । इति श्रुत्या शरव्य-स्त्वं तल्लक्ष्यमसि योगिनाम् ॥ ३७६ ॥
You are the target (Lakṣya) of the Yogis, as stated in the Śruti: "One should pierce Brahman with the arrow of the mind, using the bow of the syllable Oṁ." Thus, He is Lakṣyam (The Target).


Shloka 377: Lakṣyapradaḥ (लक्ष्यप्रदः)
लक्ष्याणि चित्तस्थैर्या-र्थ लक्षसंख्यान्यदीदृशन् । ऋषयस्तानि निर्विघ्नं ददलक्ष्यप्रदो भवान् ॥ ३७७ ॥
You bestow upon the sages the various targets (spiritual goals), numbering in the hundreds of thousands, which are situated in the mind and are to be seen. Thus, You are Lakṣyapradaḥ (The Bestower of the Spiritual Target).


Shloka 377: Lakṣyaḥ (लक्ष्यः)
लक्ष्यो लक्षणया गम्यो महावाक्यगतैः पदैः ।
He is to be known (Lakṣyaḥ) through implication and through the words of the great Upaniṣadic statements (Mahāvākyas).


Shloka 378: Layasthaḥ (लयस्थः)
लयस्थः कल्पकालेऽपि तिष्ठंश्चित्तलयेऽपि वा ॥ ३७८ ॥
He remains existing even during the dissolution of the cosmos (Kalpa), or even when the mind is dissolved in deep meditation. Thus, He is Layasthaḥ (The One Abiding in Dissolution).


Shloka 379: Laḍḍukapriyaḥ (लड्डुकप्रियः)
लड्डुका वर्तुलाकाराः प्रियालतिलखाखसैः । रचिताः शर्करा-पक्कास्तत्प्रियो लड्डुकप्रियः ॥ ३७९ ॥
Laḍḍukas are round-shaped sweets made from Priyāla seeds, sesame seeds, and khakhasa (poppy seeds), cooked with sugar. He who is fond of them is Laḍḍukapriyaḥ (The Lover of Laḍḍukas).


Shloka 380: Lānapriyaḥ (लानप्रियः)
लानप्रियो गजावास-शालायां प्रीतिसंयुतः ।
He who is affectionate in the elephant stable (Lāna is a stable or a lion's lair) is Lānapriyaḥ (The One Fond of the Stable).


Shloka 380: Lāsyaparaḥ (लास्यपरः)
विलासार्हपरं धाम यस्य लास्यपरो हि सः ॥३८०॥
He whose supreme abode is worthy of divine play or dance is Lāsyaparaḥ (The One Devoted to Dance/Play).


Shloka 381: Lobhakṛt (लोभकृत्) & Lokaviśrutaḥ (लोकविश्रुतः)
ये लाभकारिणो लोकाः शीघ्रभक्तवरप्रदाः । तेषु श्रेष्ठतया ख्यातो लाभकृल्लोकविश्रुतः ॥ ३८१ ॥
He who is renowned (Viśruta) in the worlds (Loka) as the best among those who bestow benefits (Lābha) and quickly grant boons to devotees is Lobhakṛt (The Bestower of Gains) and Lokaviśrutaḥ (The One Renowned in the Worlds).


वरेण्यो वह्निवदनो वन्द्यो वेदान्तगोचरः ॥ 
विकर्ता विश्वतश्चक्षुर्विधाता विश्वतोमुखः ॥ ११० ॥


Shloka 382: Vareṇyaḥ (वरेण्यः)
वरेण्याख्यो गणपतेरद्वैतभजने रतः । कश्चिद्राजा तद-द्वैताद्वरेण्योऽस्युत्तमोऽपि वा ॥ ३८२ ॥
He is called Vareṇyaḥ (The Most Excellent / Worthy to be Chosen) – either as a king devoted to the non-dual worship of Gaṇapati, or as the supreme one due to his non-duality.


Shloka 383: Vahnivadanaḥ (वह्निवदनः)
यद्यद्भवन्तमुद्दिश्य स्रमिद्धेऽग्नौ हुतं हविः । तेनैव तृप्यसीत्यर्थात्त्वं वह्निवदनो मतः ॥३८३॥
Whatever oblation is offered into the blazing fire with you as its object, you are satisfied by that very act. Therefore, you are considered Vahnivadanaḥ (The One with Fire as His Face).


Shloka 384: Vandyaḥ (वन्द्यः)
वन्द्यो नम्योऽस्युपनिषद्गम्यो वेदान्तगोचरः ॥ ३८४ ॥
He is to be saluted (Vandyaḥ), bowed to, reached through the Upaniṣads, and is the object of Vedānta.




Shloka 384: Vedāntagocaraḥ (वेदान्तगोचरः)
Thus, He is Vedāntagocaraḥ (The One within the Range of Vedānta).


Shloka 384: Vikartā (विकर्ता)
विकर्ता त्वं हि षड्‌भावविकाराणां प्रवर्तकः ॥
You are the Vikartā (The Modifier / Separator / Creator) because you are the instigator of the six modifications of being (birth, existence, growth, change, decay, death).


Shloka 385: Viśvataścakṣuḥ (विश्वतश्चक्षुः)
यत्र क्वचन भक्तेन पत्रं पुष्पं फलं जलम् । दत्तं तत्रैव तत्पश्यन् धारयन् भक्षयन्नपि ॥ ३८५ ॥ कीर्त्यसे विश्वतश्चक्षुर्विधाता विश्वतोमुखः ।
You see everywhere, holding and even consuming whatever leaf, flower, fruit, or water is offered by a devotee, wherever it is offered. Therefore, you are proclaimed as Viśvataścakṣuḥ (The One with Eyes Everywhere).


Shloka 385: Vidhātā (विधाता)
Thus, He is Vidhātā (The Ordainer / Creator).


Shloka 385: Viśvatomukhaḥ (विश्वतोमुखः)
Thus, He is Viśvatomukhaḥ (The One with Faces Everywhere).


वामदेवो विश्वनेता वज्रिवज्रनिवारणः ॥ 
विश्वबन्धनविष्कम्भाधारो विश्वेश्वरप्रभुः ॥ १११ ॥


Shloka 386: Vāmadevaḥ (वामदेवः)
वामदेवो वल्गुतरं दीव्यसि द्योतसे यतः ॥ ३८६ ॥
You are called Vāmadeva (The Pleasant / Left-side Lord) because you shine most beautifully and radiate delight.


Shloka 386: Viśvanetā (विश्वनेता)
विश्वनेतासि जगतो विष्णोर्वा नायके यतः ।
You are the leader of the universe (Viśvanetā), or because you are the leader of Viṣṇu.




Shloka 387: Vajrivajranivāraṇaḥ (वज्रिवज्रनिवारणः)
इन्द्रवज्रस्तंभ-नेन वज्रिवज्रनिवारणः ॥३८७॥
He who stops (Nivāraṇa) the thunderbolt (Vajra) of Indra (Vajrin) is Vajrivajranivāraṇaḥ (The Checker of Indra's Thunderbolt).


Shloka 388: Viśvabandhanaviṣkambhādhāraḥ (विश्वबन्धनविष्कम्भाधारः)
विश्वनिर्मार्णपर्याप्तो देशो विष्कंभ उच्यते । विश्वबन्धनविष्कंभाधारस्तस्यापि धारणात् ॥ ३८८ ॥
The state (place) of universal projection (creation) is called Viṣkambha (the prop). He who is the support (Ādhāra) of that prop which binds the universe is Viśvabandhanaviṣkambhādhāraḥ (The Support of the Prop that Binds the Universe).


Shloka 388: Viśveśvaraprabhuḥ (विश्वेश्वरप्रभुः)
ब्रह्माण्डानां तदीशानांचेशो विश्वेश्वरप्रभुः ।
He is the lord (Prabhu) of the lord of the universe (Viśveśvara). Thus, He is Viśveśvaraprabhuḥ (The Lord of the Lord of the Universe).


शब्दब्रह्म शमप्राप्यः शंभुभक्तिगणेश्वरः ॥ 
शास्ता शिखाग्रनिलयः शरण्यः शिखरीश्वरः ॥ ११२ ॥


Shloka 389: Śabdabrahman (शब्दब्रह्म)
शब्दब्रह्म परां वाचमतीतो नादरूपधृक् ॥ ३८९॥
You are Śabdabrahman (The Absolute as Sound) because you transcend the supreme speech and assume the form of the primal sound (Nāda).


Shloka 389: Śamaprāpyaḥ (शमप्राप्यः)
शम-प्राप्यः शान्तिदान्ती जितैचित्तेन लभ्यसे ।
You are attainable (Prāpya) through the calmness (Śama) of a peaceful and self-controlled mind. Thus, He is Śamaprāpyaḥ (The One Attained through Inner Calm).


Shloka 390: Śambhvaṣṭagaṇeśvaraḥ (शंभ्वष्टगणेश्वरः)
शैवान् शाक्तान् गणांश्चेष्टे शंभुशक्तिगणेश्वरः ॥ ३९० ॥
He who rules over the Śaivas, Śāktas, and Gaṇas is Śambhvaṣṭagaṇeśvaraḥ (The Lord of Śambhu's Hosts).
(Commentary: Previous commentators read "Śambhuśaktir" as separate names, but this is rejected as inappropriate.)


Shloka 392: Śāstā (शास्ता)
शास्ता केरलदेशीया देवताऽस्ति तदात्मकः ॥ ३९२ ॥
You are of the nature of Śāstā, a deity of the Kerala region. Thus, He is Śāstā (The Ruler/Teacher).


Shloka 392: Śikhāgranilayaḥ (शिखाग्रनिलयः)
तस्याः शिखायामध्येयः परमात्मा व्यवस्थितः ॥ श्रुतिसिद्धोऽस्ति तद्रूपः शिखाग्रनिलयो भवान् ।
The Supreme Self is to be meditated upon at the tip of the crown (Śikhāgra). You exist in that very form revealed by the Śruti. Thus, You are Śikhāgranilayaḥ (The One Dwelling at the Crown's Tip).


Shloka 393: Śaraṇyaḥ (शरण्यः)
त्राता शरण्यः
You are the protector, the giver of refuge (Śaraṇyaḥ).


Shloka 393: Śikharīśvaraḥ (शिखरीश्वरः)
प्रालेयाचलात्मा शिखरीश्वरः ॥ ३९३ ॥
You are the snowy mountain (Śikhari) itself, or its lord (Īśvara). Thus, He is Śikharīśvaraḥ (The Lord of the Mountain Peak).


षड्ऋतुकुसुमस्रग्वी षडाधारः षडक्षरः ॥  
संसारवैद्यः सर्वज्ञः सर्वभेषजभेषजम् ॥ ११३ ॥


Shloka 394: Ṣaḍṛtukusumasragvī (षड्ऋतुकुसुमस्रग्वी)
जातीचम्पककुन्दादीन्येकै कर्तुभवान्यपि । षङ्गतुकुसुम-स्रग्वी सर्वदा युगपद्दधत् ॥ ३९४ ॥
He who wears a garland (Sragvī) of the flowers of all six seasons (Ṣaḍṛtu) – such as jasmine, champaka, kunda, and others – simultaneously and eternally, is Ṣaḍṛtukusumasragvī (The One with a Garland of Flowers of All Six Seasons).


Shloka 395: Ṣaḍādhāraḥ (षडाधारः)
मूलाधारे गणेशोऽस्ति चक्रषट्‌कं तदूर्ध्वतः । षण्णामाधारभूतोऽयं षडाधार इति स्मृतः ॥३९५॥
Gaṇeśa exists in the Mūlādhāra, and the six cakras are above that. He who is the support of these six is remembered as Ṣaḍādhāraḥ (The Support of the Six Cakras).
Shloka 395: Ṣaḍakṣaraḥ (षडक्षरः)
ङेवर्मयुग्वक्रतुण्डमत्ररूपः षडक्षरः ।
He who is of the nature of the six-syllabled Vakratuṇḍa mantra ("Gaṅ Gaṇapataye Namaḥ") is Ṣaḍakṣaraḥ (The Six-Syllabled One).
(Commentary: Previous explanations of these names are disregarded as unsupported by Tantra and Purāṇa.)


Shloka 396: Saṃsāravaidyaḥ (संसारवैद्यः)
संसारवैद्य इत्युक्तो भवरोगविनाशकः ॥३९६॥
He who is declared as Saṃsāravaidyaḥ (The Physician of the World) is the destroyer of the disease of worldly existence (Bhava).


Shloka 396: Sarvajñaḥ (सर्वज्ञः)
सर्वज्ञः सकलज्ञाता सुगतात्मा विनायकः ।
He is Sarvajñaḥ (The Omniscient) – the knower of all, the soul of the blessed, Vinaayaka.


Shloka 397: Sarvabheṣajabheṣajam (सर्वभेषजभेषजम्)
सर्वेषा-मपि रोगाणां दिव्यं पीयूषमौषधम् ॥ ३९७ ॥ तस्यापि नाशयन् दोषान् सर्वभेषजभेषजम् ॥
He is the divine nectar-like medicine for all diseases. And He destroys the defects of even that medicine. Therefore, He is Sarvabheṣajabheṣajam (The Medicine for All Medicines).


सृष्टिस्थितिलयक्रीडः सुरकुञ्जरभेदनः ॥ 
सिन्दूरितमहाकुम्भः सदसद्यक्तिदायकः ॥ ११४ ॥


Shloka 398: Sṛṣṭisthitilayakrīḍaḥ (सृष्टिस्थितिलयक्रीडः)
लीलया सर्वजगतां निर्मिति-स्थिति-संहृतीः ॥ ३९८ ॥ सृष्टि-स्थिति-लयक्रीडः कथ्यसे रचयन् क्षणात् ।
You create, maintain, and destroy the entire universe in a single moment, merely as play. Therefore, You are declared as Sṛṣṭisthitilayakrīḍaḥ (The One Whose Play is Creation, Preservation, and Dissolution).


Shloka 399: Surakuñjarabhedanaḥ (सुरकुञ्जरभेदनः)
दानवैरर्चि-तश्च त्वं सुरकुञ्जरभेदनः ॥ ३९९ ॥
You are worshipped by the demons (Dānavas), and you are the splitter of the divine elephant (of Indra). Thus, He is Surakuñjarabhedanaḥ (The Destroyer of the Divine Elephant).


Shloka 399: Sindūritamahākumbhaḥ (सिन्दूरितमहाकुम्भः)
सिन्दूरितमहाकुंभः सिन्दूरारुणमस्तकः ।
He whose great head (forehead) is adorned with red vermilion (Sindūra) is Sindūritamahākumbhaḥ (The One whose Great Forehead is Colored with Vermilion).


Shloka 400: Sadasadyaktidāyakaḥ (सदसद्यक्तिदायकः)
सद्ब्रह्मैव न सद्विश्वं तयोर्व्यक्तिः प्रकाश-नम् ॥४००॥ स्वभक्तेभ्यो ददासीति सदसयक्तिदायकः । साक्षी पश्यसि साक्षात्त्वं विश्वं चित्तानपेक्षया ॥ ४०१ ॥
You bestow upon Your devotees the faculty of discrimination between the Real (Sat – Brahman, which truly exists) and the Unreal (Asat – the universe, which does not ultimately exist). Thus, You are Sadasadyaktidāyakaḥ (The Bestower of the Knowledge of the Real and the Unreal). You are the witness who directly perceives the universe without depending on the mind.


साक्षी समुद्रमथनः स्वसंवेद्यः स्वदक्षिणः ॥ 
स्वतन्त्रः सत्यसङ्कल्पः सामगानरतः सुखी ॥ ११५ ॥


Shloka 402: Sākṣī (साक्षी) & Samudramathanaḥ (समुद्रमथनः)
समुद्रमथने देवैर्मन्थनारंभणेऽर्चितः । स्वसंवेद्यः स्वयं-ज्योतिः स्वयंशक्तः स्वदक्षिणः ॥४०२॥
He is the witness (Sākṣī) of everything. He was worshipped by the gods at the beginning of the churning of the ocean. Thus, He is Samudramathanaḥ (The Churner of the Ocean).


Shloka 402: Svasaṃvedyaḥ (स्वसंवेद्यः)
He is self-revealed (Svasaṃvedyaḥ), self-luminous (Svayaṃjyotiḥ).


Shloka 402: Svadakṣiṇaḥ (स्वदक्षिणः)
He is self-capable and also means "His own right" or "skilful" (Svadakṣiṇaḥ).


Shloka 403: Svatantraḥ (स्वतन्त्रः)
खतन्त्रः स्वयमेवासि नानातन्त्रात्मना यतः ।
You are indeed independent (Svatantraḥ), assuming the form of many Tantras.


Shloka 403: Satyasaṅkalpaḥ (सत्यसङ्कल्पः)
अमोघकल्पनात् सत्यसंकल्प इति गीयसे ॥४०३॥
Because your resolve never fails, you are praised as Satyasaṅkalpaḥ (The One of True Resolve).
Shloka 403: Sāmagānarataḥ (सामगानरतः)
सामप्रीतेः सदानंदात् सामगानरतः सुखी ।
He delights in the chanting of the Sāma Veda (Sāmagāna). Thus, He is Sāmagānarataḥ (The One Devoted to Chanting the Sāma Veda).


Shloka 403: Sukhī (सुखी)
He is ever-blissful (Sukhī).


हंसो हस्तिपिशाचीशो हवनं हव्यकव्यभुक् ॥ 
हव्यो हुतप्रियो हर्षो हृलेखामन्त्रमध्यगः ॥ ११६ ॥


Shloka 404: Haṁsaḥ (हंसः)
हंसो यतिविशेषात्मा यद्वा भास्करमूर्तिकः ॥ ४०४ ॥
He is Haṁsaḥ (The Swan) – either as the embodied soul of a particular sage (Yati) or as the one who has the form of the sun (Bhāskara).


Shloka 404-405: Hastipiśācīśaḥ (हस्तिपिशाचीशः)
एतत्पञ्चाक्षरस्यान्ते लिखेच्छब्दाद्विठो मनुः । तत्प्रतीकमुपादाय तस्येशो देवतोदिता ॥ ४०५ ॥ सोऽयं हस्तिपिशाचीशो नवा-र्णमनुदेवता।
He is the lord (Īśa) of the Hastipiśācī. This deity is the presiding deity of the nine-syllabled mantra, being the lord of that symbol.
(Commentary: Previous interpretations reading "Hastī" as a separate name are disregarded as ignorance of special Ganesha mantras.)


Shloka 406: Havanaṁ (हवनम्)
यागः प्रक्षेपयुग्हो-मस्तद्रूपो हवनं भवान् ॥४०६॥
The sacrifice (Yāga) endowed with offerings is of the nature of Homa. You, being that very form, are Havanaṁ (The Oblation/Sacrifice).


Shloka 406: Havyakavyabhuk (हव्यकव्यभुक्)
देवताः पितरश्चेति द्विरूपो हव्यकव्यभुक् ।
He who enjoys (Bhuk) both the offerings to the gods (Havya) and the offerings to the ancestors (Kavya) is Havyakavyabhuk (The Partaker of Both Divine and Ancestral Offerings).


Shloka 407: Havyaḥ (हव्यः)
हव्यो हविःस्वरूपत्वाद्धविर्ब्रह्मेति हि स्मृतेः ॥ ४०७ ॥
He is Havyaḥ (The Worthy Oblation) because He is of the nature of the oblation itself, as it is remembered: "The oblation is Brahman."


Shloka 408: Hutapriyaḥ (हुतप्रियः)
हुतं द्रव्यं प्रियं यस्या देवतायाः सवैभवान् । हवि-र्भक्षणजो मोदो हर्षः सोऽपि त्वदात्मकः ॥ ४०८ ॥
The offered material (Huta) is dear to the deity. The delight arising from consuming the oblation is joy (Harṣa), and that too is of your nature. Thus, He is Hutapriyaḥ (The One Fond of Offerings).


Shloka 408: Harṣaḥ (हर्षः)
He is Harṣaḥ (Joy) itself.


Shloka 410: Hṛllekhāmantramadhyagaḥ (हृलेखामन्त्रमध्यगः)
हृल्लेखार्थ-स्तन्त्रराजे वर्णितोव्याकुलाक्षरैः । व्योम्ना प्रकाशमानस्त्वं ग्रसमानस्त्वमग्निना ॥ ४०९ ॥ तयोर्विमर्श ईकारो बिन्दुना तन्निफालनम् । इति ह्रींकारवाच्योऽसि हृल्लेखामन्त्र-मध्यगः ॥ ४१० ॥
He is described in the Tantrarāja by the letters that are the meaning of Hṛllekhā. You shine with the sky (the vowel 'a') and consume with the fire (the vowel 'i'), their union is the 'Ī' vowel, and the Bindu (dot) is the removal of that. Thus, you are signified by the syllable 'Hrīṁ'. Therefore, He is Hṛllekhāmantramadhyagaḥ (The One Abiding within the Hṛllekhā Mantra).


क्षेत्राधिपः क्षमाभर्ता क्षमापरपरायणः ॥ 
क्षिप्रक्षेमकरः क्षेमानन्दः क्षोणीसुरद्रुमः ॥११७॥


Shloka 411: Kṣetrādhipaḥ (क्षेत्राधिपः)
प्रयागादेः शरीरादेर्वेशः क्षेत्राधिपो मतः ॥ ४११ ॥
He who is the lord of sacred places (Kṣetra) such as Prayāga, and also of the physical body, is considered Kṣetrādhipaḥ (The Lord of the Field).


Shloka 411: Kṣamābhartā (क्षमाभर्ता)
क्षमाभर्ता क्षितिं क्षान्तिमपि वा यो बिभर्ति सः ॥
He who supports (Bhartā) the earth (Kṣamā) or who embodies forbearance (Kṣamā) is Kṣamābhartā (The Supporter of the Earth / The Lord of Forbearance).


Shloka 411: Kṣamāparaparāyaṇaḥ (क्षमापरपरायणः)
क्षान्तिशील-मुनिप्राप्यः क्षमापरपरायणः ।
He who is devoted (Parāyaṇa) to forbearance (Kṣamā) and is attainable by sages of patient disposition is Kṣamāparaparāyaṇaḥ (The One Utterly Devoted to Forbearance).


Shloka 412: Kṣiprakṣemakaraḥ (क्षिप्रक्षेमकरः)
शीघ्रं सिद्धिं ददासीति क्षिप्रक्षेमकरो भवान् ॥ ४१२ ॥
You grant success quickly; therefore, You are Kṣiprakṣemakaraḥ (The One Who Swiftly Bestows Well-being).


Shloka 413: Kṣemānandaḥ (क्षेमानन्दः)
क्षेमं सांसारिकं सौख्य-मानन्दः पारमार्थिकः । उभयात्मा त्वमेवेति क्षेमानन्द इती-र्यसे ॥ ४१३ ॥
Kṣema is worldly happiness, and Ānanda is transcendental bliss. You possess both natures; therefore, You are proclaimed as Kṣemānandaḥ (The One Who is Both Worldly Well-being and Supreme Bliss).


Shloka 414: Kṣoṇīsuradrumaḥ (क्षोणीसुरद्रुमः)
कल्पवृक्ष इव क्षोण्यामिष्टान्सर्वान्प्रयच्छसि । धर्मादिदुःखभोगान्तानतः क्षोणीसुरद्रुमः ॥ ४१४ ॥
Like a wish-fulfilling tree (Kalpavṛkṣa) on earth (Kṣoṇī), you bestow all desirable things – from virtue (Dharma) to the end of suffering and enjoyment. Therefore, He is Kṣoṇīsuradrumaḥ (The Divine Tree on Earth).


धर्मप्रदोऽर्थदः कामदाता सौभाग्यवर्धनः ॥ 
विद्याप्रदो विभवदो भुक्तिमुक्तिफलप्रदः ॥११८॥


Shloka 415: Dharmapradaḥ (धर्मप्रदः), Arthadaḥ (अर्थदः) & Kāmadātā (कामदाता)
कल्पद्रुमत्वं विविनक्त्येकविंशतिनामभिः । धर्मप्रदोऽर्थदः कामदातेत्युक्तस्त्रिवर्गदः ॥४१५॥
The aspect of being a wish-fulfilling tree (Kalpadruma) is elaborately described through twenty-one names. He is called Dharmapradaḥ (The Bestower of Righteousness), Arthadaḥ (The Bestower of Wealth), and Kāmadātā (The Giver of Desires). Thus, He is the giver of the three goals of life (Trivarga – Dharma, Artha, Kāma).








Shloka 415: Saubhāgyavardhanaḥ (सौभाग्यवर्धनः)
स्त्रीणां जीवत्पतित्वादि दद-त्सौभाग्यवर्धनः ॥
He who bestows upon women the state of having a living husband, etc., and increases good fortune (Saubhāgya) is Saubhāgyavardhanaḥ (The Increaser of Good Fortune).


Shloka 416: Vidyāpradaḥ (विद्याप्रदः) & Vibhavadaḥ (विभवदः)
विद्याप्रदो विभवदो ज्ञानसंपत्तिदा-यकः ॥४१६॥
He is Vidyāpradaḥ (The Bestower of Knowledge) and Vibhavadaḥ (The Bestower of Prosperity/Opulence) – the giver of wisdom and wealth.


Shloka 416: Bhuktimuktiphala-pradaḥ (भुक्तिमुक्तिफलप्रदः)
भोगजीवन्मुक्तिदानाद्भुक्तिमुक्तिफलप्रदः ॥
He who grants enjoyment (Bhukti - worldly pleasure) and liberation (Mukti) gives the fruits of both. Thus, He is Bhuktimuktiphala-pradaḥ (The Bestower of the Fruits of both Enjoyment and Liberation).


अभिरूप्यकरो वीरश्रीप्रदो विजयप्रदः ॥ 
सर्ववश्यकरो गर्भदोषहा पुत्रपौत्रदः ॥ ११९ ॥


Shloka 417: Abhirūpyakaraḥ (अभिरूप्यकरः)
आढचैः सह दरिद्राणां मूर्खाणां पण्डितैः सह । साम्यं प्रदर्शयन् सभ्येष्वाभिरूप्यकरो मतः ॥ ४१७॥
He who brings about equality between the rich and the poor, and between the foolish and the wise, in the assemblies of the learned, is considered Abhirūpyakaraḥ (The One Who Bestows Elegance / Equality of Presence).


Shloka 418: Vīraśrīpradaḥ (वीरश्रीप्रदः)
समरे कान्दिशीकानां अपि वीरश्रियं ददत् । नामस्मृतवतां बीर-श्रीप्रदः कीर्त्यसे बुधैः ॥ ४१८ ॥
Even to those who are fleeing in battle, He bestows the glory of valor (Vīraśrī). Therefore, He is praised by the wise as Vīraśrīpradaḥ (The Bestower of the Glory of Valor). (Commentary: An alternative reading as separate name "Vīra" is disregarded as inconsistent with the context.)


Shloka 419: Vijayapradaḥ (विजयप्रदः)
वीर-श्रियं जयफलां ददानो विजयप्रदः ।
He who bestows the glory of valor, which is the fruit of victory, is Vijayapradaḥ (The Bestower of Victory).
Shloka 419: Sarvavaśyakaraḥ (सर्ववश्यकरः)
सर्व भक्तवशे कुर्वन् सर्ववश्यकरो भवान् ॥ ४१९ ॥
He who brings everything under the control of His devotees is Sarvavaśyakaraḥ (The One Who Brings Everything Under Control).


Shloka 419: Garbhadoṣahā (गर्भदोषहा)
बीजदोषान् स्रावपातौ नाशयन् गर्भदोषहा ॥
He who destroys defects of the seed (semen/ovum) and the miscarriage of the fetus is Garbhadoṣahā (The Remover of Womb Defects).


Shloka 420: Putrapautradaḥ (पुत्रपौत्रदः)
पुत्रः सूनुश्च दुहितातत्पौत्रान् दुहितुः सुतान् ॥ ४२० ॥ नप्तृन्प्रणप्तृन् दौहित्रान् ददानः पुत्रपौत्रदः ॥
He who bestows sons, grandsons (by sons), nephews (sons of daughter), great-grandsons, and further descendants is Putrapautradaḥ (The Bestower of Sons and Grandsons).


मेधादः कीर्तिदः शोकहारी दौर्भाग्यनाशनः ॥ 
प्रतिवादिमुखस्तम्भो रुष्टचित्तप्रसादनः ॥ १२० ॥


Shloka 421: Medhādaḥ (मेधादः)
धीर्धारणावती मेधा मेधादस्तत्प्रदायकः ॥ ४२१ ॥
The power of retention is Medhā (retentive memory). Medhādaḥ (The Bestower of Retentive Memory) is He who grants that.


Shloka 421: Kīrtidaḥ (कीर्तिदः)
लोके या सगुणख्यातिस्तत्प्रदानेन कीर्तिदः ।
Whatever reputation (Kīrti) or fame based on virtues exists in the world, He bestows it. Thus, He is Kīrtidaḥ (The Bestower of Fame).


Shloka 422: Śokahārī (शोकहारी)
शोकहारी ज्ञान-दानात्तरति शोकमात्मवत् ॥ ४२२ ॥
He is Śokahārī (The Remover of Sorrow) because through the gift of knowledge, one crosses beyond sorrow like the Self.




Shloka 422: Daurbhāgyanāśanaḥ (दौर्भाग्यनाशनः)
दुर्भगत्वापहारेण स्त्रीणां दौर्भाग्यनाशनः ।
By removing the state of misfortune (Durbhagatva), He is Daurbhāgyanāśanaḥ (The Destroyer of Ill-Fortune).


Shloka 423: Prativādimukhastambhaḥ (प्रतिवादिमुखस्तम्भः)
वाचं स्तभ्नासि दुष्टानां प्रतिकूलं प्रजल्प-ताम् ॥४२३॥ प्रतिवादिमुखस्तम्भः कथ्यसे स्मृतिमात्रतः ।
You paralyze the speech of the wicked who speak against You. He is called Prativādimukhastambhaḥ (The One Who Silences the Mouths of Opponents) simply by being remembered.


Shloka 424: Ruṣṭacittaprasādanaḥ (रुष्टचित्तप्रसादनः)
सेवकेषु क्रोधजुषां राज्ञां चित्तानि शोधयेत् ॥ ४२४ ॥ स्नेह-शालीनि कुर्वाणो रुष्टचित्तप्रसादनः ॥
He purifies the minds of angry kings towards their servants, making them affectionate. Thus, He is Ruṣṭacittaprasādanaḥ (The One Who Calms the Angry Mind).


पराभिचारशमनो दुःखभञ्जनकारकः ॥ 
लवस्तुटिः कला काष्ठा निमेषस्तत्परः क्षणः ॥ १२१ ॥


Shloka 425: Parābhicāraśamanaḥ (पराभिचारशमनः)
पराभिचारशमनो मारणाख्यस्य कर्मणः ॥ ४२५ ॥ स्वशत्रुभिः प्रणीतस्य निष्फलीकरणान्मतः ।
He is Parābhicāraśamanaḥ (The Appeaser of Enemy Sorcery) because He renders fruitless the killing rites performed by one's own enemies.


Shloka 426: Duḥkhabhañjanakārakaḥ (दुःखभञ्जनकारकः)
भञ्जयन् सर्व-दुःखानि दुःखभञ्जनकारकः ॥ ४२६ ॥
He who shatters (Bhañjan) all sorrows (Duḥkha) is Duḥkhabhañjanakārakaḥ (The Shatterer of Sorrows).


Shloka 427-429: Lavaḥ (लवः), Truṭiḥ (त्रुटिः), Kalā (कला), Kāṣṭhā (काष्ठा), Nimeṣaḥ (निमेषः), Tatparaḥ (तत्परः), Kṣaṇaḥ (क्षणः)
अथ कालस्वरूप-त्वमुक्तं विंशतिनामभिः । पद्मपत्रसहस्रस्य सूक्ष्मसूच्या भिवेधने ॥४२७॥ दले दले लवः कालसहस्रेण लवैस्रुटिः ॥४२८॥ त्रुटेः शतं तत्परस्तैर्निमेषस्त्रिशता भवेत् । अष्टादशनिमेषैः स्यात्काष्ठा तत्रिंशता कला ॥ ४२९ ॥
Now, the nature of Time is described through these names. When a fine needle pierces the thousand leaves of a lotus, the time taken to pierce each leaf is one Lava. One thousand Lavas make one Truṭi. One hundred Truṭis make one Tatpara. Thirty Tatparas make one Nimeṣa (blink of an eye). Eighteen Nimeṣas make one Kāṣṭhā. Thirty Kāṣṭhās make one Kalā.
Thus, He is Lavaḥ (The Instant), Truṭiḥ (The Fragment), Kalā (The Small Unit of Time), Kāṣṭhā (The Division of Time), Nimeṣaḥ (The Blink), Tatparaḥ (That Which is Beyond), and Kṣaṇaḥ (The Moment) – as the embodiment of these subtle units of time, or as their controller.


घटी मुहूर्त प्रहरो दिवानक्तमहर्निशम् ॥ 
पक्षो मासोऽयनं वर्ष युगं कल्पो महालयः ॥ १२२ ॥


Shloka 430: Ghaṭī (घटी), Muhūrtaḥ (मुहूर्तः), Praharaḥ (प्रहरः), Divā (दिवा), Nakta (नक्तम्), Ahar (अहः), Niśa (निशा), Ahar-niśam (अहर्निशम्)
तत्रिंशता क्षणस्ते षडू घटी ताभ्यां मुहूर्तकम् । प्रहर-स्तच्चतुष्कं तच्चतुष्कं नक्तकं दिवा ॥ ४३० ॥ अहर्निशं त्वहोरात्रः प्रहरैरष्टभिर्भवेत् ।
One Kṣaṇa (moment) consists of as many (atomic instants)… Thirty Kṣaṇas make one Ghaṭī. Two Ghaṭīs make one Muhūrta. Four Muhūrtas make one Prahara. Four Praharas make the night (Nakta) and four make the day (Divā). Eight Praharas make the full day-night cycle called Ahorātra (Ahar-niśam). Thus, He is Ghaṭī (The Time-Measure), Muhūrtaḥ (The Hour), Praharaḥ (The Watch), Divā (The Day), Nakta (The Night), Ahar (The Daytime), Niśa (The Nighttime), and Ahar-niśam (The Full Day).


Conclusion of the Seventh Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ । 
गणेशनामसाहने समाप्तं सप्तमं शतम् ॥ ७०० ॥
Thus, in the work (named "Sahana" or "Khadyota") composed by Bhāskara, also known as Agnicit (the son of or one who has kindled the fire), the seventh hundred of the Ganesha Sahasranama is completed.


Shloka 431: Pakṣaḥ (पक्षः), Māsaḥ (मासः), Ayanaṁ (अयनम्)
ते पञ्चदश पक्षस्तौ मासः षडयनं तु ते ॥ ४३१ ॥
Fifteen Ahar-niśas make one Pakṣa (fortnight). Two Pakṣas (thirty days) make one Māsa (month). Six months make one Ayana. Thus, He is Pakṣaḥ (The Fortnight), Māsaḥ (The Month), and Ayanaṁ (The Solstice/Half-Year).


Shloka 431: Varṣaṁ (वर्षम्)
ताभ्यां तु मानुषं वर्षम् ।
Two Ayanas make one human Varṣa (year). Thus, He is Varṣaṁ (The Year).
Shloka 432: Yugaṁ (युगम्)
तैः षष्ट्या त्रिशतीयुजा । दिव्यं द्वादशसाहस्रैर्दिव्यवषैश्चतुर्युगम् ॥ ४३२ ॥
That (human year) multiplied by 360 (or as described) makes one divine year. Twelve thousand divine years make one Catur-yuga (four ages). Thus, He is Yugaṁ (The Cosmic Age).


Shloka 432: Kalpaḥ (कल्पः)
चतुर्युगसहस्रेण कल्पो दैनन्दिनो लयः ।
One thousand Catur-yugas make one Kalpa, which is one day of Brahmā (followed by a night of equal length). Thus, He is Kalpaḥ (The Eon).


Shloka 433: Mahālayaḥ (महालयः)
द्वा-सप्ततिसहस्त्रैस्तैः स्यान्महाप्रलयो विधेः ॥ ४३३ ॥
Seventy-two such Kalpas constitute a Mahāpralaya (Great Dissolution) of Brahmā. Thus, He is Mahālayaḥ (The Great Dissolution).


राशिस्तारा तिथिर्योगो वारः करणमंशकम् ॥
लभं होरा कालचक्रं मेरुः सप्तर्षयो ध्रुवः ॥१२३॥


Shloka 434: Rāśiḥ (राशिः), Tārā (तारा), Tithiḥ (तिथिः)
राशिर्द्वादश मेषाद्याः कृत्तिकाद्याश्च तारकाः । त्वमेव च तिथिश्चान्द्री कला पञ्चदशात्मिका ॥ ४३४॥
You are the twelve Rāśis (zodiac signs) beginning with Meṣa (Aries). You are the Tārās (stars or lunar mansions) beginning with Kṛttikā. You are the Tithi (lunar day), which is the fifteen divisions (kalās) of the moon. Thus, He is Rāśiḥ (The Zodiac Sign), Tārā (The Star/Lunar Mansion), and Tithiḥ (The Lunar Day).


Shloka 435: Yogaḥ (योगः)
विष्कम्भप्रथमा योगास्त्वद्रूपाः सप्तविंशतिः । योगा अमृतसिद्ध्याद्या आनंदा-द्यास्त्वमेव च ॥ ४३५ ॥
The twenty-seven Yogas (astrological combinations), beginning with Viṣkambha, are of your form. The Yogas such as Amṛtasiddhi and Ānanda are also you alone. Thus, He is Yogaḥ (The Astrological Yoga).










Shloka 436: Vāraḥ (वारः), Karaṇam (करणम्), Aṁśakam (अंशकम्)
रव्यादिसप्तवारात्मा बवादि-करणात्मकः । चत्वारि प्रतिनक्षत्रमंशकानि त्वमेव हि ॥ ४३६ ॥
You are the essence of the seven Vāras (weekdays) beginning with Ravi (Sunday). You are the essence of the Karaṇas (half-tithis) beginning with Bava. You are indeed the four Aṁśakas (quarters) per Nakṣatra (lunar mansion). Thus, He is Vāraḥ (The Weekday), Karaṇam (The Half-Lunar Day), and Aṁśakam (The Division/Quarter).


Shloka 436: Lagnam (लग्नम्)
राशीनामुदयो लग्नम् ।
The rising (Udaya) of the Rāśis is Lagna (the ascendant). Thus, He is Lagnam (The Ascendant).


Shloka 436: Horā (होरा)
होरा लग्नार्थसंमिता ।
Horā is the half of a Rāśi (equal to Lagna). Thus, He is Horā (The Horā).


Shloka 437: Kālacakram (कालचक्रम्)
कालचक्रं शैशुमारं मेरुः सौवर्णपर्वतः ॥ ४३७ ॥
Kālacakra is the cosmic wheel of time, also known as Śaiśumāra (the dolphin-shaped constellation). Meru is the golden mountain. Thus, He is Kālacakram (The Wheel of Time).


Shloka 437: Meruḥ (मेरुः)
Thus, He is Meruḥ (Mount Meru).


Shloka 437: Saptarṣayaḥ (सप्तर्षयः)
सप्तर्षयः कश्यपाद्या औत्तानचरणिर्भुवः ।
The seven sages (Saptarṣi) beginning with Kaśyapa and the son of Uttānapāda (Dhruva) are… Thus, He is Saptarṣayaḥ (The Seven Sages).


Shloka 437: Dhruvaḥ (ध्रुवः)
Thus, He is Dhruvaḥ (The Pole Star).








राहुर्मन्दः कविर्जीवो बुधो भौमः शशी रविः ॥ 
कालः सृष्टिः स्थितिर्विश्वं स्थावरं जङ्गमं च यत् ॥ १२४ ॥


Shloka 438: Rāhuḥ (राहुः), Mandaḥ (मन्दः), Kaviḥ (कविः), Jīvaḥ (जीवः), Budhaḥ (बुधः), Bhoumaḥ (भौमः), Śaśī (शशी), Raviḥ (रविः)
राह्वादयो ग्रहा अष्टौ त्वद्रूपा एव नेतरे ॥ ४३८ ॥
The eight planets beginning with Rāhu (Rāhu, Saturn, Venus, Jupiter, Mercury, Mars, Moon, and Sun) are of your form alone, not of another. Thus, He is Rāhuḥ (The Eclipse Planet), Mandaḥ (Saturn), Kaviḥ (Venus), Jīvaḥ (Jupiter), Budhaḥ (Mercury), Bhoumaḥ (Mars), Śaśī (The Moon), and Raviḥ (The Sun).


Shloka 439: Kālaḥ (कालः)
कलनात् काल इत्युक्तो जगत्संहरणक्रिया ।
Because He measures (Kalana) or because He is the act of dissolving the universe, He is called Kālaḥ (Time/Death).


Shloka 439: Sṛṣṭiḥ (सृष्टिः)
सृष्टिः स्थितिश्च जगदुत्पादनप्राणनक्रिये ॥ ४३९ ॥
The act of producing the universe is Sṛṣṭi (Creation). Thus, He is Sṛṣṭiḥ (Creation).


Shloka 439: Sthitiḥ (स्थितिः)
The act of enlivening or maintaining the universe is Sthiti (Preservation). Thus, He is Sthitiḥ (Preservation).


Shloka 439: Viśvam (विश्वम्) & SthāvaraṂ JangamaṂ Ca Yat (स्थावरं जङ्गमं च यत्)
यत्स्थावरादि विश्वं तत्त्वमित्येकाभिधानकम् ।
Whatever universe (Viśvam) there is, consisting of the moving (Jangama) and non-moving (Sthāvara), that is you alone. This is the single designation. Thus, He is Viśvam (The Universe) and SthāvaraṂ JangamaṂ Ca Yat (All That is Moving and Non-Moving).












भूरापोऽग्निर्मरुद्योमाहकृतिः प्रकृतिः पुमान् ॥ 
ब्रह्मा विष्णुः शिवो रुद्र ईशः शक्तिः सदाशिवः ॥ १२५ ॥


Shloka 440: Bhūḥ (भूः), Apo (आपः), Agniḥ (अग्निः), Marut (मरुत्), Yoma (योम / व्योम)
भूरापोऽग्निर्मरुद्व्योमपञ्चभूतात्मको भवान् ॥ ४४०॥
You are the essence of the five great elements: earth (Bhūḥ), water (Apaḥ), fire (Agniḥ), wind (Marut), and ether (Yoma / Vyoma). Thus, He is Bhūḥ (The Earth), Apaḥ (The Waters), Agniḥ (The Fire), Marut (The Wind), and Yomā (The Sky/Ether).


Shloka 441: Ahakṛtiḥ (अहकृतिः / अहङ्कारः), Prakṛtiḥ (प्रकृतिः), Pumān (पुमान्)
अहङ्कतिरहङ्कारः प्रकृतिर्मूलकारणम् । पुमांश्च पञ्चविंशस्त्वं प्रकृत्याद्यैरसंहतः ॥४४१॥
You are Ahaṅkāra (the ego principle). You are Prakṛti (the primordial nature, the root cause). You are the twenty-fifth principle, Puruṣa (the cosmic being). Thus, He is Ahakṛtiḥ (The Ego-Principle), Prakṛtiḥ (Primordial Nature), and Pumān (The Cosmic Being).


Shloka 442: Brahmā (ब्रह्मा), Viṣṇuḥ (विष्णुः), Śivaḥ (शिवः), Rudraḥ (रुद्रः), Īśaḥ (ईशः), Śaktiḥ (शक्तिः), Sadāśivaḥ (सदाशिवः)
मन्त्रस्थब्रह्मविष्ण्वीशरुद्राः पादाः सदाशिवः । फलकस्तत्र कामेशः शिवस्तस्यांकगामिनी ॥ ४४२ ॥ शक्तिः कामेश्वरीत्येते सप्तापि भवदात्मकाः ।
In the mantra (structure), Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Īśa (Rudra), and Rudra are the feet; Sadāśiva is the board (phalaka); Kāmeśa is Śiva; and moving on his limb is the Śakti named Kāmeśvarī. These seven are all of your nature. Thus, He is Brahmā (The Creator), Viṣṇuḥ (The Preserver), Śivaḥ (The Auspicious One), Rudraḥ (The Howler), Īśaḥ (The Lord), Śaktiḥ (The Divine Energy), and Sadāśivaḥ (The Eternal Auspicious One).


त्रिदशाः पितरः सिद्धा यक्षा रक्षांसि किन्नराः ॥ 
साध्या विद्याधरा भूता मनुष्याः पशवः खगाः ॥ १२६ ॥


Shloka 443: Tridaśāḥ (त्रिदशाः), Pitaraḥ (पितरः), Siddhāḥ (सिद्धाः), Yakṣāḥ (यक्षाः), Rākṣāṁsi (रक्षांसि), Kinnarāḥ (किन्नराः), Sādhyāḥ (साध्याः), Vidyādharāḥ (विद्याधराः), Bhūtāḥ (भूताः), Manuṣyāḥ (मनुष्याः), Paśavaḥ (पशवः), Khagāḥ (खगाः)
त्रिदशा दिकशैलान्ता अर्थास्त्वं तिथिभिर्मिताः ॥४४३॥
You are the thirty-three gods (Tridaśāḥ), the ancestors (Pitaraḥ), the perfected beings (Siddhāḥ), the nature spirits (Yakṣāḥ), the demons (Rākṣāṁsi), the celestial musicians (Kinnarāḥ), the divine beings (Sādhyāḥ), the knowledge-holders (Vidyādharāḥ), the ghosts (Bhūtāḥ), the humans (Manuṣyāḥ), the animals (Paśavaḥ), and the birds (Khagāḥ). Thus, He is Tridaśāḥ (The Thirty-three Gods), Pitaraḥ (The Ancestors), Siddhāḥ (The Perfected Ones), Yakṣāḥ (The Nature Spirits), Rākṣāṁsi (The Demons), Kinnarāḥ (The Celestial Musicians), Sādhyāḥ (The Divine Beings), Vidyādharāḥ (The Knowledge-Holders), Bhūtāḥ (The Ghosts/Beings), Manuṣyāḥ (The Humans), Paśavaḥ (The Animals), and Khagāḥ (The Birds).


Shloka 444: Bhūtam (भूतम्)
भवान् भूतमतीतं च भव्यं क्षेमं च भावि च । भवोद्भवः शिवोत्पन्नः संसारप्रभवोऽथवा ॥ ४४४ ॥
You are the past (Bhūtam). Also the future, the present, etc. Thus, He is Bhūtam (The Past / The Elements). (The verse continues describing Him as the origin of existence, born of Śiva, or the source of the world.)


समुद्राः सरितः शैला भूतं भव्यं भवोद्भवः ॥ 
साङ्ख्यं पातञ्जलं योगः पुराणानि श्रुतिः स्मृतिः ॥ १२७ ॥


Shloka 444: Samudrāḥ (समुद्राः), Saritaḥ (सरितः), Śailāḥ (शैलाः), Bhūtam (भूतम्), Bhavyam (भव्यम्), Bhavodbhavaḥ (भवोद्भवः)
(From the conclusion of the previous verse and beginning of this verse) You are the oceans (Samudrāḥ), rivers (Saritaḥ), mountains (Śailāḥ), the past (Bhūtam), the future (Bhavyam), and the origin of existence (Bhavodbhavaḥ). Thus, He is Samudrāḥ (The Oceans), Saritaḥ (The Rivers), Śailāḥ (The Mountains), Bhūtam (The Past/Elements), Bhavyam (The Future), and Bhavodbhavaḥ (The Origin of Existence).


Shloka 445-446: Sāṅkhyaṁ (साङ्ख्यम्), Pātañjalaṁ (पातञ्जलम्), Yogaḥ (योगः), Purāṇāni (पुराणानि), Śrutiḥ (श्रुतिः), Smṛtiḥ (स्मृतिः)
अथ सांख्यादयो विद्यास्त्वद्रूपाः सप्तविंशतिः । साङ्ख्यं कपिलमुन्युक्तं शास्त्रं पातञ्जलं पुनः ॥ ४४५ ॥ सामसूत्र-निदानाख्यं योगः फणिपतीरितः । ब्राह्मादीनि पुराणानि श्रुतयो बहृचादयः ॥ ४४६ ॥ मन्वाद्याः श्रुतयो वेदाङ्गानि कल्पादिकानि षटू ॥
Now, the twenty-seven branches of knowledge beginning with Sāṅkhya are of your form. Sāṅkhya is the system taught by Kapila. Pātañjala is the system taught by Patañjali. Yoga is the system (Sāmasūtra Nidāna) proclaimed by Śeṣa (Phāṇipati). The Purāṇas beginning with Brahmā (or Brahmāṇḍa). The Śrutis (Vedas) beginning with Ṛgveda. The Smṛtis beginning with Manu. 
The six Vedāṅgas beginning with Kalpa. Thus, He is Sāṅkhyaṁ (The Sāṅkhya Philosophy), Pātañjalaṁ (The Yoga Philosophy of Patañjali), Yogaḥ (The Path of Union), Purāṇāni (The Purāṇas), Śrutiḥ (The Vedas), and Smṛtiḥ (The Traditional Laws).


वेदाङ्गानि सदाचारो मीमांसान्यायविस्तरः ॥ 
आयुर्वेदो धनुर्वेदो गान्धर्वं काव्यनाटकम् ॥ १२८ ॥ 
वैखानसं भागवतं सात्वतं पाञ्चरात्रकम् ॥ 
शैवं पाशुपतं कालामुखं भैरवशासनम् ॥ १२९ ॥


Shloka 447: Vedāṅgāni (वेदाङ्गानि), Sadācāraḥ (सदाचारः)
वेदाङ्गानि सदाचारो... वेदाङ्गानि (the six limbs of the Vedas) and Sadācāra (righteous conduct). He is Vedāṅgāni (The Auxiliary Disciplines of the Vedas) and Sadācāraḥ (Righteous Conduct).


Shloka 448: Mīmāṁsā (मीमांसा), Nyāyavistaraḥ (न्यायविस्तरः)
मीमांसान्यायविस्तरः । मीमांसा षोडशाध्यायी संकर्षसहिता सती । त्रिचतुर्लक्षणे भक्ति-ब्रह्ममीमांसके अपि ॥४४८॥ कणाद गौतममुनिप्रणीतो न्याय-विस्तरः ॥
Mīmāṁsā is the sixteen-chapter exegesis (along with the Sānkarṣa Kāṇḍa). The detailed system of Nyāya (logic) was propounded by the sages Kaṇāda and Gautama. Thus, He is Mīmāṁsā (The Philosophical Inquiry) and Nyāyavistaraḥ (The Expanded System of Logic).


Shloka 449: Āyurvedaḥ (आयुर्वेदः), Dhanurvedaḥ (धनुर्वेदः), Gāndharvam (गान्धर्वम्)
ऋग्यजुःसामवेदानामुपवेदा अपि क्रमात् ॥४४९॥ आयुर्वेदधनुर्वेद‌गांधर्वास्तन्मयो भवान् ॥
The Upavedas (subsidiary Vedas) of the Ṛg, Yajus, and Sāma Vedas are respectively Āyurveda (medicine), Dhanurveda (archery), and Gāndharva (music and arts). You are the embodiment of these. Thus, He is Āyurvedaḥ (The Science of Medicine), Dhanurvedaḥ (The Science of Archery), and Gāndharvam (The Art of Music).


Shloka 450: Kāvyanāṭakam (काव्यनाटकम्)
काव्यनाटक-शब्दाभ्यां श्रव्यं दृश्यं च गृह्यते ॥ ४५०॥
By the terms Kāvya (poetry) and Nāṭaka (drama), that which is audible (Śravya) and that which is visible (Dṛśya) are understood. Thus, He is Kāvyanāṭakam (Poetry and Drama).


Shloka 451: Vaikhānasam (वैखानसं), Bhāgavatam (भागवतम्), Sātvatam (सात्वतम्), Pāñcarātrakam (पाञ्चरात्रकम्)
विष्णोर्वैखानसादीनि... पृथकू चत्वारि चत्वारि तन्त्राण्येतत्त्वदात्मकम् ॥ ४५२ ॥
The four distinct Tantras of Viṣṇu are Vaikhānasa, Bhāgavata, Sātvatam, and Pāñcarātram. You are their essence. Thus, He is Vaikhānasam (The Vaikhānasa System), Bhāgavatam (The Bhāgavata System), Sātvatam (The Sātvata System), and Pāñcarātrakam (The Pāñcarātra System).


Shloka 452: Śaivam (शैवम्), Pāśupatam (पाशुपतम्), Kālāmukham (कालामुखम्), Bhairavasāsanam (भैरवशासनम्)
शैवानि तु महेशितुः । पृथकू चत्वारि चत्वारि तन्त्राण्येतत्त्वदात्मकम् ॥ ४५२ ॥
The four distinct Tantras of Maheśvara (Śiva) are Śaiva, Pāśupata, Kālāmukha, and Bhairava. You are their essence. Thus, He is Śaivam (The Śaiva System), Pāśupatam (The Pāśupata System), Kālāmukham (The Kālāmukha System), and Bhairavasāsanam (The Bhairava Doctrine).


शाक्तं वैनायकं सौरं जैनमार्हतसंहिता ॥ 
सदसद्यक्तमव्यक्तं सचेतनमचेतनम् ॥ १३० ॥


Shloka 453: Śāktaṁ (शाक्तम्), Vaināyakaṁ (वैनायकम्), Sauraṁ (सौरम्), Jainam (जैनम्), Ārhata-Saṁhitā (आर्हतसंहिता)
शाक्तादित्रितयं शक्तेर्गणेशस्य विवस्वतः । तन्त्रे द्वे नास्तिकोन्नीते अपि जैनार्हते तथा ॥४५३॥
The trio beginning with Śākta (i.e., Śākta, Vaināyaka, and Saura) belong to Śakti, Gaṇeśa, and the Sun respectively. Similarly, the two tantras upholding atheism are the Jaina and the Ārhata (Buddhist) scriptures. Thus, He is Śāktaṁ (The Śākta System), Vaināyakaṁ (The Vaināyaka/Gāṇapatya System), Sauraṁ (The Saurya System), Jainam (The Jaina Doctrine), and Ārhata-Saṁhitā (The Buddhist Scripture).


Shloka 454: Sad-Asadyaktam (सदसद्यक्तम्)
कार्याणां करण-स्थान्तरवस्थानं सदात्मता । अकारणेनावस्थानं तदभावेऽ-सदात्मता ॥४५४॥
The state of being established in the cause (Karaṇa) of effects is the nature of the Real (Sat). The state of being without a cause, or its absence, is the nature of the Unreal (Asat). Thus, He is the bestower of the knowledge of both – Sad-Asadyaktam (The Bestower of the Real and the Unreal).






Shloka 454: Avyaktam (अव्यक्तम्)
सर्वकार्यात्मना व्यक्तमव्यक्तं कारणा-त्मना ॥ ४५५ ॥
He is the Unmanifest (Avyaktam) as the cause, in the form of the primordial Prakṛti.


Shloka 455: Sacetanam (सचेतनम्) & Acetanam (अचेतनम्)
सचेतनं प्राणिमात्रं व्योमादिकमचेतनम् ॥
All living beings (Prāṇimātra) constitute the sentient (Sacetanam). The sky, etc., constitute the insentient (Acetanam). Thus, He is Sacetanam (The Sentient / Conscious) and Acetanam (The Insentient / Unconscious).


बन्धो मोक्षः सुखं भोगोऽयोगः सत्यमणुर्महान्। 
स्वस्ति हुं फट् स्वधा स्वाहा श्रौषड्वौषड्वषण्णमः ॥ १३१ ॥


Shloka 456: Bandhaḥ (बन्धः)
बन्ध आत्मन्यनात्मत्वभ्रमो नात्मनि चात्मता । एतत्सर्व भवानेव भगवन् परिकीर्तितः ॥ ४५६ ॥
The illusion of non-Self in the Self, and the notion of Self in the non-Self – this is bondage (Bandhaḥ). 
O Lord, all of this is proclaimed as you alone. Thus, He is Bandhaḥ (Bondage).


Conclusion of the Eighth Hundred
इति भारत्युपाख्यस्य भास्कराग्निचितः कृतौ । 
गणेशनामसाहस्त्रखद्योते शतमष्टमम् ॥ ८०० ॥
Thus, in the work named "Khadyota" (The Firefly), which is a light on the thousand names of Gaṇeśa, composed by Bhāskara, also known as Agnicit, the eighth hundred is completed.


Shloka 456: Mokṣaḥ (मोक्षः)
(The destruction of that illusion is liberation. He is that as well.) Thus, He is Mokṣaḥ (Liberation).


Shloka 457: Sukham (सुखम्) & Bhogaḥ (भोगः)
क्षस्त्वविद्याविध्वंसस्ततो यदवशिष्यते । तत्सुखं प्रत्य-गानन्दो भोगः सोऽनुभवात्मकः ॥ ४५७ ॥
What remains after the destruction of ignorance is Sukha (Bliss). That inner Ānanda is Bhoga (Experience), which is of the nature of direct perception. Thus, He is Sukham (Bliss) and Bhogaḥ (Experience/Enjoyment).




Shloka 457: Ayogaḥ (अयोगः)
अयोगः संग-रहितः ।
He who is free from all attachment, or the state of non-union (disconnection), is Ayogaḥ. Thus, He is Ayogaḥ (Non-Attachment/Disunion).


Shloka 458: Satyam (सत्यम्)
तद्धि सत्यमबाधितम् ।
That which is uncontradicted is Satyam (Truth). Thus, He is Satyam (Truth).


Shloka 458: Aṇuḥ (अणुः)
स एवाणुर्मनःषष्ठेन्द्रियागोचरतावशात् ॥ ४५८ ॥
He alone is the Aṇuḥ (Minute/Atom) because He is beyond the reach of the mind and the five senses (and the sixth, the intellect). Thus, He is Aṇuḥ (The Subtle/Atom).


Shloka 458: Mahān (महान्)
ततोऽधिकस्य सौख्यस्याभावादेव स वै महान् ॥
Because there is no greater bliss than Him, He is indeed Mahān (The Great). 
Thus, He is Mahān (The Great).


Shloka 459: Svasti (स्वस्ति)
तदेव सम्यगस्तीति स्वस्ति नास्ति ततः परम् ॥ ४५९ ॥
That which truly exists is Svasti (Well-being/Auspiciousness). Nothing exists beyond it. Thus, He is Svasti (Auspiciousness).


Shloka 460: Huṁ (हुं) & Phaṭ (फट्)
अन्यस्य दूरीकरणात्तदेव ब्रह्म हुं मतम् । दूरो-त्सारणमात्रं न किंतु नाशोऽपि तेन फटू ॥ ४६० ॥
That very Brahman is considered Huṁ, because it repels everything else. It is not merely the act of keeping away, but also the destruction; therefore, Phaṭ. Thus, He is Huṁ (The Repelling Seed) and Phaṭ (The Destroying Mantra).


Shloka 461: Svadhā (स्वधा), Svāhā (स्वाहा), Śrauṣaṭ (श्रौषट्), Vauṣaṭ (वौषट्), Vaṣaṭ (वषट्), Namaḥ (नमः)
खधेति पितृदैवत्यं स्वाहेत्यन्नन्यादिदैवतम् । औषडित्यादयस्तत्तच्छब्दयुक्रर्मलक्षकाः ॥ ४६१ ॥
Svadhā is the invocation for the ancestors (Pitṛs). Svāhā is for Agni and other deities. Śrauṣaṭ, Vauṣaṭ, Vaṣaṭ, and Namaḥ are ritual utterances that characterize various Vedic actions (karma). Thus, He is Svadhā (The Offering to Ancestors), Svāhā (The Offering to Fire), Śrauṣaṭ (The Mantra for Praise), Vauṣaṭ (The Mantra for Pouring), Vaṣaṭ (The Mantra of Offering), and Namaḥ (The Mantra of Salutation).


Shloka 462: Mahāphale (महाफले) / Summary
सर्वेषां कर्मणामिष्टसिद्ध्यर्थत्वान्महाफले । सुखे लब्धे तु तत्रैव तत्सर्व पर्यवस्यति ॥ ४६२ ॥ तेन कर्मापि सकलं भवेदेकात्मकं सुखम् ॥
Since all rituals aim to attain desired results, their culmination is the great fruit (Mahāphale). When bliss (Sukham) is attained, everything resolves into that alone. Thus, all action (Karma) becomes one with that bliss. He is that ultimate fruit. Thus, He is Mahāphale (The Great Fruit).


ज्ञानं विज्ञानमानन्दो बोधः संविच्छमो यमः ॥ 
एक एकाक्षराधार एकाक्षरपरायणः ॥ १३२ ॥


Shloka 463: Jñānam (ज्ञानम्) & Vijñānam (विज्ञानम्)
मोक्षे धीर्ज्ञानमन्यत्र विज्ञानं तद्वयं भवान् ॥ ४६३ ॥
The knowledge that leads to liberation is Jñānam. Knowledge of everything else (worldly matters) is Vijñānam. You are both of these. Thus, He is Jñānam (Supreme Knowledge) and Vijñānam (Applied Knowledge).


Shloka 464: Ānandaḥ (आनन्दः) & Bodhaḥ (बोधः)
त्वमेव प्रत्यगानन्दः प्रत्यग्बोधस्त्वमेव हि । योगिभिः सेव्यते संविद्वाह्यवृत्तिनिरासिका ॥ ४६४ ॥
You indeed are inner Ānanda (Bliss). You indeed are inner Bodha (Awareness/Consciousness). That which is served by Yogis, the Consciousness (Saṃvit) that removes external mental states, is you. Thus, He is Ānandaḥ (Bliss) and Bodhaḥ (Awakening/Consciousness).


Shloka 464: Saṃvit (संवित्)
Thus, He is Saṃvit (Pure Consciousness).


Shloka 465: Śamaḥ (शमः) & Yamaḥ (यमः)
सर्वस्य शमनात्सर्वोपरमात्त्वं शमो यमः ॥ ४६५ ॥
Because you are the allayer (Śamana) of everything and the cessation (Uparama) of everything, you are Śamaḥ (Tranquility/Control of Mind) and Yamaḥ (Restraint/Regulation). Thus, He is Śamaḥ (Inner Calmness) and Yamaḥ (Self-Control).


Shloka 466: Ekaḥ (एकः)
एक एवाद्वितीयस्त्वं भेदत्रैविध्यशून्यतः ॥ ४६६॥
You alone are One (Ekaḥ) without a second, devoid of the three kinds of differences (variations within itself, variations from others, variations of its own nature). Thus, He is Ekaḥ (The One).


Shloka 466: Ekākṣarādhāraḥ (एकाक्षराधारः)
गंबीजान्तस्थितेरेकाक्षराधार इति स्मृतः ॥
Because you are established in the seed-syllable 'Gaṁ', you are remembered as Ekākṣarādhāraḥ (The Support of the Single Syllable).


Shloka 466: Ekākṣaraparāyaṇaḥ (एकाक्षरपरायणः)
ॐमित्ये-काक्षरस्थत्वादेकाक्षरपरायणः ॥
Because you are established in the single syllable 'Oṁ' (which is of the nature of Brahman), you are Ekākṣaraparāyaṇaḥ (The One Devoted to / Abiding as the Single Syllable).


एकाग्रधीरेकवीर एकानेकस्वरूपधृक् ॥ 
द्विरूपो द्विभुजो द्यक्षो द्विरदो द्वीपरक्षकः ॥ १३३ ॥


Shloka 467: Ekāgrasdhīḥ (एकाग्रधीः)
एकाग्रधीः स्वात्ममात्रप्रवणापि मतिर्भवान् ॥ ४६७ ॥
You are Ekāgrasdhīḥ (The One of Unwavering Intellect) — the intellect that is single-pointed and inclined only towards the Self.


Shloka 468: Ekavīraḥ (एकवीरः)
अहमि प्रलयं कुर्वन्निदमः प्रतियोगिनी । पराक्रमपरोऽस्येक-वीरस्त्वत्तो न चापरः ॥ ४६८ ॥
Destroying the ego (Aham) and its counterpart "this" (Idam), you are the supreme hero of valor. There is no other hero like you. Thus, He is Ekavīraḥ (The Sole Hero).


Shloka 469: Ekānekasvarūpadhṛk (एकानेकस्वरूपधृक्)
प्रतिभूतमवस्थानादेका-नेकस्वरूपधृक् । एकधा बहुधा चैव दृश्यसे जलचन्द्रवत् ॥ ४६९ ॥
Because you reside in every living being, you assume both one and many forms. You are seen as one and as many, like the moon reflected in water. Thus, He is Ekānekasvarūpadhṛk (The One Who Assumes Both Single and Multiple Forms).


Shloka 470: Dvirūpaḥ (द्विरूपः)
द्वे ब्रह्मणी इति श्रुत्या द्विरूपोऽस्यपरं परम् । त्वमेव द्विभुजो यक्षो द्विरदः प्रकटार्थकः ॥ ४७० ॥
According to the Śruti, "There are two Brahmans" (the Absolute and the conditioned). Therefore, you possess two forms (the transcendent and the immanent). Thus, He is Dvirūpaḥ (The Two-Formed One).


Shloka 470: Dvibhujaḥ (द्विभुजः)
Thus, He is Dvibhujaḥ (The Two-Armed One), as the meaning is clear.


Shloka 470: Dvakṣaḥ (द्व्यक्षः / द्व्यक्षः)
Thus, He is Dvakṣaḥ (The Two-Eyed One).


Shloka 470: Dviradaḥ (द्विरदः)
Thus, He is Dviradaḥ (The Two-Tusked One / Elephant).


Shloka 470: Dvīparakṣakaḥ (द्वीपरक्षकः)
सप्त-द्वीपाधिपत्यस्य प्रदानाद्वीपरक्षकः ॥
Because you bestow sovereignty over the seven continents (Dvīpa), you are their protector. Thus, He is Dvīparakṣakaḥ (The Protector of the Continents).


द्वैमातुरो द्विवदनो द्वन्द्वातीतो द्वयातिगः ॥ 
त्रिधामा त्रिकरस्त्रेतात्रिवर्गफलदायकः ॥१३४॥


Shloka 471: Dvaimāturaḥ (द्वैमातुरः)
द्वैमातुरोऽस्युमागङ्गे मातरौ तव विश्श्रुते ॥ ४७१ ॥
You are celebrated as Dvaimāturaḥ (The One with Two Mothers) — Umā (Pārvatī) and Gaṅgā are your two mothers.


Shloka 471: Dvivadanaḥ (द्विवदनः)
तवैव मूर्तिरग्न्याख्या तेन द्विवदनो भवान् ॥
The form called Agni (Fire) is indeed yours; therefore, you are Dvivadanaḥ (The Two-Faced One).








Shloka 472: Dvandvātītaḥ (द्वन्द्वातीतः)
शीतोष्णादीन्यति-क्रान्तो द्वन्द्वातीत इति श्रुतः ॥ ४७२ ॥
Having transcended dualities such as cold and heat, you are known as Dvandvātītaḥ (The One Beyond Duality).


Shloka 472: Dvayātigaḥ (द्वयातिगः)
रजस्तमश्चा-तिलङ्घय वर्तमानो द्वयातिगः ॥
Transcending the two qualities of Rajas and Tamas (and also Sattva), you are Dvayātigaḥ (The One Who Has Surpassed the Pair of Opposites).


Shloka 473: Tridhāmā (त्रिधामा)
त्रिधामा सूर्यचन्द्राग्नितेज-स्त्रितयमूर्तिमान् ॥ ४७३ ॥
You are Tridhāmā (The Possessor of Three Luminaries) — embodied as the three forms of radiance: the sun, the moon, and fire.


Shloka 473: Trikaraḥ (त्रिकरः)
त्रयाणामपि लोकानां करणात्रि-करो भवान् ॥
Because you are the agent (Kara) or the hand (Kara) for the three worlds, you are Trikaraḥ (The Three-Handed One).


Shloka 474: Tretātrivargaphaladāyakaḥ (त्रेतात्रिवर्गफलदायकः)
नर्याद्यग्नित्रया काङ्गधर्मकामार्थकर्मणाम् ॥४७४॥ फलप्रद इति त्रेतात्रिवर्गफलदायकः ॥
You bestow the fruits of the three goals of life (Trivarga: Dharma, Artha, Kāma) in the Tretā Yuga (and by extension, all ages). Thus, He is Tretātrivargaphaladāyakaḥ (The Bestower of the Fruits of the Three Goals in the Tretā Age).


त्रिगुणात्मा त्रिलोकादिस्त्रिशक्तीशस्त्रिलोचनः ॥ 
चतुर्बाहुश्चतुर्दन्तश्चतुरात्मा चतुर्मुखः ॥१३५॥


Shloka 475: Trigunātmā (त्रिगुणात्मा)
गुणत्रयवती मूलप्रकृतिस्त्रिगुणात्मता ॥ ४७५ ॥ तस्या धृतिस्त्वमेवेति त्रिगुणात्मेति गीयसे ॥
The primordial nature (Mūlaprakṛti) possessing the three qualities (Guṇas) is the state of being Trigunātmā. You are the sustainer of that; therefore, you are sung as Trigunātmā (The One Whose Self is the Three Qualities).
Shloka 476: Trilokādiḥ (त्रिलोकादिः)
त्रयाणामपि लोकानां त्रिलोकादिश्च कारणम् ॥ ४७६ ॥
The creator (Ādi) and cause of the three worlds: thus, He is Trilokādiḥ (The Origin of the Three Worlds).


Shloka 477: Triśaktīśaḥ (त्रिशक्तीशः)
रमालज्जानङ्गयोग स्त्रि-शक्तिः कथ्यते मनुः । प्राभावौत्साहमान्च्या वा त्रिशक्तीशस्तदीश्वरः ॥ ४७७ ॥
The triad of powers (Triśakti) is spoken of as: Ramā (Lakṣmī), Lajjā (Modesty), and Aṅgayoga? or as power of manifestation (Prabhāva), enthusiasm (Utsāha), and wisdom? He is the lord (Īśa) of those three powers. Thus, He is Triśaktīśaḥ (The Lord of the Three Powers).


Shloka 477: Trilocanaḥ (त्रिलोचनः)
त्रिलोचनः... इति स्फुटम् ॥
He who has three eyes: thus, Trilocanaḥ (The Three-Eyed One) is clear.


Shloka 478: Caturbāhuḥ (चतुर्बाहुः)
चतुर्बाहुः... इति स्फुटम् ॥
He who has four arms: thus, Caturbāhuḥ (The Four-Armed One) is clear.


Shloka 478: Caturdantaḥ (चतुर्दन्तः)
चतुर्दन्त... इति स्फुटम् ॥
He who has four tusks: thus, Caturdantaḥ (The Four-Tusked One) is clear.


Shloka 478: Caturātmā (चतुरात्मा)
आत्मान्तरात्मा ज्ञानात्मा परमात्मेति भेदतः ॥४७८॥ आत्मोपनिषदि प्रोक्तश्चतुरात्मा त्वमेव हि ॥
The different forms of the Self: the individual self (Ātmā), the inner self (Antarātmā), the knowledge self (Jñānātmā?), and the supreme self (Paramātmā). As proclaimed in the Ātmopaniṣad, you indeed are Caturātmā (The Fourfold Self).


Shloka 479: Caturmukhaḥ (चतुर्मुखः)
चतुर्विधा मुखे वेदा यस्य स त्वं चतुर्मुखः ॥
The four Vedas exist on your face; therefore, you are Caturmukhaḥ (The Four-Faced One).






चतुर्विधोपायमयश्चतुर्वर्णाश्रमाश्रयः ॥
चतुर्विधवचोवृत्तिपरिवृत्तिप्रवर्तकः ॥ १३६ ॥


Shloka 480: Caturvidhopāyamayaḥ (चतुर्विधोपायमयः)
भेदो दण्डः साम दानमित्युपायचतुष्टयम् । चतुर्विधो-पायमयस्तज्जन्य फलसाधकः ॥ ४८० ॥
